- `e63334287a3deaa3/quartets/`: Quartets (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept), a daily "find the groups" game like Connections: 16 word tiles hide four groups of four, coloured yellow, green, blue and purple from the most straightforward to the trickiest; four mistakes allowed, "One away…" when three of four belong together, repeated guesses don't count, shuffle and deselect; found groups slide to the top as coloured bars with their category, and on a loss the rest are shown one by one. Keyboard: click words, Enter submits, Esc deselects. **`puzzles.js`** holds 125 puzzles written for the site (`[category, [four words]]` × 4, in colour order); every word is in exactly one group and the red herrings were planned so each puzzle splits only one way. Tile order for the day is seeded from the date, so everyone starts with the same board. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `add('perfect')` on a daily win with no mistakes. Thanksgiving Day 2026 has its own puzzle, kept after the end of the list and never counted in its length (`NP`, `TG_DAY` in `index.html`), so the seeded order of every other day is unchanged.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/crosslet/`: Crosslet (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept), a daily mini crossword like the NYT Mini: 5 × 5 grids with black corners (and some open 4 × 4s), the clue bar above the grid (tap it to switch direction), the clue lists beside the grid on wide screens, an on-screen keyboard on touch screens; letters auto-advance, Backspace steps back, arrows move (a sideways arrow first switches direction), Space switches, Tab/Enter go to the next clue; Check and Reveal by square, word or puzzle; a timer that starts at the first letter and pauses when paused, hidden or behind a dialog. A daily "win" is a clean solve (no reveals); the best clean daily time is kept in `crosslet:best`. **`puzzles.js`** holds 64 grids (`g` rows with `#` for black squares, `a`/`d` clue lists in number order). The grids were filled by a one-off script from a list of about 3,000 common 3- to 5-letter words (with awkward, rude and touchy words removed); every entry is at least 3 letters, every square is in an across and a down word, and all 530 different entries have clues written for the site. The first time round, days follow the file's order. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `min('fastest', seconds)` on a clean daily solve (`fastest` is in stats.js `LOWER`). Thanksgiving Day 2026 has its own 4 × 4 puzzle, kept after the end of the list and never counted in its length (`NP`, `TG_DAY` in `index.html`), so every other day is unchanged.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/sleight/`: Sleight (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept), a daily cryptic clue like Minute Cryptic: letter boxes with the letter count, five hints taken in order (1 underlines the definition, 2 names the kind of wordplay and its indicator, 3 explains the wordplay, 4 gives the first letter, 5 shows the answer), wrong answers cost nothing; the score is hints used (lower is better) and showing the answer counts as a loss. Afterwards the clue is shown with its definition underlined and indicator dotted, and a breakdown of how it works. **`clues.js`** holds 128 clues written for the site (`c` clue, `a` answer, `d` definition, `t` type, `i` indicator, `p` explanation), covering anagrams, hidden words, reversals, charades, containers, homophones, deletions, double definitions, first letters and &lits; every anagram, hidden word, reversal, container and deletion was checked by script against its answer. The first time round, days follow the file's order, which goes from easier to harder. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `add('clean')` on a daily solve with no hints.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/sem-data/`: shared files for the two daily meaning games (Nearword, Warmer). Closeness in meaning comes from real word vectors: GloVe 6B 300d (Stanford NLP, Wikipedia 2014 + Gigaword 5, Public Domain Dedication and License); nothing is computed in the browser except a lookup, and nothing is loaded from other sites. **`words.txt`**: the 22,101 guessable words, one per line, alphabetical (line *n* = word number *n*): common lowercase dictionary words from SCOWL (sizes ≤ 40, via the npm package wordlist-english, MIT; SCOWL's notices are in `SCOWL-Copyright.txt`) ordered by how common they are in GloVe's corpus, the first 22,000 plus a hand-picked list of everyday words news text undercounts (kitten, crayon, snowman…) and every secret word; no names or abbreviations, and rude words, slurs and sexual words are left out. A word ending in `*` (214 of them: drink, drugs, weapons, violence, insults and a few borderline words) can be guessed but the game never shows it by itself (hints and the closest-words list skip it). **`d/000.bin` … `d/429.bin`**: one file per secret word (430, about 11 KB each, 4.8 MB in all; a game downloads only the one it plays), zlib-compressed: the 1,000 words nearest the secret in exact order with their similarity (cosine × 10000), and for every other word a "level" by rank (1,001–1,200, 1,201–1,400 … 13,001–22,101) with each level's average similarity; the format is described at the top of `tools/build.py`. Ranks past 1,000 are shown as the middle of the level (Nearword shows them with ~) and scores past 1,000 as the level's average (Warmer shows one decimal), so they are approximate. The secret word is the first word in its file; files have no names or words in them that give the answer away, but anyone reading the files could find it, as with any game like this. **`sem.js`** sets `window.Sem`: the daily choice (puzzle #1 is 2026-11-01, local date; days walk through the files in order, which the build shuffled, then a new seeded shuffle each 430-day round; Warmer runs 215 files ahead of Nearword so they never share a word on a day; the count is `COUNT` in sem.js and must match the files), loading `words.txt` and a day file (it uses the browser's `DecompressionStream` and has its own small inflate for older browsers), the `daily:<slug>` record, and `Sem.game(cfg)`, the whole game page both games share: top bar, How to play, Stats with countdown and share, the guess box (Enter to guess; on touch screens an on-screen keyboard with an "abc" key for the phone's own keyboard), the latest guess on its own and the sorted list, Hint (adds the word ranked about halfway between your best guess and #1, never the answer), Give up (a loss), and after the game the 100 closest words. Progress is saved in `<slug>:day` and, for Unlimited, `<slug>:free` (by word, so it survives a rebuild of the word list); `<slug>:seen` marks How to play as seen. **`sem.css`** is the shared look (Deepword's colours and fonts). **`tools/build.py`** rebuilds `words.txt` and `d/` from the downloaded vectors and word lists (instructions at its top; the vectors are 480 MB and aren't in the repo). Rebuilding with a different word list or secret list changes future daily words and makes saved games of the day restart, so do it rarely; with the same inputs it gives byte-identical files.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/nearword/`: Nearword (Back Room), a daily "find the secret word" game like Contexto: every guess shows its rank among all the words by closeness in meaning to the secret (the secret is #1), with a bar that is green in the top 300, yellow to 1,500 and red beyond. Unlimited guesses, hints and give up. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `min('guesses')` on a daily win. **Spanish:** the page around the game is in Spanish (`sem-data/es.js` for the shared parts, `nearword/es.js` for its own); the words are English by design, kept out of translation (`data-noi18n`) and tagged "En inglés".
- `e63334287a3deaa3/warmer/`: Warmer (Back Room), like Semantle: every guess gets a similarity score (cosine × 100, the secret is 100) and, inside the 1,000 nearest words, a nearness out of 1000 (999 = the nearest); temperatures run 🧊 Freezing, ❄️ Cold, 🌥️ Cool outside the 1,000 and 🌤️ Tepid, ☀️ Warm, ♨️ Hot, 🔥 Burning inside. Unlimited guesses, hints and give up. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `min('guesses')` on a daily win. **Spanish:** the page around the game is in Spanish (`sem-data/es.js` for the shared parts, `warmer/es.js` for its own); the words are English by design, kept out of translation and tagged "En inglés".
- `e63334287a3deaa3/yardstick/`: Yardstick (Back Room, a daily puzzle under Estimation on the Daily Puzzles page): judge real-life sizes by eye. Two silhouettes stand on one ground line at the same scale: the blue **reference** with its real size given, and the yellow **target**, which starts at an arbitrary size. The player stretches the target until it looks right next to the reference and locks it in; no number is ever typed. Then the real size is drawn over the guess as a green outline, with both measurements, the score, a fact and the source. Five rounds a day (the same for everyone), up to 100 points each; after the fifth: the total out of 500, the five rounds, a share text and a gallery of the day's five targets at one true scale (tap a name to zoom to it). **Unlimited** is practice: random pairs for ever, optionally from one category, never saved to the daily record. No libraries, no Firebase, no network (stats.js is not loaded). **Spanish:** fully Spanish: `es.js` has the page's words (lang.js) and, keyed by object id, each object's name, description, note and fact in Spanish (`window.YardstickObj`); an object added to `objects.js` without a Spanish entry just shows in English, so add one to `es.js` when you add an object. Sources keep their published titles; sizes keep a dot for decimals.
  - **Resizing and zooming are separate.** Resizing changes the answer: drag the target or its round handle (the measured side follows the pointer; pushing against the edge makes the view back away so the shape can keep growing), the slider (a log scale from 1/60 to 60 times the reference, about 0.8% a step), the − and + buttons (1%, hold to repeat), arrow keys (1%, Shift 0.2%), Page Up/Down (10%), Enter locks in. Zooming (the −/Fit/+ buttons, the wheel, a two-finger pinch, + − 0 keys; dragging empty space pans) only changes how big both shapes are drawn. The view frames both shapes by itself after every change. The size in metres is hidden until the reveal; the grid has no numbers, only "one square = …".
  - **Calibration (the thing to keep true).** Every object has one measured side: `axis:'v'` (height, or a ball's diameter, up from the ground) or `axis:'h'` (length along the ground). Its drawing is scaled so that the full extent of that side is exactly 1000 units, with its lowest point on the ground line (y = 0) and its left edge at x = 0; the game draws 1000 units as the stated metres, so the drawn extent is the measurement and nothing else (no stroke, no padding, no stand). Heights are only paired with heights and lengths with lengths. Nothing is measured by wingspan or at the withers, because a side view cannot show that honestly; the elephant is measured at the shoulder because that is its highest point as drawn. `node check.js` (run it after any change; `-v` prints the table) works out each silhouette's bounding box from its path data (curve and arc extremes solved exactly in `pathgeom.js`) and fails unless the measured side spans 1000 ± 0.02 units (the rounding of the saved numbers, 0.002%) and stands on the ground; it also checks 13 drawings against the source's other dimension (within 5%), that every object has a source address, a date and a fact, that the `what` text shown before the reveal contains no measurement, and the pair and schedule rules below.
  - **Files.** `source.js` is the hand-written data: for each of the 67 objects its name, category (animals, vehicles, buildings, everyday, sports), `m` (metres; `lo`/`hi` where the real thing has a range, such as a rule book's limits or an animal's adult range: any guess inside the range counts as exact), `what` (exactly which one and what is measured, never a number), `note` (other units or what the figure leaves out, shown after the reveal), `src` (name and address), `checked` (date looked up), `fact`, and `art`: silhouettes drawn for this game as SVG paths in any units (helpers in the file: circles, polygons, smooth outlines, mirror), filled in one flat colour with the even-odd rule so an inner shape in the same string is a hole. `node make.js` writes **`objects.js`** (what the page loads: drawings scaled to 1000 units). `node make.js --pairs` also rewrites **`pairs.js`**: 332 reference/target pairs (same axis, real sizes 1.12 to 25 times apart, up to five references per target, familiar ones first, a hand-kept skip list for giveaway pairs) and `YARDSTICK_DAYS`, 60 days of five pair numbers. **Do not rerun `--pairs` casually**: day n uses `YARDSTICK_DAYS[(n − 1) % 60]`, so a new schedule changes every future day; adding objects with plain `node make.js` changes nothing for the daily until a new schedule is made. The schedule uses 300 different pairs, no target twice in a day or on two days running (including day 60 to day 1), nothing as both reference and target in one day, at least three categories and both axes every day; after 60 days the same 60 come round again. Each target appears 2 to 5 times in the 60 days, each time beside a different reference.
  - **Scoring (version 1, `SCORE_V`).** With G the guess and A the real size (the nearest end of the real range if there is one), error = max(G/A, A/G), so twice too big and half the size are the same error. Error up to 1.03 (within 3%) scores 100. Beyond that, with u = log2(error / 1.03) and d = log2(2 / 1.03), score = round(100 × 0.2^((u/d)^1.3)): smooth at the edge of the tolerance, exactly 20 at a factor of two. Roughly: 10% out 92, 25% out 72, half as much again 46, double or half 20, three times 5, four times 2, six times 0. The starting size is seeded from the date and round and chosen from the reference's size (a fifth of it to five times it), never from the answer. Change `SCORE_V` if the curve changes: a day saved under another version starts again.
  - **Storage.** `daily:yardstick` (the daily contract; `win` is always true for a finished day, `score` = total out of 500, `dist` = band of the total; each day also keeps `sv`, the scoring version, and `r`, the five round scores), `yardstick:day` (`{ day, sv, pairs, rounds:[{g: guess in metres, s: score}], t0, t1, done }`, so a day can be picked up mid-way), `yardstick:cat` (practice category), `yardstick:seen`. The shared Stats window's "Win %" box is replaced by the average daily score. Uses `wordplay/kit.js` and `kit.css` for the shell, mode switch, storage and share. Test hook: `window.__ys` under webdriver (`play(refId, targetId)`, `setG(metres)`, `lock()`, `next()`, `st`, `scoreOf`).
- `e63334287a3deaa3/ninefold/`: Ninefold (Back Room), a daily sudoku: three new puzzles every day (Easy, Medium, Hard) plus Unlimited. **`sudoku.js`** (sets `window.Sudoku`, also runs in Node) makes and grades every puzzle in the browser, no libraries: a seeded random full grid (mulberry32 seeded by an FNV hash of the text seed, the same as the geography games, so every device makes the same grid), then clues taken out in mirror-image pairs (cell i and 80 − i) in a seeded order, each removal kept only if the puzzle still has exactly one solution (a quick brute-force count) and a person-style solver can still finish it with the level's techniques. Tiers: Easy = naked and hidden singles only, at least 36 clues; Medium = also pointing, box/line, naked/hidden pairs and triples, at least 26 clues; Hard = also X-Wing, XY-Wing, Swordfish, XYZ-Wing and naked/hidden quads. A puzzle is used only if its hardest needed technique is exactly its level's tier (otherwise the next seeded attempt is tried), so nothing ever needs guessing and every hint has a reason. Daily seed: `ninefold-YYYYMMDD` (level and attempt number are added), so **changing `sudoku.js` changes future daily puzzles**; today's puzzles are cached in `ninefold:cache` and each saved game keeps its own puzzle string, so an update mid-day doesn't break games in progress. Generation takes about 1/15/30 ms (Easy/Medium/Hard) on a computer, under half a second on a slow phone. Hints: if a number on the board is wrong, the hint takes it out; otherwise the solver works from the current board and fills the next cell it can prove, explaining each step it used (in my own words). Storage: `daily:ninefold` (see the daily contract; `score` = puzzles finished that day, `lv` = seconds per level), `ninefold:day:<level>` and `ninefold:free:<level>` (the game in progress: puzzle, numbers, notes as 9-bit masks, hinted cells, seconds, hints, mistakes, undo list), `ninefold:levels` (solved count, total seconds, best time without hints per level), `ninefold:set` (settings: show mistakes, tidy notes, highlight matching numbers, highlight row/column/box, show timer), `ninefold:lv` (last level), `ninefold:seen`. The clock runs while the puzzle is on screen and no window is open; a day counts as played from the first move. Keys: arrows/WASD move, 1–9 fill, Shift+number or N for notes, Backspace/0 erase, Z or Ctrl+Z undo, H hint. Hooks: `add('wins')` per daily puzzle, `max('streak')`, `min('fast-<level>', seconds)` for hint-free solves. **Spanish:** `es.js` has its words (lang.js), with a language switch in Settings. The hint explanations in `sudoku.js` go through lang.js when it is loaded (`TR()` there; Spanish cells are written f<row>c<column>), and are exactly as before in English and in Node.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/thereabouts/`: Thereabouts (Back Room), a daily estimation game: three questions a day whose answers are numbers nobody knows off by heart (heartbeats in a lifetime, tennis balls to fill a pool, trees on Earth), to be worked out roughly from smaller guesses. Each question has a number box with its unit and a built-in calculator; one guess, locked on Lock in. **Scoring:** the miss as a multiple, `max(guess ÷ answer, answer ÷ guess)`: 1× is exact, double and half are both 2×, lower is better; the day's score is the plain average of the three. Zero and negative guesses are refused with a reason; `2,500,000`, `2.5 million`, `40k`, `3e6`, `1.2 x 10^9` and a trailing unit are all read, and a line under the box shows how the number was read before it is locked. After each guess the page shows the guess, the accepted answer, the multiple, high or low, a bar where each mark is ten times, and the "back of the envelope": three to five worked steps that reach about the same answer, a note saying how sure the answer is, and the sources. After three: the average, a verdict in words, Share, and Past days (any earlier day's three, kept in `thereabouts:arch`, never counted). Unlimited gives one random question at a time, never one from today's set or the next six days' sets, and counts for nothing. No Firebase, no stats.js, nothing about other players: everything stays in the browser. Three files beside `index.html`, all plain JavaScript that also runs in Node: **`questions.js`** (`window.THEREABOUTS = { checked, list }`, 114 questions: `id`, `c` category of nine, `d` difficulty 1 to 3, `k` measured / calculated / estimated, `q`, `u` unit, `a` accepted answer, `r` what the worked route reaches when that differs, `s` steps, `n` note, `src` `[name, address, the figure taken from it]`; every source was opened and read on the `checked` date and every number in the steps was printed by a calculation), **`core.js`** (`window.TBCore`: `parseGuess`, `score`, `fmtNum`/`fmtFull`/`fmtMult`, `calcEval` for the calculator: + − × ÷ ^ brackets sqrt cbrt pi and the same shorthand, `band`, `daySet`) and **`check.js`** (`node e63334287a3deaa3/thereabouts/check.js`: run it after any edit; it checks every question's fields and sources, that each worked route lands within 1.5× of its answer and each calculated one within about 1%, the number reader and calculator, and the daily sets). **The daily sets:** `daySet(list, dayNum − 1)` goes through the whole list once every 38 days (114 ÷ 3), in an order seeded by `thereabouts-cycle-<n>` with the same hash and mulberry32 as the kit, so every device gets the same three; each day's three come from different categories and different answer sizes (under 100, hundreds and thousands, ten thousand to a million, a million and up) and are never all Hard, except on about one day in 250 where the questions left don't allow it; they are shown easiest first, and the questions from the last three days of one round are kept out of the first days of the next. So **adding, removing or reordering questions changes which questions come up on future days and in Past days**; today's game keeps its own question ids in `thereabouts:day`, so an update mid-day doesn't change a game in progress. To add a question: look the number up, add an entry with its source and worked steps, run `check.js`, and keep the count a multiple of three if you can (any left over simply sit out each round). Storage: `daily:thereabouts` (the daily contract; `win` is always true so the streak is days played in a row, `score` = the day's average × 100, so 137 means 1.37×, `dist` = six bands of average from ≤1.25× to >10×), `thereabouts:day` (`{ day, ids, g }`, today's guesses), `thereabouts:stats` (this player's own numbers, daily only: `games`, `sum` of daily averages, `best` and `bestDay`, `nq` questions, `slog` the sum of log10(guess ÷ answer) which gives the high-or-low lean, `hi`, `lo`, `last`), `thereabouts:arch`, `thereabouts:free` (the last 60 Unlimited question ids, so practice doesn't repeat), `thereabouts:set` (calculator shown or hidden), `thereabouts:seen`. The Statistics button opens the game's own window (played, average, best day, streak, high-or-low lean, spread of daily averages) in place of the kit's win-and-loss one. On a phone the calculator sits under the question with its own keys (the phone's keyboard stays down for it), and sideways it sits beside it. Test hook: `window.__tb` under webdriver (`st`, `start`, `type`, `go`, `next`, `lock`, `idsFor`, `shareText`, `stats`). **Spanish:** `es.js` has its words (lang.js): the page, the calculator, `core.js`'s messages (translated where they are shown, `core.js` unchanged), grades, categories and units; big numbers are written with Spanish number words (`NUM()`: "2.5 millones", "3 mil millones"). The questions, worked steps, notes and sources are English prose by design (`data-noi18n`, tagged "En inglés"), and typing a guess is unchanged in both languages (a dot for decimals, English number words, the thousand/million chips), so scores never change.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/poopdle/`: Poopdle (Back Room, Nico's idea), a daily word ladder like Weaver: change one letter at a time from the day's start word to POOP, every step a real word. **`words.js`** has 3,106 four-letter words: 1,909 everyday words (start words and hint words) and 1,197 rarer ones that are accepted too. Built once from sindresorhus/word-list and pyspellchecker's English list (both MIT), ordered by wordfreq frequency, then checked by hand: no names, abbreviations, swearing, slurs or grown-up words (POOP and harmless silly words like BURP and FART stay; it's the joke). **`ladder.js`** (sets `window.Ladder`, also runs in Node) finds shortest ladders by breadth-first search over all accepted words, so par can't be beaten. Daily start words are everyday words whose shortest ladder to POOP is 4 to 8 steps **and** can be done with everyday words only (738 words); they come round in a seeded shuffle (`poopdle-cycle-<n>`), so none repeats for two years. Puzzle #n is the n-th word of that order; changing either word list changes future start words. Undo goes back a word but the move still counts; Hint first names the letter to change, then a word on a shortest ladder (everyday words first); Give up (tap twice) shows a shortest ladder. Letters already matching the goal are green. Unlimited has random start words and a "Goal: any word" switch for random goal words. Storage: `daily:poopdle` (`score` = moves over par), `poopdle:day` and `poopdle:free` (the ladder, moves, emoji log, hints, times), `poopdle:any`, `poopdle:seen`. Share: one square per move (🟩 closer, 🟨 no closer, 🟥 further, ↩️ undo, 💩 arrival), no words. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `add('perfect')`. On Thanksgiving Day 2026 the start word is CORN (set where the daily is started in `index.html`; par 4). **Spanish:** `es.js` has its words (lang.js); the ladder words are English by design and tagged "En inglés".
- `e63334287a3deaa3/geo-data/`: shared files for the four daily geography games (Hotspot, Outline, Unfurl, Overland). No libraries and nothing loaded from other sites at run time. **`countries.js`** sets `window.GEO_DATA.list`, one row per guessable place (231): `[id, name, aliases, continent, label lon, label lat, level, area km², neighbours]`. `id` is the ISO 3166 two-letter code in lower case (`xk` Kosovo, `gf` French Guiana); `aliases` are the other names people type, separated by `|` (UK, USA, Holland, Ivory Coast, Czech Republic, Burma, DRC…); `level` 2 = an answer in every game (164 sovereign states), 1 = an answer in Unfurl only (30 tiny or scattered island states and microstates, whose shapes are too small to play with), 0 = guessable but never the answer (territories such as Greenland, Puerto Rico and Hong Kong, and the disputed or partly recognised Kosovo, Taiwan, Palestine and Western Sahara); `neighbours` are land borders. **`shapes.js`** sets `window.GEO_SHAPES`: each place's outline as rings of longitude/latitude in 1/1000 degree, delta-encoded like the Google polyline format (`Geo.rings(id)` decodes it); ids starting with `_` are only drawn (Antarctica, the Siachen glacier, and France's and the Netherlands' Caribbean and Indian Ocean islands). Both files were generated from Natural Earth 1:50m admin-0 countries v5.1.2 (public domain): borders were simplified as a topology (so neighbours' borders stay identical; small countries keep relatively more detail), islands smaller than 60 km² or 0.04% of a country's land were dropped (the biggest piece of every place is always kept), Northern Cyprus was merged into Cyprus and Somaliland into Somalia, France's overseas parts were split off (French Guiana became its own place, so France doesn't border Brazil), and neighbours are countries sharing a border line in that data, plus Zambia–Botswana (the short Kazungula border the 1:50m map misses). There's no Spain–Morocco border, because Ceuta and Melilla are too small for the 1:50m map. Regenerating the data is a one-off Node script (topojson-server/-simplify), not part of the site. **`flags/`** holds the 194 answer flags as SVG, copied unchanged from flag-icons 7.6.0 (MIT; the licence is in `flags/LICENSE`), 4:3 versions, 1.3 MB in all but each game loads only the one it needs. **`geo.js`** sets `window.Geo`: name matching (`norm` ignores case, accents, punctuation, "the" and "St."/"Saint"; `find` matches a name or alias exactly; `suggest` ranks names starting with the text, then words starting with it, then anywhere in it), distances (`km` between label points, `bearing` the rhumb-line direction, `borderKm` between the nearest points of two outlines, 0 for neighbours), the daily choices, the `daily:<slug>` record, the page shell (top bar, How to play, Stats with countdown, share), the guess box (suggestion chips; Enter takes the highlighted one; on touch screens an on-screen keyboard and `inputmode="none"`, with an "abc" key to switch to the phone's own keyboard) and `Geo.Globe`, a canvas globe in orthographic projection (drag to turn, pinch, scroll or +/− to zoom, tap to pick a country; parts of a country behind the globe are pushed to the rim). **`six.js`** is the six-guess game Outline and Unfurl share. **`geo.css`** is the shared look (Deepword's colours and fonts). **Daily choices:** puzzle #1 is 2026-11-01 (local date). Hotspot and Outline walk through one shuffled order of the 164 level-2 countries (a new shuffle each time round, Outline 59 places ahead, so they never match on a day and nothing repeats within 164 days); Unfurl has its own order of the 194 flags and skips ahead if it would match either. Overland walks through a shuffled list of all 2,920 pairs of level-2 countries joined by land with 3 to 6 countries in between on the shortest route, so a pair doesn't come back for eight years. Changing `countries.js` (pools or neighbours) changes future daily puzzles, so do it rarely. Every game saves its progress for the day in `<slug>:day` and whether How to play was seen in `<slug>:seen`. A test hook reads `window.__GEO_TODAY` (YYYYMMDD) only when `navigator.webdriver` is set.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/hotspot/`: Hotspot (Back Room), a daily "find the mystery country" game like Globle: every guess is coloured on the globe from cream (far) to deep red (next door) by the distance between the nearest points of the two borders, the list keeps the nearest at the top, and guesses are unlimited (give up is offered after 10). Small countries get a ring on the globe; tapping a guessed one or its row shows it. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `min('guesses')` on a daily win. **Spanish:** `es.js` (loaded between `countries.js` and `geo.js`) has its words and, with Spanish on, swaps each shown country name for the browser's Spanish name (`Intl.DisplayNames`), keeping the English name as an alias so both can be typed; guesses are still saved by id. The names follow the language the page was opened in.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/outline/`: Outline (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept), like Worldle: name the country from its outline (drawn in an equal-area projection centred on it, leaving out small far-off islands such as the Canaries or Hawaii) in six guesses; each wrong guess shows the distance between the countries' label points, an arrow towards the answer and a percentage (100% minus distance over half the world's circumference). After the game the answer is shown on the globe. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `min('tries')`.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/unfurl/`: Unfurl (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept), like Flagle: the flag starts under six tiles with one lifted (the order is shuffled from the day) and each wrong guess lifts another; six guesses, with the same distance, arrow and percentage as Outline. Loads only `countries.js` and the one flag; the globe at the end loads `shapes.js` then. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `min('tries')`.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/overland/`: Overland (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept), like Travle: get from the start country to the end one by naming countries joined by land borders; you have the shortest route's length plus 4 guesses and win as soon as your countries join up. Guesses are green (on a shortest route), yellow (a detour of one or two countries) or red (off track or no land link); at the end one shortest route (alphabetical at ties) is shown on the map. Hooks: `add('wins')`, `max('streak')`, `add('perfect')` when the route is as short as possible.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/football-data/`: shared data and code for the daily soccer games (Deadline Day, Journeyman, Scout Report, Crossbar, Teamsheet, Back Four, Stat Duel, Top Bins). `players.js` sets `window.FOOTBALL`: `LEAGUES` (country of each league; Monaco counts as France, Swansea/Cardiff as England, Canadian MLS clubs as MLS), `NATIONS` (`[name, continent]`), `CLUBS` (`[name, league, colour 1, colour 2]`: clubs are drawn as two-colour chips we picked, never badges), and one row per player: `[name, nation, GK/DF/MF/FW, birth year, shirt number at his main club (0 = not sure), career, aliases, extra]`. The career is first-team spells in order, `CODE start-end;` with `~` for loans, `2023-` for "still there at the start of 2025-26", and a `+` token meaning the list is incomplete (those players stay out of Journeyman and Back Four). Extra facts: `main:` (main club when it isn't the longest spell), `wc:` (World Cup-winning squads, up to 2022), `bdo:` (Ballon d'Or, up to 2025), `caps:`/`ig:` (men's caps and goals, only for players who have finished with their country), `plg:` (Premier League goals, only for players who have left it). Everything is as of 2025 and only facts we were sure of; anything doubtful was left out. `kit.js` (`window.FBK`) builds the top bar, How to play and Stats modals, the player picker (accents, case and punctuation ignored; unique surnames and listed aliases like "Mbappe" or "CR7" work), shares, and the storage contract: `daily:<slug>` = `{v:1, days:{YYYYMMDD:{done,win,score,tries,time}} (60 days), played, wins, streak, maxStreak, last, dist}`; the daily puzzle is `dailyIndex(n, slug)`, a fixed shuffled order walked one per day from 2026-11-01 (#1), so nothing repeats until every puzzle has been used. `cats.js` (`window.FBCAT`) defines the grid/group categories (`c:` club, `n:` nation, `l:` league country, `wc`, `wy:` World Cup year, `bdo`, `d:` birth decade, `y:` birth year, `p:` position). **After any data edit run `node e63334287a3deaa3/football-data/check.js`**: it checks clubs/nations, careers in order, that every name and alias finds its player, that each transfer matches the career, that every Crossbar square has 3+ answers and every grid can be filled with 9 different players, that Back Four groups are exclusive, line-ups have 11 matching the formation, and that no daily repeats too soon. `crossbar/grids.js` and `back-four/puzzles.js` were generated from `players.js`; if you change a player, the checker tells you whether any of them broke. A test hook (`window.__fb`, `window.__game`, and `?day=YYYYMMDD` to fake today) exists only when `navigator.webdriver` is set.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/deadline-day/`: Deadline Day (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept, a daily soccer puzzle): a famous transfer (from, to, year, reported fee from `transfers.js`, 169 of them) → name the player in 6; misses unlock nationality, position, age that year and initials.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/journeyman/`: Journeyman (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept, a daily soccer puzzle): a career revealed club by club (183 players with complete careers, unique club paths) → name him in 6; "show next club" costs a guess.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/scout-report/`: Scout Report (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept, a daily soccer puzzle): a mystery player (294 with a known shirt number) in 8 guesses; each guess compares nation (yellow = same continent), league and club of each player's main club (yellow = the mystery player also played there), position, birth year and shirt (yellow within 2, arrows).
- `e63334287a3deaa3/crossbar/`: Crossbar (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept, a daily soccer puzzle): 3×3 grid (404 in `grids.js`), 3 lives, one player per square, each player once; afterwards every square lists all players in our list who fit ("1 of N").
- `e63334287a3deaa3/teamsheet/`: Teamsheet (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept, a daily soccer puzzle): a starting XI from a World Cup, Euros or Champions League final (42 in `lineups.js`) with one listed player hidden (344 puzzles); 5 guesses, hints born year, country/main club, initials.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/back-four/`: Back Four (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept, a daily soccer puzzle): 16 players into four groups (418 in `puzzles.js`), 4 mistakes, "one away" hint, colours easiest to hardest.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/stat-duel/`: Stat Duel (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept, a daily soccer puzzle): higher or lower on finished totals (international caps or goals of players retired from international football, Premier League goals of players who left it); daily run of 10 (ends on a miss) built from the date, plus an endless unlimited streak.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/top-bins/`: Top Bins (Back Room; off the puzzle lists since 18 Nov 2026, folder kept, a daily soccer puzzle): name a whole list (14 in `lists.js`: Ballon d'Or winners by decade, most Ballon d'Or wins, World Cup Golden Ball / Golden Boot, Euro and Copa América winners, first ten European Cup winners, Premier League all-time scorers at the end of 2024-25, England's top scorers, Spain's and Italy's most-capped, Premier League champions, Zlatan's clubs) in 3 minutes; the daily list repeats every 14 days.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/party.js`: Parties (Back Room). A Party panel above the games: create a party (5-character code) or join one, up to 8 people; the person who has been in it longest is the leader, picks a multiplayer game and presses Play together, and every member's browser opens that game in a new room (`#<prefix>-p<6 random characters>`) and joins it. That room code (`p` + 6 characters from `rand`'s alphabet, no i, l, o, 0 or 1) is how a game knows a room is a party's: Showdown, Boostball, Coil, Slipstream, Sky Duel, Blockstorm, Tumble Trials, Driftfall's modes, Deepword and Puffer Party add no bots there (each game's `isPartyRoom`/`partyRoom`), so keep that pattern if you change `playTogether` or `rand`. Back returns everyone to the party. Party invite links are `/#pt-<code>`: the home page holds them until the Back Room code is entered, then joins the party instead of opening a game. Works on the phone version too (`m/index.html` loads `party.js`, lends it its `openGame`, and sends `#pt-` invites to the party instead of Rumble Karts), and both after the home-page reveal (the home page's `reveal()` loads `party.js` from the Back Room folder and lends it `openGame` through `window.BackRoom`) and on the Back Room page opened directly (a `<script>` tag; games then open in party.js's own layer). Storage: Firebase Realtime Database, same project and rules as Rumble Karts, at `rooms/pt-<code>/players/<uid>`. The rules only let a browser write its own node and require the same fields the games write (`n`, `c`, `x`, `z`, `h`, `ts`; `x`/`z`/`h` are always 0), so the party keeps everything there: `j` (join time, which decides the leader), `pk` (game the leader picked), `go` (the leader's latest launch: game, room, time) and `kick` (leader only: the people it removed, `[{ u: uid, t: time }]`, up to 8, kept for 10 minutes), `pub` (1 while the leader's party is Public, which it is unless the leader switches it to Private; the choice is kept in localStorage `party:pub`) and `pn` (party size, leader only, while public), `av` (the member's edited avatar as a code, see `avatar.js`; absent if never edited) and `nt` (the colour of the member's name tile as a code, `'t1:<index>'` or `'t0'`, see `avatar.js`; only a player signed in with Google writes one). Each member's card takes that colour (`BackRoomAvatar.paint`; colours only, so a card keeps its size), and a guest's own avatar there carries a padlock instead of the pencil. **Public or Private:** a new party is Public unless its leader chose Private before in this browser (`party:pub` is `'0'`); nothing is saved until the leader presses one. The leader's view has a "Who can join" control right under the party code, Public | Private, with one line saying what each means; members see which it is but can't change it. **Public** means anyone in the Back Room can see the party and join it: the leader's Online node (social.js) carries the party code, so the leader's row in the Online panel and the conversation with any member show a Join party button that needs no request, and Playing now lists the game the party is in. That join (`Party.join(code, true)`) first reads the party and is refused with "That party is private now" unless the leader's node still says `pub: 1`, so switching to Private locks the party at once even for someone looking at an old button. **Private** means invite only: the invite link or code, Invite to party, or a Request to join the leader accepts. The code doesn't change when a party goes Private, so someone who already has it can still type it in; removing them (✕) keeps them out for 10 minutes, and a new party gets a new code. Only the leader's setting counts, so a new leader's own choice takes over, with one exception: a party that was Private under its last leader stays Private when the lead passes on, until the new leader chooses (kept for the tab in sessionStorage `party:keep`, not as that browser's choice). Everyone in the party is told when someone joins, and after removing someone from a Public party the leader is reminded that Private stops others joining. `Party.openParty` is `{ pp, pn }` while we lead a Public party (in a game or not). On the Back Room page itself (no home-page layer), the Play links of the party games open in party.js's own layer instead of replacing the page, so the Online and Playing now panels keep running and others can join you; a click with Ctrl/Cmd/Shift still opens the link normally. `Party.launch` takes any room code a game's own room box takes (letters, digits and hyphens, so Quick match rooms like `pub-2` and Rumble Karts' `lobby-2` too), and `Party.priv` says whether you're in a party its leader set to Private. Nodes are removed on disconnect and ignored after 2 minutes without a heartbeat. The leader sees a small ✕ on every other member; because it can't delete their node, pressing it (after a confirm) lists them in its own `kick`. Every member watches the current leader's list (a list in anyone else's node is ignored), and one that finds its own uid there with a time from the last 10 minutes leaves by removing its own node and shows "You were removed from the party by <leader>"; the leader's row shows "Removing…" until the node is gone. Joining checks the leader's list the same way, so a kicked person can't come back for 10 minutes while that leader is still the leader (a new leader starts with an empty list). Supported games: Shell Crackers, Rumble Karts, Slipstream, Boostball (6 max), Coil, Showdown, Backrooms 1v1–4v4, Chromaflage, Spire (12 max), Turbo Trail (12 max), Escapade (12 max), Updraft (12 max), Odd One Out (16 max), Tumble Trials (16 max), Deepword (12 max), Puffer Party (12 max), Blockstorm (12 max), Lanternhold (4 max), Sky Duel (8 max) and Putterby (8 max). Trivium isn't supported (its contests get their code from the host's screen). party.js joins the game for each member by filling in the game's name and room boxes and pressing its own Play in room button (`#name` / `#room` / `#joinRoom`), so the games themselves weren't changed: keep those ids if you rework a game's menu. Party launches always use the member's party name, even over a name the game had saved or made up (Shell Crackers' Egg123, Rumble Karts' Driver123, Backrooms' Wanderer123, Hail Mary's random team), so everyone shows up under the name the party knows them by. Because the games save whatever is in their name box, party.js then puts the game's previously saved name back in its localStorage key (right after joining, again 3 and 15 seconds later, and when the game closes), unless the player has typed a different name since; a game that had no saved name keeps the party name. To add a game, add a row to `GAMES` in party.js (prefix, folder, room size, `nk` for the localStorage key the game saves its name under, and `ids` if its menu uses other ids). At the end of party.js, `window.Party` lets the Online panel (social.js) share its sign-in, read the party code, size, leader (`leader`: uid and name), member uids and `max`, create or join a party, and open a game room; it doesn't change how the Party panel works. `Party.roster` (members with their party names) and `Party.out` (uids on the current leader's kick list, for the 10 minutes it lasts) are there for the party chat. The Party panel has a Party chat button (once someone else is in the party) that opens the Online panel's Party chat. Signing in waits up to 25 s here, in `feedback.js` and in the online games' `Net.init` (Rumble Karts and Backrooms have no limit; the others' menus say "Still signing in…" after 8 s and put their message back once signed in), because a slow phone on school Wi-Fi can take 10-20 s. Each member's card shows their avatar (`avatar.js`; your own is a button that opens the avatar editor) and, on its own line, their status tier badge (`miles.js`) when those scripts are loaded; without them, a coloured initial and no badge.
- `namefilter.js` (site root): the name filter. Every page where players pick a name others can see loads it with a plain `<script src="/namefilter.js"></script>` right after `<meta charset>` (every Back Room game, the Back Room and phone pages, Hype Cycle Exchange, Boxed and the home page; not the admin page). `NameFilter.clean(name)` masks rude words at the start of a word after their first letter (`F***face`), the same way and with the same look-alike digits as `social.js`'s and `leaderboard.js`'s `mask`, and turns a name that hides one of the worst words some other way (`f.u.c.k`, `fuuuck`, `xfuckx`) into `Player`; clean names come back unchanged. On its own it cleans the name boxes the games use (`#name`, `#inName`, `#joinName`, `#hostName`, `#newName`, `#ptyName`, or any `input[data-name-filter]`, such as the Online name box) on load, on blur or change, and on any click, Enter or submit before the game's own handlers run, so Play, Join and Host always read a cleaned name; it doesn't change a name while it's being typed. `social.js`, `party.js`, `leaderboard.js` and `stats.js` also pass every name through it in their `cleanName`, so names other people already saved are masked where those panels show them. A new game gets it by using one of those ids for its name box (or `data-name-filter`) and adding the script tag. The word lists are stored in ROT13; the comment at the top of the file says how to add words, and which list also looks for words hidden inside names.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/social.js`: Online (Back Room). An Online panel under the Party panel lists everyone who has the Back Room open, by name plus a seat tag worked out from their uid (rows 1–40, seats A–F, like `Nico · 14C`), so two people with the same name can be told apart (seats can still repeat now and then; there are 240). Each person shows as Idle, in a party (and its size), or in a game. **The list's size:** on a computer it scrolls inside a box 252 px tall, and the page carries on scrolling once the box reaches its end (it used to hold the scroll, so the mouse got stuck over a long list); on a phone (600 px wide or less) it isn't a scroll box at all, because a finger on it couldn't scroll the page past it, so it shows the first 6 people and a **Show everyone (N)** / **Show fewer** button (`PHONE_FEW` in social.js), while the Find box still searches everyone. **Redraws:** the panel's draw runs every time anyone online changes anything (each person's 15-second heartbeat included), so the list, your badge row, your name card and the open drawer are only rebuilt when what they'd show actually changed (`swapIn` and `drawnSig` compare against what was built last time, ignoring avatar.js's clip-path ids, not against the page, because aisles.js and lang.js add to the drawn copy); and while a mouse button or finger is down in the panel or drawer nothing there is redrawn until it comes up (`pressing`), so a click is never lost to a rebuild. The drawer's list is full height so the drawer itself scrolls and keeps its place; the people view opens at the top. The name starts as the signed-in account's first name, else the saved party or game name, and can be changed in the panel (saved as `ol:name`). **One name everywhere:** `ol:name` is the player's name for the Online panel, the Party panel and the Leaderboards, in one order (`ol:name`, then the Google account's first name, then `party:name`, then a game's saved name) in `social.js`, `party.js` and `stats.js`. A rename in the Online box (or the Party panel's name box, which saves both keys) is written to the Online node and, while in a party, to the party node within half a second, so other people see it in about a second; `social.js` also sets just `n` on the player's leaderboard entry (`rooms/lb-all/players/<uid>/n`, only if the entry exists, at most every 4 seconds; once after each page load too, so an entry left with an old name catches up), and fires `backroom:name` (`{uid, n, mine}`) on `document`, as it does when someone else's Online node changes name, so the Leaderboards rename that row at once without a new read. Other tabs follow through the `storage` event. Measured with two windows: before, a rename reached the other window's Online list in 0.8 s but never reached the Party panel (the party node kept `party:name`), and the leaderboard entry only changed the next time a game published (up to 60 s with a game open, never without one; the other window needed a Refresh after that); after, Online 0.9 s, Party 0.9 s, Leaderboards 1.2 s live and the entry in about 2 s. Game name boxes (`ss:name`, `shellcr-name` …) stay each game's own and don't rename you; party launches fill them with the one name. Hide me (`ol:hidden`) takes you off the list, and you can't chat while hidden. **Joinable games:** next to Hide me, **Let people join my games** (localStorage `ol:join`, `'0'` = off; on by default; saved with the account by account-nudge.js; also a "Let people join: On / Off" button in the Online drawer, for use over a game) lets anyone in the Back Room join the online game you're in, party or not. While it's on, you aren't hidden and you aren't in a party set to Private, your Online node carries `jg` (game key from party.js's `GAMES`), `jr` (room code after the game's prefix) and `jt` (when you got there, server time); they're removed when you leave the room or switch it off, and go stale with the node's heartbeat like everything else there. Single-player games and games not in party.js's `GAMES` are never advertised. **Finding the room:** the game runs in its own window (the home page's game layer, the phone page's frame, or party.js's layer on the Back Room page, where party games now open instead of replacing the page) and keeps its connection to itself (every game's `Net` sits inside the game's own function, so the page can't read it). So social.js lists the rooms the open game could be in: the room in the game's address (`#<prefix>-<code>`, which every party game sets when it joins a room by code), the room party.js launched it into, the code in its room box, and its Quick match rooms (`pub-1` to `pub-6`; Rumble Karts' `lobby` to `lobby-6`). It reads `rooms/<prefix><code>/players/<uid>/ts` for each (one small read each) and takes the first that heard from you in the last 20 s; the found room is checked again every 4 s while the game is open, the others every 6 s until one matches. Not covered: Backrooms' Quick match (it pairs two players into a 1v1 room with no code), single-player modes, and a game opened in its own tab. Everyone in a joinable game gets a **Join** button on their row ("In Shell Crackers · Join", or **Queue** when the room is full and the game plays in rounds), from playingnow.js's checks (`window.BackRoomPlaying`); it opens the game in that room the same way Playing now does, and closes the drawer if it was open over a game. When someone's node starts advertising the room you're in after you got there, a small toast says "Ben joined your game" for 5 s (not for your own party, who arrive together; not for blocked or muted people). Tap someone to open their card: Request to chat, invite them to your party (one is created first if you're not in one; they get a Join / No thanks card and Join puts them in your party through party.js), or send them a room code (pick a game and a code; while you're in a game it fills in the room you're in). Private messages need a chat request first: the other person gets a toast and a card with Accept / Decline (and Block), and only once they accept can either side message the other; a message from someone who hasn't been accepted (an older client, say) is dropped, and that person's thread just says to request a chat or to accept theirs. A request runs out after 10 minutes if nobody answers, and then the requester can ask again (one open request per pair; at most one request per person every 10 minutes and ten in 10 minutes in all); a declined request shows "Declined" and can be repeated after the same 10 minutes. If both people request each other at once, that counts as a yes. Accepted pairs are kept in this browser (`ol:chats`, uid to time, at most 60) so you don't have to ask again every visit; End chat in the thread forgets the pair here and tells the other side, which forgets it too. Party invites and room codes aren't chat, so they don't need a request; the cabin chat is unchanged. **Parties:** people in the same party can message each other straight away, without the chat request (their card says so and has no Request to chat button); "the same party" comes from party.js's own member list (the `rooms/pt-<code>` nodes, through `Party.members`), minus anyone on the current leader's kick list (`Party.out`), never from what someone writes in their Online node, so nobody can claim to be in your party. Outside a party, and once someone leaves or is removed, the request is needed again. Block, session block and mute work exactly as before (a blocked party member can't message you and is hidden). **Party chat:** a group chat for the party, opened from the Party chat button in the Online panel (shown while in a party), in the Party panel, in the people drawer, or in a game (see `gamechat.js`). Each message is sealed separately to every member who is in the party at that moment and online here (not to anyone you blocked), with the same ECDH + AES-GCM as private messages, and kept in your own node as `pc/<member uid>` (the last 6 to each member, `{ i, t, b }`; inside: `{ f, to, k: 'g', c: party code, x: text }`); copies for people who leave the party are dropped, and the oldest copies are trimmed if the node would grow past 50,000 characters. The node also carries `av`, the player's edited avatar as a code (see `avatar.js`; absent if never edited), and `nt`, the colour of their name tile (`'t1:<index>'` or `'t0'`, see `avatar.js`; absent if never chosen), which colours their card in the list and in the drawer. A browser only shows a party message that names its current party code and comes from someone on its current member list, so only the party can read the chat, and people who join later don't see earlier messages; everyone online can see that something was sent to whom and when, as with private messages. Blocked and muted people's party messages are hidden (like the cabin chat), the word filter and the shared rate limit (one message every 1.5 s, five in 10 s) apply, a party message from someone else shows a toast when no game is showing it, and party chat is never logged. Report in someone's private conversation also includes the last 20 party-chat messages (everyone's, marked `[party]`) when that person wrote in the party chat, and the confirm box says so. The party chat is kept for the tab in sessionStorage `ol:pchat` (`{ c: code, m: [...] }`, 80 messages) and starts empty in a new party. `window.BackRoomChat` (`state`, `recent`, `send`, `listen`, `seen`, `open`) lets the in-game chat inside a game's iframe read and send it through this page; it only hands out messages already checked, filtered and masked here. While a game is open, a Chat button next to Online in the game's top bar (shown while in a party with someone) opens the game's own chat box, or the Party chat drawer for a game without one. Deepword is in `GAMES` too (status "In Deepword", room codes), so its top bar gets these buttons. **Join party / Request to join:** when someone's party is Public (its leader's node carries `pp`, see party.js), the leader's row in the list has a Join party button ("Party full" at 8; a game's Join button takes its place while the party's game is joinable), the card of anyone in that party has Join party in place of Request to join, and their status reads "Public party of 3"; it joins through `Party.join(code, true)`, which refuses if the party has gone Private, is full, or its leader removed you in the last 10 minutes, and asks first if you'd be leaving a party of your own. Otherwise the card of someone in a party has Request to join, next to Invite to party. It sends a sealed `j` to that party's leader: to them if they lead it, otherwise to their leader when that person is online (the card says so and shows how it's going, with a button to open the leader's chat); if a `j` reaches someone who isn't the leader (their leader is offline, say), their browser answers `x` with `lead` and who the leader is, and the requester sees "Only the leader can accept — ask <leader>" with an Ask button when that leader is online. A leader with room gets a toast and a card with Let them in / Decline; Let them in sends `y` with the party code, and the requester gets a Join party button (toast and card) that joins through party.js, so a person who was kicked is still kept out by the party's own 10-minute rule; Decline sends `x`. A full party (8), someone who isn't the leader, or someone no longer in a party answers `x` straight away with the reason. One open request per party (per leader) at a time, 10 minutes to answer, a repeat only 10 minutes after a no, and the same ten-requests-in-10-minutes limit as chat requests; a `y` or `x` only counts when it answers a request this tab sent. Join-request state lives in the thread in sessionStorage (`jq`, `jx`, `jw`, `jc`, `jl`, `jln`, `jin`, `jnd`), like chat requests. Room invites only ever become the site's own invite links (`/#<prefix>-<room>`, Rumble Karts `/#<room>`) built from a known game and a checked code, never a link someone typed, and opening one goes straight into that game's room. The cabin chat is one public chat for everyone online; it shows the last 30 minutes from people who are still here. Each cabin message is also saved, sealed, to Firestore `chatlog` for the admin page (see below). A Privacy link to `/privacy.html` sits at the bottom of the panel. Mute (`ol:mute`) hides someone's cabin-chat messages and silences their notifications; Block (`ol:block`) also hides them from the list and hides their messages, requests and invites; both are saved only in this browser. Block for this session does the same as Block but is kept in sessionStorage (`ol:sblock`), so it's forgotten when the tab closes; the Blocked section of the people view lists both, with "(this session)" on the latter, and Unblock clears either. While a game is open in the page's game layer (home page, phone page, or a party launch), an Online button in the game's top bar opens the same list, chats and invites, and the status shows "In <game>"; a game opened by its card on the Back Room page opened directly leaves the page, so that person goes offline until they come back. Loaded like party.js: a `<script>` tag on the Back Room page, by the home page's `reveal()` and by `m/index.html`. It signs in through party.js (`window.Party`, a small hook at the end of party.js that also lets it read the party code and size, create or join a party and open a game room). Storage: Firebase Realtime Database, same project and rules as Rumble Karts, one shared room, `rooms/ol-lobby/players/<uid>` (prefix `ol`, not an invite prefix). The rules only let a browser write its own node and need `n`, `c`, `x`, `z`, `h`, `ts` (`x`/`z`/`h` always 0), so everything is in that node: `v` (format 1), `pk` (the browser's ECDH P-256 public key), `p` (party size), `pl` (the party leader's uid while in a party, so a join request can go to the leader; older clients leave it out and ignore it), `g` (game key), `pp`, `pn` (a public party's code and size, leader only, written whenever the party is Public so people can join it; older pages only wrote them during a game and ignore them without the next three), `pg`, `pr`, `pt` (its game, room and launch time while the game it launched is open, for Playing now), `l` (cabin chat: the last 20 messages, 200 characters each, dropped after 30 minutes), `d/<recipient uid>` (the last 6 private items to each of the last 6 people: messages, kind `m`, the chat-request handshake, kinds `q` request, `a` accept, `n` decline and `e` end chat, and the join-request handshake, kinds `j` request, `y` yes with the party code `c`, and `x` no with why in `w`: `no`, `full`, `lead` (only the leader can accept; `l` and `ln` are the leader's uid and name) or `gone`, all in the same sealed `{ i, t, b }` shape) and `i/<recipient uid>` (the latest party or room invite to each of the last 6 people, dropped after 10 minutes). Private messages, requests and invites are sealed like Showdown's hole cards: AES-GCM with a key from ECDH between the sender's private key and the recipient's `pk`, so only those two browsers can read them. A browser only acts on an `a`, `n` or `e` that answers something it did itself (a request it sent within the last 10 minutes, a chat it has accepted), so a stray "yes" from someone you never asked opens nothing; unknown kinds are ignored. Request state (asked, declined, asked by them) lives with the conversation in sessionStorage, so a request you sent is forgotten when the tab closes (ask again), while accepted pairs are in localStorage `ol:chats`. Everyone else can see that a message or invite exists, who it's to and when, but not what it says. The cabin chat, names and statuses are public to everyone online. **Safety log:** when a cabin message is sent, `social.js` also writes `chatlog/<random id>` = `{kind: 'cabin', uid, ts (server time), box}`, with the name, seat, text and send time sealed inside `box` to the admin key (`adminkey.js`), so only the admin page with both admin codes can read it. Private messages and chat requests are never logged. Instead, a **Report** button in each private conversation saves that conversation as the reporter's browser shows it (the last 20 messages, invites and request steps, decrypted on the reporter's side, at most 4,000 characters), after a confirm box that says exactly what is sent: `{kind: 'report', uid (the reporter), ts, box}` with the reporter's name and seat, the other person's uid, name and seat, and the transcript inside `box`; one report a minute per person. Each browser also keeps a plain `who/<uid>` document (`uid`, `vid` = the analytics visitor ID from localStorage `an_vid` if there is one, `name`, `seat`, `ts`), written when it goes online and when the name changes (not while hidden), so the admin stats can put a name to a visitor. All three go through the Firestore REST API with the account's ID token, like `feedback.js`; until the rules from the admin page entry are added, Firestore refuses them, and that is silent and never holds up the chat (the report says reports aren't switched on yet). The key pair is kept (non-exportable) in the browser's IndexedDB (`backroom-online`), so it survives reloads; conversations are kept for the tab in sessionStorage (`ol:threads`) and are gone when the tab closes. Closing the tab removes your node (onDisconnect), so your cabin-chat messages (apart from the sealed copy in `chatlog`) and anything you sent that the other person hasn't opened go with it; nodes without a heartbeat for 75 seconds are ignored. Everything other people write is checked before it's shown: text is never parsed as HTML, numbers are clamped (times can't be more than a few seconds ahead), oversized or malformed nodes are skipped, a short word filter blanks a few rude words (the list is in ROT13 in the file), and sending is limited to one message every 1.5 seconds and five in ten seconds. These are client-side protections only; the shared rules don't limit node size, so someone with developer tools could still write junk into their own node (which other browsers then skip). Every browser downloads every node, so it's meant for tens of people at once, not thousands. Two tabs signed in as the same person share one node and can overwrite each other's chat. Names come with an avatar (`avatar.js`, drawn from the uid unless the player edited it; the "You" row has the **Edit avatar** button) and a status tier badge (`miles.js`): in the list and cards (the badge on its own line under the name, so rows are a little taller), the cabin chat, the thread headers, the Blocked list and toasts; your own "You" row and your card in the drawer also say your miles and how far to the next tier ("2,500 miles · 7,500 to First Class"). **The Name tile row** sits under the "You" row: for a player signed in with Google it shows their own card exactly as everyone else sees it and sixteen swatches (Default and the fifteen colours in `avatar.js`'s `TILES`; radio buttons, so the arrow keys step through them); picking one saves it at once (`BackRoomAvatar.saveTile`), the card beside it changes straight away, and it is published as `nt` in the Online node, the party node and the player's leaderboard entry (only an entry that already exists, like `av`; one write about a third of a second after the last change). **Google only:** a guest (the anonymous account the games sign every browser in with; `BackRoomAvatar.google(uid)` is the test) sees the row locked: the colours as small dots, the line "Sign in with Google to choose your colour and edit your avatar." and a **Sign in with Google** button, which calls the site's own `SiteAccount.signIn()` (`account/account.js`, untouched; the home page's when this is its phone frame) straight from the tap; **Edit avatar** and the guest's own avatar button carry a padlock, and tapping either opens nothing and moves to that button (`avatar.js` fires `backroom:avatar-locked`). When the sign-in started from that button brings in the Google account, the avatar editor opens by itself, on the look the guest had (`carryAvatar`). On the phone page opened on its own there is no sign-in, so the line says "on the main site" and the button is a link to it. With nobody signed in at all (no uid) the row, Edit avatar and the button are not shown. The tier of each person online comes from their leaderboard node, read once per uid through `miles.js` and kept for 10 minutes; without the two scripts, a coloured initial and no badge. **Find:** a search box above the people list (shown whenever anyone else is online) filters the whole list, not just the part in view, by name, seat or status line ("Coil", "party", "seat 23b"); every word must match, Esc clears it. The people view inside the chat window ignores it.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/playingnow.js`: Playing now (Back Room). A panel right above the Party panel (`#playingnow`) that lists the online games anyone can join right now: every **public** party's game, and the game of every player who lets people join their games (on by default, see social.js), **grouped by game and room**, one row each: the game's thumbnail (`m/thumbs/<folder>.jpg`) and name ("Shell Crackers", or "Shell Crackers · party of 6" when a public party is there), up to three avatars, who's there ("Room with Ava, Ben and 3 others", or "Led by Ava · with Ben and 2 others" for a party; only people who advertise the room are named, everyone else in it is a number), how long since the first of them got there, how many are in the room out of its limit, and a button. **Join** opens the same game in the same room through party.js's own launch (`Party.launch`: the name and room are filled in and the game's Play in room button is pressed), without making you a party member. When the room is full, games played in rounds (Shell Crackers, Rumble Karts, Slipstream, Boostball, Coil, Showdown, Backrooms, Chromaflage, Spire, Turbo Trail, Escapade, Updraft, Odd One Out, Tumble Trials) offer **Queue for next game**: the tab checks the room every 3 s and launches you as soon as there's a spot (after a short random wait and a second look, so two people queued for one spot don't both rush in; one queue at a time, stops after 30 minutes or when that game ends or nobody there lets people join any more, or you open a game). Deepword and Puffer Party show **Full** instead. Room limits are each game's own (its join check), in `CAP` at the top of the file: Shell Crackers 8, Rumble Karts 12, Slipstream 22, Boostball 2 × the room's team size (read from its `round.sz`, else 6), Coil and Showdown 10, Backrooms, Chromaflage and Putterby 8, Spire, Turbo Trail, Escapade, Updraft, Deepword and Puffer Party 12, Odd One Out and Tumble Trials 16; a player counts if their node in `rooms/<prefix>-<room>/players` was heard from within the game's own live window (8–12 s). A game added to party.js's `GAMES` later without a `CAP` row uses party.js's `max`, a 10 s window and no queue. **Public parties:** the Party panel's leader view has a Public / Private control ("Who can join", see party.js; Public also lets anyone join the party itself from the Online panel). Parties are **Public by default**; the leader's choice is remembered in the browser (localStorage `party:pub`, `'1'` or `'0'`, saved with the account by account-nudge.js), so a leader who switched to Private keeps getting Private parties. While the leader's party is Public it writes `pub: 1` and `pn` (party size) to the leader's own party node, and members see "This party is public". Only the leader's setting counts: a new leader's own choice takes over. Private (invite only) also stops every member's own game from being advertised (`Party.priv`). **How the list is built (no rules change):** the rules don't let a browser list `rooms/`, so it can't look through every `rooms/pt-<code>` or game room. Instead everything is advertised on each player's own Online node `rooms/ol-lobby/players/<uid>` (social.js; the same listener data the Online panel already downloads), and checked before it's shown. A public party: while the leader's party is public and the game it launched the party into is open (`Party.pub` in party.js), the leader's node has `pp` (party code), `pg` (game key), `pr` (room), `pn` (size) and `pt` (launch time), and outside that game only `pp` and `pn`, which this panel ignores; playingnow.js checks it against the party's own nodes (`rooms/pt-<pp>/players`, read at most every 15 s): the advertising node must be the party's leader (earliest `j` among nodes with a heartbeat in the last 2 minutes, party.js's rule) with `pub: 1` and `go` naming that game and room. A player's own game: `jg` (game key), `jr` (room code after the game's prefix, e.g. `ab12` or `pub-3`) and `jt` (when they got there); playingnow.js checks it against the game's own room (`rooms/<prefix><jr>/players`, the same read as the player count): that player's uid must be there, heard from within the game's live window. Private parties, games not in party.js's `GAMES`, your own party, Online nodes over 75 s (players) or 2 minutes (parties) without a heartbeat, and anyone with Hide me on (no Online node) or "Let people join my games" off (no `jg`/`jr`) are never listed. The room counts and checks are read every 5 s, only while the page is visible and no game is open (or while the Online list is open over a game, for its Join buttons). **For the Online panel:** `window.BackRoomPlaying` gives social.js a Join button for each person's row: `state(uid)` (`{ label, game, pri, off }`, or null when they aren't in a listed room) and `act(uid)` (join, or start or leave the queue); the file fires `backroom:playing` on `document` whenever the list or a button changes. This file writes nothing.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/aisles.js`: Aisles (Back Room page, home page Back Room and phone page), the site's clans. Any player (signed in the way the Party panel signs everyone in) can start one from the **Aisle** button in the Online panel: a name (3–24 characters, checked with `namefilter.js`), a tag of 2–4 letters or digits (also checked, spaced out too) and one of 12 colours (only an index is stored). One Aisle per player, up to 6 seats (`MAX`, open invites included). The leader invites from a person's card in the Online panel (**Invite to Aisle**) or with an invite link `/#xj-<id><code>` or the same 16-character code typed into the Aisle window; the invitee sees a toast and **Accept** / **Decline** in the window. The leader can remove (kick) members, make someone else leader (hand over), make a new invite code (the old one stops working) and disband; members can leave (a leader must hand over first, unless alone). The tag is drawn as text in front of members' names wherever a name is marked with `data-aisle-u="<uid>"`: the Party panel (`party.js`), the Online list, a person's card and the cabin and party chat (`social.js`) and the Leaderboards rows (`leaderboard.js`); a MutationObserver tags names as they are drawn. Playing now's sentences are not tagged. The Aisle window shows the members with their tier badge, miles and Elo, and the Aisle's two totals and ranks. **Boards:** the Leaderboards have their own **Aisles** tab (Players | Aisles, a pill above the Board menu, Back Room and phone pages only, not a game's own panel) with **Aisle Miles** | **Aisle Elo** (the last one picked is kept in localStorage `lb:aisle`). They are drawn by `aisles.js` from `leaderboard.js`'s own checked and merged rows (`BackRoomBoards.data()`, which `leaderboard.js` now exposes, and the `backroom:boards` event after each read), never from raw nodes; each member counts once, through the merged account their row was folded into. Aisle Miles = the sum of the members' `BackRoomMiles.of(row).miles` (the number behind their tier), with Total ÷ members as the average. **Weekly contest:** the Aisle Miles board has This week / All time (This week unless the browser picked All time, localStorage `ais:span`). This week ranks Aisles by their members' miles from play time since Monday (the viewer's clock, the same week as the Miles board, `BackRoomMiles.HOUR` an hour; the note under the board says only that miles come from time spent playing, with no rate or hours), from leaderboard.js's checked per-day history `dy` (every day from last week's Monday on, merged like the week), and only from the day each member joined the Aisle (the later of the leader's `m` time and the member's `aj`; the founder from `t0`), so a big week can't be carried into another Aisle. The top Aisle is marked Leading this week, and the board's note names last week's winner (worked out the same way from last week's days, with the Aisles as they are now). Nothing is stored for it: every browser works it out from the boards. Aisle Elo = for each member, the average of their checked Elo over every game whose board has an Elo stat and in which they have a rating; those member averages are added up for the total, and the average is Total ÷ members with a rating; a toggle (saved in `ais:elosort`) sorts by Total or Average. A note under each board says this. **Storage:** Realtime Database, same project and rules as the games, prefix `xj` (the invite links use it; the room itself is not a game room). The rules only let a browser write its own node and need `n, c, x, z, h, ts`, so everything is in each player's own node `rooms/xj-all/players/<uid>` (`v` 1, `x/z/h` 0, `c` the Aisle colour): `a` the Aisle they say they're in and `aj` when; `ap` a proof of an invite code, `SHA-256(id:code:uid)`, only while waiting; `ad` invites they declined; and `L`, the records of Aisles they founded or led (`{f, ld, q, nm, tg, cl, m, iv, kx, ch, t0, dis}`: `m` members, `iv` invites with their time, 7 days, `kx` people removed, `ch` a hash of the current invite code, whose plain code stays in the leader's browser as `aisle:code:<id>`). An Aisle's id is a hash of the founder's uid and a small number, so only the founder's node can start the trail; its current record is found by following `ld` from the founder's record to each next leader's own record with a higher `q`. **Both sides must agree:** a player is in an Aisle only while their own node names it (`a`, written only by their browser when they accept or use a code) AND the leader's record lists them (`m`, or a live invite in `iv`), so nobody is added without accepting and nobody gets in without the leader's invite or code. A code join is let in by the leader's browser (which checks the proof against its code) the next time the leader has the Back Room open. **Captains** remove an Aisle with a bad name on the admin page's new **Aisles** tab (the button works only for Captains, `captains.js`); the removal is a list signed with the admin key (ECDSA, like the hours resets) at `rooms/xj-ban/players/<uid>` (`r` JSON `{<id>: {t, x, nm}}`, `sig`), and only correctly signed nodes count. A removed Aisle is hidden everywhere (tag, boards, joining) but nobody is let go, so **Put back** restores it; its members can leave it for good from the Aisle window. Like the rest of the Back Room this is checked in every browser, not enforced by the server: someone using developer tools could write odd data into their own node (it is size-capped and cleaned when read), but they can't change anyone else's membership, and the boards only use the checked leaderboard rows. Hooks in shared files are small: `leaderboard.js` (the two board options, `BackRoomBoards.data()`, the event, `data-aisle-u`), `party.js` and `social.js` (`data-aisle-u`, and the Invite to Aisle button through `BackRoomAisles.inviteBtn`), `index.html` (home page: loads it and holds a `#xj-` link in sessionStorage `aisle:inv` until the Back Room opens), `m/index.html` (same for the phone page), `e63334287a3deaa3/index.html` (a `<script defer>`) and the admin page (the tab, loading `captains.js` and `aisles.js` with `data-admin`).
- `e63334287a3deaa3/avatar.js`: avatars (Back Room). A small cartoon face for every player, drawn as inline SVG from their uid, so one person looks the same in the Online panel, the Party panel, the Leaderboards and the admin page, and distinct from the people around them; players can edit theirs (below) and everyone else sees the edit. Loaded on the Back Room page and the phone page (one `<script defer>` in each page's `<head>`), by the home page's `reveal()` and by the admin page. `window.BackRoomAvatar = {seed(uid), gen(uid), svg(uid, size, opts), el(uid, size), edit(uid, opener), editButton(uid, size), save(uid, choice), mine(uid), learn(uid, code), parse(code), close(), rev, FEATURES, PALETTE, google(uid), tile(uid), paint(el, uid), saveTile(uid, i), tileMine(uid), learnTile(uid, code), parseTile(code), TILES}`: `gen` gives the features a uid gets from its hash (a background from a 12-colour palette, 6 skin tones, 8 hair styles and 9 hair colours, 6 eyes, 5 brows, 6 mouths, glasses, freckles, blush and 5 hats, each an index) plus some that are not drawn from the hash, so nobody's avatar changed when they were added: `eyec` (7 eye colours; 0 is the dark ink every avatar had, and a coloured eye keeps an ink rim and pupil so it reads on every skin tone), `shirt` (14 shirt colours: the palette, white and charcoal; it starts as the colour the background has always picked), `face` (3 face shapes: 0 is the oval every avatar had, 1 narrows a little to the chin and adds lashes, 2 has a squarer jaw; the top half is the same on all three so hair, hats and glasses fit each), `hatc` (the hat's colour, from the same 14; it starts as the colour the background has always picked) and hair styles 8 to 10 (none, buzz cut, ponytail), which the hash never picks, so `FEATURES.hair` is 11 while `GEN.hair` stays 8.
