Under a cap, beanie or party hat a bun or spikes are drawn as short hair (tucked in) rather than poking through; the hash never made that pairing, and the ponytail hangs behind one shoulder so it shows under every hat. `seed` is `gen` with the player's edit on top, `svg` the drawing as a string and `el` the same inside a `<span class="bra-av">`, at any size from 20 to 96 px. Keep the order of `GEN` in the file and never add a hash-drawn feature to it: that would change every face. The features come from the same hash as the seat tag in `social.js` (`hash('seat:' + uid)`) through a small seeded generator, so an unedited avatar is stored nowhere and the markup is built only from numbers (never from a name or anything else someone typed). **The editor:** a player signed in with Google (see Google only, below; with no sign-in at all there is no button) gets **Edit avatar** in the Online panel's "You" row, and their own avatar there and on their Party card is a button with a small pencil (`editButton`); all three open `edit(uid)`, a panel with a live preview and one row for **every** feature the drawing has, the same rows and options for every player (`ROWS`; nothing can be worn only by luck of the uid): Skin, Gender (Neutral, Feminine, Masculine: the `face` shapes; a line under it says it only changes how the face is drawn, and nothing else on the site reads it), Hair style (11), Hair colour, Eyes (6), Eye colour, Eyebrows (5), Mouth (6), Cheeks (plain, freckles, blush, both), Glasses (none, round, square), Hat (none, cap, beanie, headband, party hat), Hat colour (shown only with a hat on), Shirt and Background, plus Reset (every row back to the choices drawn from their id), Cancel and Save. It opens on the avatar the player has now. Colour rows are swatches; the others are small pictures of the player's own avatar with that one option (`svg`'s `opts.box` shows a close-up; most are drawn without the hat so a beanie doesn't hide the choice), and each row names its current choice. Every row is a group of radio buttons (arrow keys move within a row, Tab between rows), Esc or a tap outside closes without saving. The preview never scrolls: the rows scroll under it inside the panel, and on a phone held sideways the preview sits beside them. Because the editor lives in this file, the Back Room page, its copy on the home page and the phone page all have it. **Where an edit lives:** `save` writes localStorage `avatar:v1` = `{v: 1, uid: '<the account it is for>', bg, skin, face, hair, hairc, eyes, eyec, brows, mouth, glasses, freckles, blush, hat, hatc, shirt}` (after a reset just `{v: 1, uid}`), and `seed()` uses every valid field when `uid` matches. An edit saved by the first editor holds only `skin, hairc, eyec, shirt, hat`; it draws the same as before and is filled out to all fifteen from the uid when this file loads (`fillOut`). Other browsers can't read that, so the edit also travels as a short code in a field called `av` in nodes the player already writes (no rule or config change; the shared rules accept extra fields in a player's own node): `'a2:'` and then fifteen numbers joined by dots, in `CODE_KEYS` order (`bg, skin, face, hair, hairc, eyes, eyec, brows, mouth, glasses, freckles, blush, hat, hatc, shirt`), for example `a2:3.2.0.6.4.1.2.2.0.1.0.1.2.7.12` (36 characters at most); the first editor's code, `'a1:<skin>.<hairc>.<eyec>.<shirt>.<hat>'` (`a1:3.5.2.7.1`), is still read exactly as before (those five on top of the uid's own face); `'a0'` means "I went back to my own"; no `av` means "nothing to say" (an older page, or a device with no saved edit) and never erases anything. `mine(uid)` is what this browser should publish. `social.js` puts it in the Online node (everyone online sees a change within a second or two) and on the player's leaderboard entry `rooms/lb-all/players/<uid>` (only an entry that already exists, like the name; it is still there when the player is offline), `party.js` in the party node, and `stats.js` keeps it in the leaderboard entry on every publish (this browser's saved edit when it is for that account, else what the entry already says; a guest account merged into a Google one brings its `av` if the Google entry has none). **Reading it:** `social.js`, `party.js`, `leaderboard.js`, `miles.js` (the one-node tier read) and the admin page hand every `av` they read to `learn(uid, code)`, which accepts only those two exact shapes with every number in range (`parse`; an `a2` number has no leading zero) and otherwise leaves the uid's own avatar in place, so nothing from a node is ever drawn except checked indexes. `social.js` and `party.js` drop an `av` longer than 47 characters before that, and `stats.js` (`okAv`) keeps only `a0`, `a1` and `a2` shapes in the entry. A page from before `a2` (one still open in a tab) ignores an `a2` code and draws that player's id-drawn avatar, or the last `a1` it had learned; its own editor still saves and publishes `a1`, which new pages read. Learned codes are kept in localStorage `avatar:seen:v1` (300 people, 30 days), so a game in a frame (Rampline's board) and the next visit draw them at once; `seed` uses the saved edit for this browser's own account, else the learned code, else the hash. A browser with no saved edit for its account (a second device) draws and republishes what it learned from the entry; two devices with different saved edits each publish their own, and the last to publish wins. A guest who edits and then signs in with Google on that page keeps their choices on the new account unless that account already has an `av` (`carryAvatar` in `social.js`). To add a feature later: never add it to `GEN`, give it a default that draws exactly what was drawn before, add a row for it to `ROWS` (every feature must be a choice), and put it at the end of the code under a new prefix (`a3:`) while still reading `a1:` and `a2:`; older pages ignore a prefix they don't know, and the length checks in `social.js` and `party.js` and `okAv`/`localAv` in `stats.js` need the new shape too. The existing drawings must not change (the beanie's band has always sat low over the brows; moving it would change every beanie wearer's avatar). The scripts that show names (`social.js`, `party.js`, `leaderboard.js`, the admin page) call `BackRoomAvatar.el` when it is there and fall back to a coloured initial when it isn't; the file fires `backroom:avatar` on `document` when it loads and whenever any avatar changes (`detail.mine` when it is this browser's own; `rev` goes up each time), so panels drawn before it arrived redraw and the scripts above publish (the home page loads it alongside the others, in no fixed order). **Name tiles:** a player's name sits on a card (a tile) in the Online panel, the Party panel and the Leaderboards, and a player signed in with Google can pick its colour: `TILES` is Default and fifteen colours (Red, Orange, Amber, Lime, Green, Teal, Sky, Blue, Indigo, Violet, Pink, Rose, Brown, Slate, Charcoal; four are light, marked `d`, and take dark text). `paint(el, uid)` gives a card the classes `bra-nt` and `bra-nt-<index>` (and `bra-nt-d` on a light colour), or takes them off; the rules at the bottom of the file set the card's background, edge and text colour and point the page's own `--ink`, `--ink-2`, `--accent`, `--accent-ink`, `--line` and `--panel` at the tile's colours on the card's children, so the name, seat, status, Leader and You tags, the rank and the plain Economy badge follow without each script knowing about tiles; the avatar gets a ring and the solid badges (Business, First, Private Jet, the all-time #1/#2/#3 tags) an edge so they don't melt into a tile of their own colour. The tiles are the same colours in light and dark mode and on the phone page, the name is at 5.7:1 or better and the small text at 4.9:1 or better on every one, and only colours change, so a card or row keeps its size. To add a colour, add it to the end of `TILES` (the index is what travels) and check its contrast. `social.js` (cards, the drawer, the picker), `party.js` (member cards) and `leaderboard.js` (rows; your own row keeps a ring in the page's accent) call `paint`. **Where a tile colour lives:** `saveTile(uid, i)` writes localStorage `nametile:v1` = `{v: 1, uid, t: <index, 0 = Default>}`, which `account-nudge.js` saves with the Google account, so it is there on another device after signing in. It travels like `av`, in a sibling field `nt` in the same three nodes: `'t1:<index>'` (1 to 15, no leading zero) or `'t0'` ("I went back to Default"); no `nt` means "nothing to say". `tileMine(uid)` is what this browser should publish, `learnTile(uid, code)` what every reader hands an `nt` to (`social.js`, `party.js`, `leaderboard.js`, `miles.js`): only that exact shape with an index in range is kept (`parseTile`), anything else leaves the default card; learned codes are kept in localStorage `nametile:seen:v1` (300 people, 30 days). A sibling field rather than a new avatar code (`a3:`) keeps pages from before name tiles safe: they never read `nt`, so they draw those players' avatars and default cards exactly as before, where an avatar code they don't know would have cost the player their edited avatar there. An old `stats.js` still open in a game tab drops `nt` from the leaderboard entry when it publishes; `social.js` puts it back the next time a hub page loads. **Google only:** editing an avatar and choosing a name tile are for players signed in with Google, to give people a reason to sign in. `google(uid)` is true when the site's sign-in (`SiteAccount.user()`, which only ever reports Google accounts; the home page's when this is its phone frame) or a Firebase app on the page says `uid` is signed in and not anonymous (the phone page opened on its own has no `SiteAccount` but shares the site's sign-in). `edit()`, `save()` and `saveTile()` refuse a guest, `tileMine()` says nothing for one, `editButton()` draws a padlock for one, and `edit()` then fires `backroom:avatar-locked` on `document`, which the Online panel answers by moving to its Sign in with Google button (see `social.js`). An avatar a guest edited before this rule is still drawn and still published (`mine()` and `stats.js`'s `localAv` are unchanged), so it still shows to others; they can't change it until they sign in, and then it carries over (`carryAvatar`). **This is enforced in the page only.** A node doesn't say what kind of account wrote it (Firebase's anonymous and Google uids look alike, and the `ga` mark on a leaderboard entry is written by the entry's own browser), and the shared rules accept any extra field in a player's own node, so readers take a valid `av` or `nt` from any node: a guest who writes one with developer tools, or who edits an avatar on a page still open from before this rule, will be shown. To enforce it for real the database rules (which this repo doesn't hold) would need, on each `players/$uid` node, something like `"nt": { ".validate": "auth.token.firebase.sign_in_provider === 'google.com' && newData.isString() && newData.val().matches(/^t(0|1:[1-9][0-9]?)$/)" }` (and the same provider test on `av` once guests' old edits no longer need to be re-published). A test hook (`window.__avatarT`, only when `navigator.webdriver` is set) lets the automated checks answer `google()`.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/captains.js`: who may see the cosmetics that are not public yet (Back Room). One answer for every page, `BackRoomCaptains.may(open, name, remember)`: true when the feature's own switch is on for everyone (`open`), or the account signed in on the site is a captain, or the test switch `?<name>=1` is used, which works only on localhost or in an automated browser (on the live site the address does nothing; `remember` is a sessionStorage key that keeps it for the browser tab). Driftfall's Locker (`lockerOn`), Boostball's extra bodies and Kit (`garageOn`) and Slipstream's Garage (`garageOn`) ask it; `cosmetics.js` asks `open()`, the same without the captains' pass, so the shop, the wallet, earning and buying stay shut for captains too. **Captains** here are the accounts in `miles.js`'s `OWNERS` only (the ones shown as Captain), not `VICE_CAPTAINS`; the lists stay in `miles.js` and are asked through `BackRoomMiles.isOwner` / `isVice`, and a page without `miles.js` fetches it only once a Google account is signed in (a guest or a signed-out visitor never downloads it, unless a game has to check whether another player wearing an exclusive gift is a captain: see below). **It is Captains only (`VICE_CAPTAINS_TOO` is `false`): Vice Captains see nothing, like everyone else; to let them in too, change that one line to `true`.** The account is the Google sign-in the whole site shares (`account/account.js`), read straight from where Firebase keeps it in the browser (IndexedDB `firebaseLocalStorageDb`, read only), so no Firebase is loaded and nobody is signed in for it; the games' guest sign-ins never count. The answer is false until that first look has finished, so nothing shows for anyone else while a page is still finding out, and it is looked at again when the tab comes back into view, when `account.js` reports a change and when another tab signs in or out (`localStorage` `acct`); `onChange(fn)` tells the games, which show or hide their screens. Game pages load it in the `<head>`; hub pages and the shop load it with `data-switches` (the test switches only: the account is not looked up there). **What captains get:** the three games' screens with every item free to wear. What they wear is saved in their browser as before (`driftfall:skin`; `boostball-gbody` and `boostball-kit`; `ss:kit` and `ss:sticker`) and is sent to the room as before, so every player in a match sees a captain's outfit, car and sticker. **It is not a lock:** the check runs in the visitor's browser and the account ids are public, so someone who edits the page in their own browser can still open these screens; nothing on the server enforces it. **To open a feature to everyone later, flip its own one switch** (`LOCKER_PUBLIC`, `GARAGE_PUBLIC`, `ENABLED`), as before. **Exclusive gifts:** a few items are made for one player alone. `GIFTS` near the top of the file is a small table, account uid → item ids (today one entry: Carbon's account → `bb-goal-carbon-star`, Boostball's Carbon Star, see Boostball above), and `CAPTAIN_GIFTS` lists the gifts the captains may wear too (Carbon Star; Vice Captains do not get it). `gift(item)` says whether the account signed in here has a gift, `gift(item, uid)` whether another player's account has it (a captain is only known once `miles.js` is here: `giftsReady()` fetches it), `account()` gives the signed-in account's uid and `onAccount(fn)` reports when it changes. A game lists a gift only when `gift(item)` is true and draws it only for a player whose account has it, whatever that player's data claims. **To add another gift:** put `'<account uid>': ['<item id>']` in `GIFTS` (and the id in `CAPTAIN_GIFTS` if the captains should have it too); add the item at the end of its game's block in `cosmetics.js` with `give = 'exclusive'` (in no pack or chest, not sold, not giftable, left out of the shop's Locker); and in the game, list it only when `gift(item)` is true and draw it only when `gift(item, uid)` is true for the player it belongs to (Boostball's `giftOk` and `giftScorer` are the pattern). Like everything here this runs in the browser, so it hides a gift rather than locking it. A test hook (`window.__captains`: `as(uid)` a Google account, `as(uid, false)` a guest, `as(null)` signed out, `real()` the browser's own, `gift(uid, items)` adds a `GIFTS` entry for the visit) exists only in an automated browser.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/cosmetics.js`, `wallet.js` and `shop/`: skins, accessories, chests, packs and the coins to buy them with (Back Room). **Switched off**: the one switch is `PAYMENTS.enabled` in `wallet.js` (false; see "Server wallet" below), with `var ENABLED = false;` at the top of `cosmetics.js` also false; while both are false every part of it is hidden (no Shop chip, no wallet, no chests, no skin picker, nothing saved), and `shop/` then only says the shop isn't open yet, except the captains' preview (below). `?cosmetics=1` turns it on for testing, on localhost or in an automated browser only (the check is `captains.js`'s, above, which every page loads before `cosmetics.js`; without it the address does nothing). The site's captains are not let in here early: for them only the three games' own screens open, and the shop, the wallet, earning and buying stay shut like for everyone else (`on()` asks `captains.js`'s `open()`, which leaves captains out). Hub pages load both scripts (`cosmetics.js` with `data-hub`, which adds an "emeralds · Shop" chip to the `.topbar` or to an element marked `data-cosmetics-slot` when switched on). Odd One Out's 33 items (14 hunter outfits, 6 hunter tools, 8 prop taunts and 5 catch poofs) are in `GAME_ITEMS` under `odd-one-out` (prefix `oo`), drawn and played by `odd-one-out/wardrobe.js`, in no pack: captains wear them free in the game's Wardrobe (see Odd One Out above). Chromaflage's 13 Seeker kit items (7 hats, 4 tools, a catch splash and a caught animation; ids `cf-…`) are in `GAMES` as `chromaflage` and in no pack: captains wear them free in the game's Seeker kit (`chromaflage/kit.js`; seekers only) and nothing is sold. Rampline's Captain's garage items (cars, paints, wheels, tyre smoke, horns; game `rampline`, prefix `rl`) are in the catalogue too, drawn by `rampline/garage.js` and free to wear for captains while the shop is shut. Asteroid Run's 39 game items (19 aliens, 10 run trails and 10 tunnel colours) are in `GAME_ITEMS` too, in no pack: captains wear them free in Asteroid Run's Wardrobe, which draws them by slot and number (see Asteroid Run above; the shop's small `itemSvg()` picture is the plain fallback for its aliens and tunnel colours). Shell Crackers' 43 game items (19 egg types, 7 hats, 12 gun skins and 5 crack effects; slots `egg`, `hat`, `gun`, `crack`, ids `sc-…`) are drawn by `shell-crackers/kit.js`, are in no pack yet, and for now are only worn by captains, free, through the game's Locker (see Shell Crackers above). Tumble Trials' 83 Closet items (10 colours, 6 premium colours, 9 patterns, 9 underpants, 14 hats, 6 faces, 12 costumes, 6 victory dances, 6 fall screams and 5 knockout splashes; ids `tt-…`) are in `GAMES` as `'tumble-trials'` and in no pack: captains wear them free in the game's Closet (`tumble-trials/closet.js`) and nothing is sold. Rumble Karts' 53 (17 drivers, 15 karts, 8 wheels, 5 horns and 8 wreck effects) are in `GAMES` as `rumble-karts` but in no pack: today only captains wear them, free, through the game's own Garage (see Rumble Karts above), which keeps what they wear in its own `rk_cos`. Updraft's 40 items (22 skins, 6 backpacks, 3 height rewards marked `give: 'earned'`, 5 fall screams and 4 trails) are in `GAME_ITEMS` under `updraft` (prefix `up`), drawn by `updraft/gear.js`, in no pack, and worn only through the game's own Gear screen for now (captains only; see Updraft above). Coil's 74 game items (colours, animal and design patterns, object snakes, heads, a trail and death bursts) are in `GAME_ITEMS` too, in no pack: captains wear them free in Coil's Wardrobe, which draws them by slot and number (see Coil above). Showdown's 35 items (18 seat looks, 7 card backs, 5 felts and 5 win flourishes; game `showdown`, prefix `sd`) are drawn by `showdown/wardrobe.js`, are in no pack, and are worn free by captains only through the game's Wardrobe (see Showdown above). Puffer Party's 32 items (18 puffer hats, 6 answer trails, 5 pops and 3 victory effects; game `puffer-party`, prefix `pp`) are drawn by `puffer-party/wardrobe.js`, are in no pack, and are worn free by captains only through the game's Wardrobe (see Puffer Party above). Lanternhold's 30 items (12 tower banners, 9 lanterns, 5 flame colours and 4 build bursts; game `lanternhold`, prefix `lh`) are drawn by `lanternhold/wardrobe.js`, are in no pack, and are worn free by captains only through the game's Wardrobe (see Lanternhold above). Sky Duel's 35 items (19 paints, 5 noses, 6 trails and 5 shoot-down effects; slots `paint`, `nose`, `trail`, `boom`, ids `sd-…`) are in `GAMES` as `'sky-duel'` and in no pack: captains wear them free in the game's Paint Shop (`sky-duel/hangar.js`) and nothing is sold. Blockstorm's 30 items (16 outfits, 7 hats and 7 gun skins; slots `outfit`, `hat`, `gun`, ids `bs-…`) are in `GAMES` as `blockstorm` and in no pack: captains wear them free in the game's Locker (`blockstorm/locker.js`) and nothing is sold.
  - **Coins.** Mangos are the small coin and emeralds the big one: 17 mangos make an emerald (13 until 19 Nov 2026; saved test balances were kept as the same number of mangos, since the system has never been public). Emeralds are only bought with real money, in `wallet.js`'s `EMERALD_PACKS`, the owner's six packs as created in Stripe: Emerald Handful 7 for $0.99, Emerald Pouch 39 for $4.99, Emerald Sack 80 for $9.99, Emerald Chest 180 for $19.99, Emerald Buttload 1,000 for $49.99 and Emerald Vault 4,000 for $99.99 (the owner's numbers: every bigger pack is better value on purpose). The server has its own copy of these counts and prices (`functions/_catalog.js`, generated) and never takes a count from Stripe or the page. "Emerald Chest" is a pack of emeralds, not the loot Chest: the shop lists it under "Buy emeralds" with its card saying "180 emeralds". With the server wallet each pack has a Buy button that goes to Stripe Checkout (see "Server wallet"); otherwise the shop shows it as "Not available yet". The packs' old `link: ''` fields are no longer used. Mangos only come from breaking emeralds: the balance is whole mangos, and a price that isn't whole emeralds leaves the change in mangos (shown as "3 emeralds 9 mangos"). `BackRoomWallet.{balance(), inEmeralds(), format(), earn(mangos, why), spend(mangos, why), log()}` fires `backroom:wallet` on `document` when the balance changes.
  - **Earning: off on purpose (19 Nov 2026).** Coins come from real money only: `PLAY_PAYS = false` and `WELCOME = 0` in `wallet.js`, so playing pays nothing and there is no welcome gift (the shop's Wallet tab says so). What follows is how earning works if `PLAY_PAYS` is ever set back to true. Play pays mangos through stats.js's existing hooks. `wallet.js` wraps `BackRoomStats.add/max/min` in the page and in a hub page's game frames, and pays from `EARN`: mangos per unit for counts, per call for bests, and a daily cap per game by the browser's date. Every change is logged in `wallet:log` (the last 200). There's a one-time 20-emerald welcome gift when the shop first opens.
  - **Rarities and prices.** Five rarities, priced the same for skins, accessories and every game item (`PRICE` in `cosmetics.js`, whole mangos): common 60 ($0.50), uncommon 119 (7 emeralds, $0.99), rare 420 (24 emeralds 12 mangos, $3.49), epic 600 (35 emeralds 5 mangos, $4.99) and legendary 1,200 (70 emeralds 10 mangos, $9.99). Every item can be bought on its own from the shop's Locker (`buy('item:<id>')`), including the game items described above as "in no pack"; only items with `give` (handed over by their game) are not sold. Uncommon was added on 19 Nov 2026 by one rule, so the owners can move items later: common, epic and legendary items stayed as they were; of the items that were rare, within each group in file order (skins by theme, accessories by slot, game items by game and slot) the first half, rounded up, became uncommon and the rest stayed rare. For skins that left only one rare (Winter Frost), so every rare chest roll would give the same skin; four were put back to rare by hand (Circuit Scout, Red Fox, Autumn Ember, Magma Walker). Skins were then 12 common, 4 uncommon, 5 rare, 6 epic, 3 legendary (40, 20, 17, 15, 8 since the 19 Nov additions); accessories 8, 6, 3, 5, 1 (20, 17, 10, 9, 3); game items 265, 141, 115, 168, 33. Every rarity must keep at least one skin, or a chest roll of that rarity can't be paid out.
  - **Catalogue.** `SKINS` has 100 skins in the five rarities above. Each has an avatar look and a 3D colour set `colors {body, accent, trim, emissive, pattern}`, and `texture(id, size)` paints the pattern on a canvas. `ACCESSORIES` has 59, one per slot `hat, glasses, cape, trail, aura, pet` worn with any skin; each has a `three` description (`attach, shape, color, color2, size`) for 3D games. All of them are drawn in code. Item `no` numbers are permanent. Skins 31–100 and accessories 24–59 were added on 19 Nov 2026 in the owner's themes: 25 sports skins (soccer, basketball, American football, baseball, hockey, tennis, track, swimming, golf, skateboarding, surfing, skiing, a referee, a coach, boxing, karate, fencing, cheer, a racing driver, a goalkeeper, a wrestling champ, a hawk mascot, Gold Medalist, All-Star and Hall of Famer), 8 pirates with eye patches (up to the Pirate King), 10 suits in costume-party style (business suit, banker, tracksuit with gold chain, 1920s pinstripes and fedora, detective, bodyguard in shades with an earpiece, tuxedo, secret agent, The Don, The Boss in a long coat and fur hat), 5 robots and 22 others (wizards, ninja, DJ, rock star, two superheroes of our own, mummy, vampire, samurai costume, knight, astronaut, chef, firefighter, animals, food, seasons): 28 common, 16 uncommon, 12 rare, 9 epic, 5 legendary, so the chest odds by rarity are unchanged but each skin within a rarity is now rarer. The new accessories are sports headgear, a fedora, tricorn, bowler, flat cap and fur hat, eye patch, shades, aviators, eye black, a cyborg eye, a bow tie and gold chain (worn in the cape slot, `attach: 'neck'`), a number cape, ball, coin and music trails, a spotlight and other auras, and a parrot, penguin chick, robot pup, drone and the legendary Pocket Kraken; they come in 18 new packs at the end of `PACKS` (epic and legendary skins only from chests, as before). Their drawings are new parts after `accFront` in `cosmetics.js` (`bgMore`, `outfitMore`, `hatPart`, `facePiece`, `fxPart`, `accBackMore`, `accFrontMore`), reached only through the old switches' `default`, so every older skin and accessory draws exactly the same SVG as before; looks may now carry `face` (eye patch, shades, moustache, beard, masks), `fx` (animated SVG shine, gold frame, orbiting stars, twinkles: the legendaries and some epics), `num` (a kit number), `h1`/`h2` (head piece colours) and `nohat` (hides avatar.js's own hat). The shop has no Spanish strings, so the names are English only.
  - **Game items.** `GAME_ITEMS` holds looks worn inside one game instead of on the avatar; today they are Boostball's 211 (62 decals, 49 boost trails, 42 goal explosions and Carbon Star, an exclusive gift with `give: 'exclusive'` that is in no pack, never sold, gifted or drawn from a chest and left out of the shop's Locker, 36 antenna toppers, 12 roof hats, 5 sets of wheels, 4 engine sounds; see Boostball's Kit above) and Slipstream's 76 (the next point). One entry per game in `GAMES` gives its name, id prefix and slots. Each has its game, one of that game's slots (`GAMES`), a number `no` that is permanent within its game and slot (the game sends it to other players and draws by it, so add new ones at the end and never renumber), a rarity that sets its price (`PRICE`, above) and two colours for the small picture `itemSvg()` draws. They are sold in packs of kind `items` (19 Boostball packs and 8 Slipstream packs, each priced at the sum of its items; shown under "Boostball packs" and "Slipstream packs" in the shop), worn with `equip(id)` and taken off with `unequip('boostball:decal')`, shown in the shop's Locker under their own chips, and read by the game with `gameEquipped('boostball')`. They are not mirrored into the Google account yet (`w.a` holds at most 128 item numbers, and a new field in `w` needs the rules checked with a signed-in account), so they stay in the browser that bought them. Driftfall's 246 (87 outfits, 22 backpacks, 42 gliders, 14 glider trails, 37 gun skins, 6 build skins, 22 emotes, 4 victory poses, 12 pickaxes; game `drop-zone`, prefix `dz`) are worn from the game's own Locker by captains only and are in no pack: see Driftfall's Locker extras above. Spire's, Turbo Trail's and Escapade's 37 each (33 outfits and 4 celebrations; games `spire`, `turbo-trail` and `escapade`, prefixes `sp`, `tr` and `ec`) are worn from each game's own Wardrobe by captains only and are in no pack; the outfits, the same in all three, are drawn by `outfits.js` (see those games above).
  - **Game items: Slipstream.** Slipstream's 76 are 15 sponsors, 23 liveries (8 patterns and the 15 sponsors' own), 23 helmets, 8 boost effects, 5 tyre smoke colours and 2 podium celebrations. The 38 that are sold come in the eight Slipstream packs (the Garage in the game buys the same packs). The other 38 are in no pack and carry `give`: `'free'` (the sponsors and the eight plain helmets: anyone may wear them, and the game puts one in the locker the first time it is worn) or `'earned'` (a sponsor's livery, handed over when its challenges are finished); `grant(id)` adds one of these and refuses anything that is sold. Worn with `equip(id)`, taken off with `unequip('slipstream:livery')`, read by the game with `gameEquipped('slipstream')`; the game draws them (`slipstream/garage.js`), and its page loads `cosmetics.js` and `wallet.js` itself so its Garage can buy. Kept in `cosmetics:v1` (`g`, `ge`) and not mirrored into the account yet (this browser only).
  - **Chests** (called draws inside the code: pack ids `draw-1/5/10`, kind `draws`, `draw()`). A chest costs 7 emeralds (119 mangos; 5 chests 35 emeralds, 10 chests 70, no discount), is bought with emeralds only, and gives one random skin. Odds are weights out of 10,000, shown in the shop next to the chest: common 6,000 (60%), uncommon 2,000 (20%), rare 1,250 (12.5%), epic 500 (5%), legendary 250 (2.5%). They are picked with an unbiased whole-number roll: `crypto.getRandomValues` in play, `seeded(n)` in tests. A duplicate gives back 48 mangos (40% of a chest, rounded).
  - **Packs.** `PACKS` holds what is bought with emeralds, with prices in whole mangos: chests (1, 5 and 10), themed skin packs, accessory packs and game item packs. Every pack of items costs the sum of its items' prices. `buy(id)` (a pack id, or `item:<id>` for one item) checks the wallet, takes the mangos, fills the locker and refunds duplicates; something already fully owned is refused, and an emerald pack id is refused with reason `money`.
  - **Captains.** Captains (`captains.js`, which asks miles.js's `OWNERS`; Vice Captains are not included) have no limit while the cosmetics are on: the shop's wallet and the Shop chip show "∞", `spend()` never charges them (a log line says it was free), every skin, accessory and game item counts as owned (`load()` in `cosmetics.js` lists everything, and `save()` writes back only what they really own, so nothing extra is mirrored into their account or kept after they sign out), they are never paid rewards, and their gifts are free. The shop page now loads `captains.js` without `data-switches` so it can tell who is signed in.
  - **Weekly titles.** Every week (Monday to Sunday, the boards' own week), whoever is #1 on a game's main board gets 10 mangos for that game, for each week they hold it, and 10 more for each other game they top; ties for #1 all get it. The main board is the first board `leaderboard.js` lists for the game after Hours, all time: Rumble Karts, Shell Crackers, Backrooms 1v1–4v4, Driftfall, Lokop, Slipstream, Boostball, Chromaflage, Turbo Trail, Odd One Out, Tumble Trials, Deepword, Puffer Party and Sky Duel: Elo; Blockstorm: Most kills in a match; Coil: Longest snake; Trivium: Best Daily score; Showdown: Biggest pot (practice); Spire: Fastest tower; Escapade: Stars; Updraft: Fastest to the Crown; Asteroid Run: Longest Endless run; Rampline: Medals; Tidewick, Gridpop, Melon Merge, Patchwork Tower and Flickerwood: Best score; 2048 and Stackfall: Best score (Classic); Lanternhold: Most waves held; Daily Puzzles: Best daily streak; Hunch, Fourcast, Quartets, Nearword, Warmer, Hotspot, Outline, Unfurl, Overland and Poopdle: Daily wins; Crosslet: Clean daily solves; Sleight: Daily clues solved; Ninefold: Daily puzzles solved; Deadline Day, Journeyman, Scout Report and Teamsheet: Most guesses to spare; Crossbar: Most squares filled; Back Four: Most mistakes to spare; Stat Duel: Best daily run; Top Bins: Most entries found. The winner's own page claims it at its first scan after the week ends (`wallet.js` `scan()`, at most every 10 minutes per browser on hub pages and every time the shop opens), judged from the boards as they stand at that moment, since nothing records how they stood on Sunday night. A captain at the top is not paid and nobody else is paid for that board: `leaderboard.js` shows captains like anyone, so they keep the top spot. The claimed week is kept as `tw`.
  - **Weekly Aisle reward.** The Aisle that wins last week's contest in `aisles.js` (most miles flown; a tie: every tied Aisle) gets 100 mangos split evenly: each member gets floor(100 / members) (16 each for 6), and the leftover mangos are not paid. Each member's own page claims their share on the Back Room page (it needs `aisles.js` and the boards read there), once per week (`aw`), using the Aisle's members at that moment.
  - **Gifts.** Every skin, accessory, game item, pack and chest in the shop has a Gift button next to Buy: the same price (free for captains), sent to someone picked from the people online (`social.js`'s room), the sender's Aisle and party, each of whom must be signed in with Google; an optional note of up to 60 characters goes through `/namefilter.js`. Emeralds and mangos can't be gifted, and a single skin or accessory their account is known to own is refused (game items aren't mirrored, so those and anything in a pack already owned become the duplicate refund for the recipient). Players can only write their own node, so the gift is written into the sender's own `w.gs` (at most 3 unopened at a time); the recipient's page finds it in its next scan, puts it in their locker (a chest opens there), shows "Morgan sent you a gift: Golden Egg" and records the sender's newest claimed gift time in its own `w.gc`, which is how the sender's page knows to forget it. Both sides get a log line. The sender must be signed in with Google so the gift can be published.
  - **Rewards with the server wallet: off.** Titles, Aisle rewards and the node gifts above are worked out in the player's own browser, so with the server wallet in use `wallet.js` claims none of them (`grant()` refuses, `scan()` only reads the server wallet) and gifts go through `/api/wallet/gift`. Weekly titles and the Aisle reward need a scheduled job on the server before they can come back. What it would need: a Cloudflare Worker Cron Trigger (Pages Functions can't run on a schedule; a small separate Worker with the same three secrets, or a scheduled GitHub Action calling a secret-protected endpoint) that runs once after each week ends (Monday, by one fixed time zone, since today's weeks follow each browser's clock); reads `rooms/lb-all/players` from the Realtime Database with the service account (it needs the `firebase.database` scope as well as Firestore's) and works out the #1 of each game's main board with the same rules as `leaderboard.js` (its `parse`, `collapse` and `rows` would have to be shared with the server, and the boards as they stand then are all there is: nothing records Sunday night), and the winning Aisle from `aisles.js`'s data the same way; then pays each winner in one Firestore transaction per wallet, keyed by the week (e.g. `rewards/{week}_{uid}` written in the same transaction, so a rerun pays nothing twice), skipping captains, with a ledger line like any other.
  - **For games.** `BackRoomCosmetics.equipped()` returns `{skin, accessories: {slot: id}, colors}`, and `avatarSvg(uid, size)` returns the avatar wearing them.
  - **Storage.** Everything is in this browser: `wallet:v1` (`base` balance at last sync, `pend` change since, `su` synced uid, `dc` today's earnings per game, `dv` browser id), `wallet:dev`, `wallet:log` and `cosmetics:v1` (`s`, `a` owned ids, `e` worn per slot; `g` owned game items and `ge` worn per `game:slot`, written only once a game item is owned). Signed in with Google, the wallet is mirrored into the player's own stats node `rooms/lb-all/players/<uid>` as `w {v, b (mangos), s, a (owned bitmasks by item number), e ('s12,h3'), lh (log hash), t, tw, aw (claimed weeks), gc (claimed gifts: sender uid to time), gs (unopened gifts sent)}`, kept under 950 characters so the node stays under the boards' 8,000. This is a transaction on `w` that adds this browser's `pend` to the node's balance, so devices add up. The mirror waits until the node exists, and it rewrites `w` when a stats.js publish drops it.
  - **Local mode is not cheat-proof.** In the old browser-only mode (now only the `?cosmetics=1` test switch) the balance is self-reported: a player can edit their own storage and node. The server wallet below is what real money uses.
  - **Server wallet (emeralds for real money).** Emeralds are sold for real money, so the balance and everything bought with it are held and changed only by the server: Cloudflare Pages Functions in `functions/` (`_wallet.js` and `api/wallet/`), writing Firestore with the service account, like the item shop (see `store.js` and `functions/` below). The server wallet is in use whenever `BackRoomCosmetics.server()` is true: `wallet.js`'s `PAYMENTS.enabled` (the one switch, false today), `ENABLED`, or the captains' preview. Then `wallet.js` keeps no balance of its own: it reads the balance, what's owned, gift notices and the last 40 ledger lines from `GET /api/wallet`, and sends every purchase (`POST /api/wallet/buy`, a pack or `item:<id>`), chest (`POST /api/wallet/chest`, 1, 5 or 10, rolled on the server with crypto randomness) and gift (`POST /api/wallet/gift`, to a uid, note at most 60 characters) to the server; `POST /api/wallet/seen` drops gift notices the page has shown. `cosmetics.js` takes what's owned from the server (only what is worn, and the free items a game hands over itself, stay in `cosmetics:v1`) and its `buy()` then returns a promise (`slipstream/garage.js` handles that). Every endpoint needs the same verified Google sign-in as the item shop; signed out, the shop says "Buying needs a Google sign-in". Each change is one Firestore transaction (read, check balance, debit, grant, ledger) retried on conflicts, and each click carries a request id the server does only once, so double clicks and concurrent requests can't overspend. Limits, kept in the wallet document (`lim`) and checked in the same transaction so they cost nothing extra: 20 changes a minute per player, 30 gifts sent a day (captains 200), 40 gifts received a day per player, and 120 calls a minute per player per Cloudflare instance (best effort, reads included); a request body over 4 KB is refused. The server enforces the same numbers as the page: 17 mangos an emerald; items by rarity 60, 119, 420, 600, 1,200 mangos; a pack costs the sum of its items; a chest 119 mangos at 60 / 20 / 12.5 / 5 / 2.5 %, equal chance within a rarity; 48 mangos back for each skin or pack item already owned; captains (miles.js's `OWNERS`) are never charged, own everything, are never paid refunds, and can't be sent gifts; a gift costs the sender the same price, can only go to someone whose wallet exists (they have opened the shop once, signed in with Google), and a single item or a pack they already fully own is refused; a captain's free gift pays the recipient no duplicate refunds (otherwise a captain could make mangos for anyone by sending chests to a player who owns every skin); no sequence of buys, packs, chests and gifts makes mangos, because a duplicate pays back 48 while every item costs at least 60, a chest 119 and every pack more than 48 for each thing in it, so each one takes more from the payer than it can pay back to anyone (`server-data.js` refuses to write a catalogue where that stops being true, and a server test plays 250 random purchases and gifts to check it); the note (at most 60 characters, longer is refused) and the names shown with it go through the server's copy of `/namefilter.js` (`nameClean` in `functions/_wallet.js`, its word lists generated into `functions/_namefilter.js`), which masks a rude word and refuses a note hiding one of the worst ones. Firestore: `wallets/{uid}` (`balance` in whole mangos, below zero after a refund or chargeback took back mangos already spent, `owned {item id: time}` (an item the catalogue no longer lists is kept, not dropped), `lim` the rate-limit counters, `recent` last 40 ledger lines, `notices` unseen gifts, `reqs` the last 30 request ids), `ledger/{id}` (one row per change, never edited: `uid, t, d, b, w, r, kind`; `kind` `reversal` is mangos taken back for a refund or chargeback, or given back when a chargeback is won) and the existing `orders/{Checkout session id}` (for emeralds also `kind: 'emeralds'`, `mangos`, `pi` the payment intent, and `status` `credited`, `mismatch`, `unclaimed` (with `reason`), `reversed` (refunded before it was credited, so it never will be), `refunded`, `partly-refunded` or `disputed`, with `refunded` cents, `dispute` Stripe's dispute status and `clawed` mangos taken back). The pages never read or write these directly: the Firestore rules must keep them closed to browsers (the exact rules are in the setup checklist below; the server's safety depends on them, and this change doesn't touch the rules). **What can't be made cheat-proof:** what a player sees on their own screen (their page is theirs to edit, so they can draw any skin for themselves); other players' screens are covered only in games that use `BackRoomOwned` below; a stolen Google sign-in (or a script injected into any page on ascoding11.github.io, which can read the sign-in) can spend that player's balance; a Firebase sign-in that is disabled stays valid for up to an hour. The server's numbers come from the browser files: `node e63334287a3deaa3/shop/tools/server-data.js` writes `functions/_catalog.js` (from `cosmetics.js` and `wallet.js`, including `PAYMENTS.enabled`) `functions/_captains.js` (from miles.js's `OWNERS`) and `functions/_namefilter.js` (`/namefilter.js`'s word lists, ROT13 as there); run it after changing any price, item, pack, emerald pack, captain or filtered word and commit all three; `--check` fails while they are out of date. Tests: `node e63334287a3deaa3/shop/tests/server.test.mjs` (the functions with Stripe and Firestore faked) and, with `python3 -m http.server 8962` running from the repo root, `node e63334287a3deaa3/shop/tests/shop.test.mjs` (the shop in Playwright, its API answered by the real functions over the fakes).
  - **Buying emeralds.** The shop's emerald packs have a Buy button that calls `BackRoomWallet.buyEmeralds(id)`, which uses `store.js`'s `Store.buy('emeralds_7')` (and so on): Google sign-in, then `/api/checkout`, which for the six emerald lookup keys skips the "already owned" check (they are consumables), refuses anyone but captains while `PAYMENTS.enabled` is false, and refuses a Stripe price whose amount or currency doesn't match the pack. Stripe sends the tab back to the shop with `?purchase=success&sku=emeralds_7&session=cs_…`; the webhook, and only the webhook, credits `wallets/{uid}` with emeralds × 17 mangos and a ledger line, exactly once per Checkout session (it reads and writes `orders/{session id}` in the same transaction), and only when the amount and currency paid match the pack (otherwise it records the order as `mismatch` and credits nothing). The player credited is the `metadata.uid` our checkout wrote (with `kind: 'emeralds'`), never `client_reference_id`, which a Stripe Payment Link fills from its own address (`?client_reference_id=` anyone): a Payment Link payment, a session not made by `/api/checkout`, or an event whose `livemode` doesn't match the key in Cloudflare (a test payment while the live key is set, or the other way round) credits nobody and is written to `orders/{session id}` as `unclaimed` with a `reason`, for the owners to refund or credit by hand; events from a connected account (`event.account`) and signatures older than 5 minutes are ignored. **Refunds and chargebacks take emeralds back**: `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created` and `charge.dispute.closed` find the Checkout session from the payment intent (a Stripe API call) and take back the pack's mangos (all of them while a dispute is open or lost, else the share refunded, rounded up), in one transaction with the order, worked out as a target so a repeated or out-of-order event changes nothing twice and a won dispute gives them back. The balance may go below zero: such a wallet can't buy, open chests or send gifts (`reason: 'negative'`) and counts as owning nothing in `/api/owned?uids=` until emeralds top it up; items already bought stay in the wallet. The shop waits for that ledger line and then shows the new balance. Whether money is real is decided only by the Stripe key in Cloudflare: with a test key (`sk_test_…`) the shop shows a "Test mode" tag (`GET /api/wallet` says `test: true`; the key itself is never sent).
  - **All sales are final; Payment help.** The site never offers a refund: "All sales are final. No refunds." is shown under each emerald pack's Buy button in the shop and on Stripe's own page above the Pay button (`custom_text.submit.message`, `FINAL_TEXT` in `functions/api/checkout.js`; Stripe's `consent_collection` is not used, it needs dashboard setup). A refund the owners make by hand in the Stripe dashboard, or a chargeback a bank forces, takes the emeralds' mangos back automatically (see "Buying emeralds"; the webhook events are in the setup checklist). Someone with a problem uses the **Payment help** link under the emerald packs (also shown with "Your emeralds haven’t arrived yet." when a purchase didn't show up within 45 seconds): their email (required), what happened (required, at most 500 characters), and automatically their account id, the Checkout session they came back from (if any) and the wallet's last 5 ledger lines (time, change, what, and the Stripe session id for emerald purchases); never card data, which the site doesn't have. It is sent through `feedback.js`'s `BackRoomFeedback.send` (loaded on demand; the shop has no suggestion box) as one sealed note starting `Payment help:`, so the owners find it on the admin page's Feedback tab and email back one to one; the form says "Sent. We'll email you, usually within a few days." One request a day per account in this browser (`localStorage['payhelp:v1']`), on top of feedback.js's own limits (one a minute, five a day). The shop now loads `lang.js`: only these new words have Spanish so far (and lang.js's shared words, such as Cancel and Close, follow the language too). **For the owners:** `privacy.html` should get a sentence saying the Payment help form collects an email address, used only to answer that request (not changed here).
  - **Captains' preview.** While `PAYMENTS.enabled` is false, a captain who opens `shop/` on the live site gets the real shop with the server wallet in that browser tab: once `captains.js` confirms the signed-in account is a captain, the page sets `sessionStorage['cosmetics:preview']` and opens, with a bar saying it is the captains' preview. Everyone else still sees "not open yet", and the server refuses everyone but captains (`functions/_catalog.js` `PUBLIC: false`), so the preview can't be opened by anyone else by editing their browser. If the account stops being a captain the tab goes back to "not open yet". **To turn the preview off**: set `var CAPTAIN_PREVIEW = false;` near the top of `cosmetics.js` (nothing else; the server needs no change). `?preview=0` on the shop page ends it for one tab.
  - **Wearing only what you own (`owned.js`, for games).** Buying is checked on the server, but each game decides in the browser what a player wears, so a player can edit their own page and wear any skin, and other players' pages would draw it. `GET https://ascoding11.com/api/owned?uids=a,b,c` (public, no sign-in, at most 24 uids, cached 60 s at Cloudflare's edge by the sorted list, cache misses limited per address) answers `{ v: 1, players: { uid: '*' | [item ids] }, give: [item ids] }`: `'*'` is a captain (owns everything), the list is the wallet's `owned` (gifts included) plus item-shop skus from `entitlements`, empty for an unknown uid or a wallet below zero, and `give` the free and earned items anyone may wear (a game hands those out in the browser, so the server can't know). No balance, history, notes or email are ever in it. `e63334287a3deaa3/owned.js` wraps it: **to wire it into a game**, add `<script src="../owned.js"></script>` after `cosmetics.js`, call `BackRoomOwned.want([uids])` as players join, and wherever the game draws ANOTHER player's skin, accessory or item id (never the local player's own, which the page already knows), use it only when `!BackRoomOwned.active() || BackRoomOwned.check(uid, id) === true`, otherwise draw the default look; `check()` returns `true`, `false`, or `undefined` while the answer isn't in yet or the server can't be reached (draw the default look then too: it never throws), and `BackRoomOwned.onChange(fn)` calls `fn(uids)` when answers arrive so the game can redraw those players. `active()` is true once `PAYMENTS.enabled` is on; until then nothing is sold and games needn't check. Answers are batched (24 uids a request) and kept 60 seconds, so a game adds one small request a minute at most. The uid is the player's Firebase uid (the one games already put in their room data); a player not signed in with Google owns nothing on the server, so they are drawn with the default look once a game checks. No game uses it yet.
  - **Setup checklist for the owners (nothing here works until this is done).** 1. Cloudflare (Workers & Pages → the site → Settings → Variables and secrets), names only, values never in the repo: `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` (start with the test key `sk_test_…`), `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (`whsec_…` from step 3), `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` (the whole JSON key of a service account of project rumblekarts that can write Firestore). The item shop already uses these three; nothing new is needed if they are set. 2. Stripe, in test mode first: on each of the six emerald prices set the lookup key `emeralds_7`, `emeralds_39`, `emeralds_80`, `emeralds_180`, `emeralds_1000`, `emeralds_4000` (one-time prices in USD of exactly $0.99, $4.99, $9.99, $19.99, $49.99, $99.99; any other amount is refused). 3. Stripe → Developers → Webhooks: an endpoint `https://ascoding11.com/api/stripe-webhook` with the events `checkout.session.completed`, `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded`, `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created` and `charge.dispute.closed` (the item shop's endpoint, if it exists, already is this: tick the three `charge.` events on it; without them refunds and chargebacks don't take emeralds back); its signing secret is `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`. Only this one endpoint, in the mode of the key in Cloudflare: don't tick "Listen to events on Connected accounts" for it. Payment Links (the six test ones) credit nobody: archive them, and refund any payment that shows up in Firestore `orders` as `unclaimed`. 3b. Firebase → Firestore → Rules: confirm, without changing anything else, that no rule lets a browser read or write `wallets`, `ledger`, `orders` or `entitlements`; the server writes with the service account, which skips the rules, so the minimal rules are `match /wallets/{id} { allow read, write: if false; }` and the same four lines for `ledger/{id}`, `orders/{id}` and `entitlements/{id}`, and there must be NO wide rule such as `match /{document=**} { allow read, write: if … }` or a test-mode `request.time < …` rule (Firestore allows a request when ANY rule allows it, so a wide rule opens these too: a player could then write their own balance). 3c. Cloudflare (optional, recommended): Security → WAF → Rate limiting rules, one rule for paths starting `/api/` (for example 60 requests per 10 seconds per IP, block for 10 seconds); the server's own limits are per player and per instance, and this one covers floods before they reach Firestore. 4. Check it worked: signed in with Google as a captain, open `https://ascoding11.com/e63334287a3deaa3/shop/`; the preview bar and the "Test mode" tag show, and the balance shows "∞". Buy the Emerald Handful with Stripe's test card 4242 4242 4242 4242; you come back to the shop, which says "Added 7 emeralds"; in Firebase's Firestore console `wallets/<your uid>` has `balance` 119 and a `ledger` row, and `orders/<cs_test_…>` says `credited`; in Stripe the webhook delivery shows 200. Open a chest and send a gift to the other owner (who must have opened the shop once): their `wallets` document shows the item and a notice. 5. To go live: swap both Stripe values in Cloudflare together for the live key and the live webhook's secret (with the same lookup keys on the live prices; while only one is swapped every payment is recorded `unclaimed` with reason `mode` and credits nobody), set `PAYMENTS.enabled: true` in `wallet.js`, run `node e63334287a3deaa3/shop/tools/server-data.js`, and commit `wallet.js` and `functions/_catalog.js` together.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/miles.js`: status tiers, "miles with us" (Back Room). Every player is **Economy**, **Business Class**, **First Class** or **Private Jet**, except the site's two owners, who are always **Captain** (see below), shown as a small pill next to their name in the Online panel, the Party panel, the Leaderboards and the admin page (loaded in the same four places as `avatar.js`). `window.BackRoomMiles = {of(node, uid, now), tierOf(miles), isOwner(uid), badge(result, {miles}), line(result), peek(uid), load(uid), fmt, TIERS, RULES, CAPTAIN, OWNERS, setTops(tops), topOf(uid), needTops(), awardsOf(result), aceOf(result), crackerOf(result), AWARDS, AWARD_KINDS}`. `of` takes a leaderboard node (`rooms/lb-all/players/<uid>`, raw or already parsed by `leaderboard.js`), checks it with `BackRoomBoards.parse` (the same caps and ranges the boards use, so a stale, oversized or impossible node is simply Economy with `ok: false`, and nothing it contains is ever shown as HTML) and gives `{miles, tier, next: {tier, miles} or null at the top, hours, play, bonus, ok}`. **The formula:** 100 miles per hour played (the hours the boards accept: at most 24 a day since 21 October 2026, all games together) plus points for success in each game, each kind capped so no single game carries someone (the table is `RULES` in the file): running counts, wins 40 each in Rumble Karts, Backrooms and Boostball (cap 4,000), kills 2 in Shell Crackers and Backrooms (2,000), wrecks 2 (2,000), goals 10 (2,000), Coil cut-offs 5 (2,000), Chromaflage hiders found 10 (2,000) and rounds hidden 20 (2,000), Odd One Out props found 5 (2,000) and rounds won 20 (2,000), Tumble Trials crowns 50 (4,000) and shows 5 (1,000), Spire towers 20 (2,000), Trivium streak 30 a day (1,500), Showdown hands 2 (1,000), Gridpop lines 2 (1,000), Stackfall lines 5 (1,000), watermelons 50 (1,500), Puffer Party online wins 20 (2,000) and answers 1 (1,000), daily puzzles finished (the Daily Puzzles page's `done`) 5 (2,000) and its best daily streak 10 a day (1,000); personal bests, Elo above 1,200 at 4 a point (3,000 at 1,950 Elo), fastest Spire tower under 10 minutes at 3 a second (1,500 at 100 s), fastest obby under an hour at 0.4 a second (1,200 at 10 min), fastest Updraft summit under an hour at 0.5 a second (1,500 at 10 min), Updraft height ÷ 2 (500 at the summit), a Slipstream lap within 2.5× the track's quickest possible lap up to 200 a track (2,000 for all tracks) and 100 a course record held (1,000), a 2048 tile of 2048 (500), 4096 (1,000) or 8192 and up (1,500) and best score ÷ 100 (500), Gridpop score ÷ 50 (1,000), Stackfall Classic and Sand scores ÷ 200 (1,000 each), Melon Merge score ÷ 10 (1,500), Coil's longest snake ÷ 10 (1,500), Trivium's best Daily ÷ 10 (1,500) and Showdown's biggest pot ÷ 200 (500). **Tiers:** Economy under 2,000, Business Class 2,000–9,999, First Class 10,000–39,999, Private Jet 40,000 and up. So an hour a day for three weeks is Business Class; First Class takes about 100 hours, or 50 with a lot of winning (a 1,950 Elo, 100 kart wins and an 8192 tile are 8,500 on their own); Private Jet is a year of play. **Captain:** a tier above Private Jet for the site's owners, Nico and Arjun, listed by account uid in `OWNERS` near the top of `miles.js` (Nico's two accounts and Arjun's three, each with a comment saying whose it is; add or delete a line to change it, and never match on names, since anyone can type any name). An owner's `of` result has `tier` Captain, `next: null`, `owner: true` and `real`, the tier their miles alone would earn; their `miles` are worked out exactly like everyone else's, so the admin page's Miles column shows and sorts by their real miles (with the Captain badge beside it), and nobody else's tier or the maths changes. The badge is pilot's wings in gold on a near-black navy pill with a gold rim (`.bra-tier-captain`), and on an owner's own Online card the progress line is "12,345 miles · flies the plane" instead of "N to <next tier>" (`line(result)`). **Arjun (site founder):** one step above Captain, for Arjun alone (the site is his): Captain flies the plane, the founder built the airline. The tier is named Arjun, with a bow for its icon (Arjuna, the archer of the Mahabharata). His three accounts are in `FOUNDER_LIST` just under `OWNERS` (each must also stay in `OWNERS`, so `captains.js` and everything else a Captain gets is unchanged; only the badge differs). The badge is a gold drawn bow with an arrow nocked and pale-gold "Arjun" on a violet storm with a gold rim and a purple glow, with a flash of light sweeping across it every few seconds (`.bra-tier-founder`; still for anyone with reduced motion turned on), the same size as every other pill; the tooltip says "Arjun · site founder" and his Online card's line is "12,345 miles · site founder". `of` gives `tier` `FOUNDER` (named Arjun) with `owner: true` and `founder: true`; `isFounder(uid)` and `FOUNDER` are exported. **Every badge:** a **Badges** button in the Online panel's header, beside Cabin chat (`social.js`; on a phone the panel's buttons share their own row under the title), and another beside Refresh at the top of the Leaderboards (everywhere `leaderboard.js` draws them: the Back Room, the phone page, Slipstream and the admin page) open `BackRoomMiles.guide()`, a window listing every badge there is and how to get it: the four miles tiers, the crew (Vice Captain, Captain, Arjun), the Miles board's #1–#3 tags, and every award in `AWARD_KINDS` (the given ones, then the earned Slipstream ace and Top Cracker). Its words never state hours (players see no hours anywhere but the admin page): miles "come from time spent playing", Business Class is "a few weeks of regular play" and First Class "a lot of play, or about half as much with a lot of winning". Each is drawn by the same code as the real badges, so a new tier style or a new line in `AWARD_KINDS` shows up there by itself; it names no holders and reads nothing. The Leaderboards' button stays hidden until `miles.js` has loaded; the Online panel's does nothing on a page without it. This is a display rule, not a security feature: tiers are worked out in each viewer's browser from public data, so it only decides how pages draw the badge and grants nothing. The score rules were tuned against the live boards a week after they began (85 accounts: 77 Economy, 7 Business, 1 First under the first draft, several Business with under an hour of play because one good Stackfall or Coil sitting hit a cap), so a single sitting of a puzzle game is now worth a few hundred miles at most. Worked examples: 10 h of Coil + 100 cut-offs + an 8,000-long snake = 1,000 + 500 + 800 = 2,300 (Business); 50 h of Shell Crackers + Elo 1,950 + 3,000 kills = 5,000 + 3,000 (the cap) + 2,000 (the cap) = 10,000 (First Class exactly). For people online, `peek(uid)` gives the cached result (and starts a read when there is none or it is over 10 minutes old) and `load(uid)` a promise of it: one read of `rooms/lb-all/players/<uid>` per uid through `party.js`'s sign-in; a finished read fires `backroom:miles` on `document` (`detail.uid`), and so does the file when it loads. The Leaderboards and the admin page already hold every parsed node, so they call `of` directly. **Everything behind a tier is self-reported** by the player's own browser (see `stats.js`), so tiers are for fun, like the boards; nothing here writes to the database, and `stats.js`'s node format is unchanged. On the Leaderboards (`leaderboard.js`), rows show the player's avatar and tier badge (the tier worked out from the same checked node, so a node the boards would skip can't earn one) on a second line under the name, and the first board, **Miles**, ranks everyone by these miles (All time, the same number as the badge) or by miles from this week's play time (This week: `HOUR`, exported as `BackRoomMiles.HOUR`, per hour since Monday; bests and counts have no date, so they stay in All time). The badge always shows the all-time tier, on the weekly board too. **#1, #2, #3 tags:** the top three on the Miles board get a small squared tag after their tier pill, "#1 all time", "#2 all time", "#3 all time" and the same for this week (one player can hold one of each; the tooltip says "Ranked #2 in miles, all time"), everywhere the badge is drawn: Leaderboards rows, the Online panel (cards, your own row, an open chat's header), the Party panel and the admin page. All-time tags are solid gold, silver and bronze; this week's are the same three metals as text and rim on slate, so a week's medal reads as a step below an all-time one. The places are the board's own ranks, so after a tie for first nobody is second and the next player is third. `setTops` takes `all2`, `all3`, `week2`, `week3` beside `all` and `week`, and `topOf(uid).all` / `.week` are the place (1 to 3, or 0); an answer saved in `lb:tops` by a page from before this has only the firsts. It all happens inside `badge()`, so no panel works out ranks: for a leader `badge()` returns a `<span class="bra-tw">` holding the usual pill and the tags (`.bra-top-all`, `.bra-top-week`), for everyone else the plain pill exactly as before (`of` results now carry `uid`, which is how the badge knows whose it is). Who is first comes from `leaderboard.js`, not from here: after every read of the boards it calls `setTops({all: [uid…], week: [uid…], allTie, weekTie})` with the uids its own rows rank 1 (`BackRoomBoards.tops`), so the tag uses the same checked nodes, the same week and the same tie rule as the board and always agrees with it. **A tie for first gives every tied player the tag** (the board shows rank 1 beside each of them; the tooltip adds "tied for first"), an account shown as one row with the leader (merged) is tagged too, nobody on This week means no weekly tag, and a node the boards skip or an account the admin removed can never be first. No reads per row and no writes: the answer is kept in memory and in this browser's localStorage (`lb:tops`, with the time), shared by every Back Room page and tab (a `storage` event passes it on). When a badge is drawn and the answer is over 15 minutes old (`TOPS_TTL`), miles.js asks the boards to read once (`BackRoomBoards.readTops()`, the same single read of `rooms/lb-all/players` plus `rooms/lb-rs/players` the Leaderboards section makes anyway, about 330 KB in November 2026; the section then reuses it for 5 minutes instead of reading again when scrolled to), at most once a minute and never in a tab nobody is looking at; an answer over an hour old (`TOPS_KEEP`) isn't shown. So the cost is at most one board read per browser per 15 minutes while a badge is on screen, where before only people who scrolled to the Leaderboards read it; raise `TOPS_TTL` to make it cheaper. A change of who is first fires `backroom:miles` with `detail.tops`, so panels redraw. The words are dropped to just "#1" where the row hasn't room (a phone row, a narrow party card): once the badge is on the page `fit()` checks whether the pill is being squeezed and adds `bra-tw-s`, again on resize; tags are never taller than the pill, so rows keep their height. With no connection, an empty board or a leader who isn't on the page, nothing shows and nothing breaks. The admin page hands over its own answer from the nodes it has read (`BackRoomBoards.topsOf(nodes, now)`, which reads them as the public boards do, without removed or merged accounts). **Merged accounts:** when the admin page has merged accounts into one person (a signed `a`), a badge loaded for any of them (`load`, for the Online and Party panels) reads the signed merges (at most once per 10 minutes) and the other accounts' nodes, and shows the miles of the merged person (leaderboard.js `collapse`), so the hours and counts each account earned are added back. **Awards:** an honour the owners give to a particular player, shown as a small medal on a ribbon after the tier pill and any #1 tags, everywhere the badge is drawn (it is added inside `badge()`, like the tags, so no panel knows about it; an award holder's badge is the same `bra-tw` wrapper with a `<span class="bra-aw">` at the end). It is not a tier or a rank and has nothing to do with miles: a round medal with the award's mark, and its name on a swallow-tailed ribbon in the game's own colours, so it can't be taken for a tier pill or a gold, silver or bronze tag. The first is **Updraft devotee** (tooltip "Awarded for dedication to Updraft"; a summit with a rising arrow in Updraft's night navy, sky blue and sunrise orange), held by Morgan, who was first on the Miles board all time when it was given (November 2026), with the most Updraft play time on the boards and the only climb to the Crown. The second is **Boostball star** (tooltip "Awarded for dedication to Boostball"; the ball with a boost flame in Boostball's navy, blue and orange), given to Nico at Nico's own request (November 2026) and listed for both of Nico's accounts. Two tables near the top of `miles.js`: `AWARD_KINDS`, one line per kind of award (`n` the words, `tip` the tooltip, `icon` the mark as SVG in a 16 × 16 box, `disc` and `rim` the medal's colours, `band` and `ink` the ribbon's), and `AWARDS`, who holds which: one line per person, `'<account uid>': ['updraft']`, with a comment saying whose it is and why. **To give someone an award, add their line to `AWARDS`; to take it away, delete the line; a new kind of award is one more line in `AWARD_KINDS`.** Anyone with an account can hold one, whatever their tier and even with no node on the boards, and one person can hold several (they are drawn in the order listed). The uid is the key of the player's leaderboard node (the admin page shows it; never match on names, since anyone can type any name). Accounts the Leaderboards show as one row share the award, so it shows whichever of them is on screen: `awardsOf(result)` looks at the accounts in the result (`ids`: its own, and the entry it was merged into) and at the admin page's signed merges once `leaderboard.js` has read them, so list any one of the person's accounts, ideally the one the board shows; a node that only claims to be merged with a holder gets nothing. Nothing is read, written or stored for an award: the table is in the code, so it changes with a pull request. Where the row hasn't room the ribbon folds away to just its medal (`bra-tw-a`, before the tags lose their words), and also wherever its words would make a row that wraps taller (your own Online row on a laptop), so rows keep their height; the tooltip still names it. **One award is earned, not given: the Slipstream ace.** It belongs to whoever leads the most Slipstream tracks, and only while they lead 3 or more at once (`ACE_MIN`); leading a track means holding its fastest lap this record season, the driver the Leaderboards' every-track board names for it. Course records and clean laps don't count (a track has one of each for every race length, so one driver could reach three on a single track, and every record needs a lap time there anyway). If nobody leads three, nobody holds it; on a tie for most, everyone tied holds it, as with the #1 tags; accounts the boards show as one row count as one driver, and removed or skipped nodes never count. It changes hands as records fall: nobody edits a table. It is the same medal on a ribbon, in Slipstream's near-black and racing red with a chequered flag, labelled "Slipstream ace", tooltip "Leads 4 Slipstream tracks" with the live number, drawn last in the badge: after the tier pill, the place tags and any award that was given. Its kind is in `AWARD_KINDS` with `earned: true`, which also means a line for it in `AWARDS` is ignored. Like the #1 tags it is worked out in `leaderboard.js` (`ace()`), from the same read, and comes over in the same answer: `setTops` takes `ace` (the holders' uids, with their merged accounts) and `aceN` (the tracks they lead), kept in `lb:tops` with the rest, so it costs no read and no write, every page and tab shares it, and it updates whenever the boards are read (a new holder or number fires `backroom:miles` with `detail.tops`). `aceOf(result or uid)` is the number of tracks for a holder, else 0. An answer saved by a page from before it has no `ace`, so nobody holds it until the boards are next read; fewer than three tracks or anything malformed is dropped. **The second earned award is Top Cracker**, for the best Shell Crackers player: whoever has the most Shell Crackers kills of all time on the Leaderboards (the `kills` stat of Shell Crackers' row in `leaderboard.js`'s `GAMES`, the Kills (all time) board's own checked rows, so merged accounts count as one player with their kills added as the board adds them, and removed or skipped nodes never count), and only with 25 kills or more (`CRACK_MIN`), so it isn't handed out for a handful; on a tie for most, everyone tied holds it. It is worked out and carried exactly like the ace: `leaderboard.js`'s `cracker()` from the same read, `setTops` takes `crack` (the holders' uids, with their merged accounts) and `crackN` (their kills) in the same `lb:tops` answer (part of the change check, so a new holder or count redraws the badges), `crackerOf(result or uid)` gives the holder's kills, else 0, and an answer from a page from before it (no `crack`), under 25 or malformed means nobody holds it. Its kind is `shellcrackers` in `AWARD_KINDS` (`earned: true`): a cracked white egg with a yolk drop on Shell Crackers' ink-brown disc with a yolk rim, and "Top Cracker" in brown on an eggshell-cream ribbon with a thin brown edge (`edge`, an optional line round a pale ribbon, so it still shows on a cream row in light mode, while staying clear of the gold tags and the First Class pill); tooltip "Most Shell Crackers kills: 1,520" with the live number. It is drawn after the ace, so a badge goes tier, place tags, given awards, Slipstream ace, Top Cracker. When one player has place tags, a given award and the earned awards together, room is made in this order: both ribbons fold to their medals (`bra-tw-a`), then the tags lose their words and each medal after the first tucks under the one before (`bra-tw-s`; checked with a given award, both earned awards and two place tags in one phone row), then, only if under half the tier's name would still show (a phone row), the pill keeps just its icon and colours (`bra-tw-p`); nothing in a badge is taller than the pill, so rows never change height.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/avismiles.js`: Avis miles (Back Room page and phone page): players who booked a car through Avis claim bonus miles. Opened from the **Avis miles** button the Online panel shows by your miles (social.js), `BackRoomAvisMiles.open()` or a link with `#avis-miles`. Only for a Google sign-in whose account already has a leaderboard node. The form takes the Avis confirmation number, the pick-up date and an optional note, sealed to the admin key (`adminkey.js`'s `BackRoomSeal` of `{ k: 'ac', c, d, n, u: uid, t }`) and written to the player's own node as `ac` (the box) and `act` (when it was sent); `stats.js` carries both on in every write. One claim at a time: a new one replaces one still waiting. The admin page's Stats tab lists claims newer than the last one decided (**Avis miles claims**), opens them with the admin key (with the player's Google email from `ge`), and approves (adding `AVIS_MILES`, 1,000 by default, or another amount) or turns them down in its signed resets: `m` the Avis miles granted in all, `mt` the claim time last decided, `mr` 1 approved / 0 not (leaderboard.js `checkResets` keeps them; every signed write on the admin page carries them on). leaderboard.js hands `m` to miles.js as `p.am` (added up across merged accounts), which adds it to the person's miles everywhere. Nobody but the admin page can grant miles; the dialog shows the claim's state from `act`, `mt` and `mr`.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/keybinds.js`: rebindable controls shared by the keyboard Back Room games (2048, Backrooms 1v1–4v4, Boostball, Coil, Escapade, Gridpop, Hail Mary, Kartwheel, Melon Merge, Odd One Out, Quarry, Rumble Karts, Shell Crackers, Slipstream, Spire, Stackfall, Starhop, Tumble Trials, Turbo Trail, Updraft, Driftfall, Asteroid Run, Rampline, Tidewick, Flickerwood, Beatvault). Plain JavaScript, no dependencies. Each game loads it with `<script src="../keybinds.js"></script>`, lists its actions in `Keybinds.create({game, actions: [{id, label, keys: ['KeyW', 'ArrowUp']}]})` (keys are `e.code` names, so they're physical keys on any layout; mouse buttons are `Mouse0`/`Mouse1`/`Mouse2` in games that pass `mouse: true`), checks input with `KB.is(action, e.code)` and `KB.held(action, keys)` instead of comparing key codes, and opens the panel from a Controls button with `KB.open()`. Changed keys are saved only in the player's browser, under localStorage `keybinds:<game>` as `{v: 1, b: {action: [codes]}}` (only the actions that differ from the defaults; Reset to defaults removes the entry), and never go to Firebase. Esc is never rebindable. Elements with `data-kb="action"` show the current keys and update themselves. The panel takes each game's look from `--kb-*` CSS variables and the game's own button classes; the comment at the top of the file lists the options. To add it to a game, do the same, and keep text inputs (names, room codes, chat) returning before any key lookup. Touch controls don't go through key codes: each game's touch buttons feed its input directly (in Shell Crackers, Quarry and Backrooms they hold actions by id in `tAct`, which the game checks alongside `KB.held`), so they keep working whatever keys are bound, and the Change keys / Controls buttons are hidden in the touch layout (`body.touch`). **Spanish** (lang.js, when the page loads it): keybinds.js adds the panel's own words and common key and action names once (guarded by `if (window.BackRoomLang)`), and builds its messages and aria-labels with the action and key names in the page's language (a game's own dictionary supplies its action names); in English, or without lang.js, every string is as before.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/school.js`: Your school (Back Room page, the home page's copy of it and the phone page). A small box at the end of the page header where each player picks the school they go to: one button per school plus Not listed, as a radio group (click, or the arrow keys). The schools are Avenues (New York) and St. Andrew’s Scots School (`standrews`, Olivos, Buenos Aires, Argentina; a bilingual English–Spanish school, so lang.js's Spanish matters there); to add one, add a row to `SCHOOLS` (`id`, a short lowercase id that never changes, `name`, `place`). The script fills in every empty `[data-school]` element: the Back Room page has one at the end of its `<header>` (so the home page's `reveal()`, which copies the header, gets it too, and loads the script after `events.js`), and the phone page has one at the end of its header. Being in the header, layout.js never moves it. **Storage:** localStorage `school:v1`, the school's id (`avenues`) or `other` for Not listed; no key means nothing picked yet. It is in account-nudge.js's `KEYS`, so a Google account's save carries it to the player's other devices (visible to other signed-in browsers, like everything in that save). Nothing else is stored online. **Search and Add my school:** only schools marked `suggest: true` in `SCHOOLS` (Avenues) are shown before a search, plus the one the player picked; every other school is found by typing in the search box above the choices, which filters them as you type (name and place, accents ignored; Enter picks the first match). With no match, or from the dashed "Add my school" button, a small form asks for the school's name, its city or town and its country (only the general area; it says not to put a name or address) and sends one note through the suggestion box (`BackRoomFeedback.send` in feedback.js, the same sealed `feedback` documents, limits and admin Feedback tab, so no new storage or rules): "School request: <name> (<city>, <country>). Sent from Your school." The player's choice becomes Not listed, and localStorage `school:req` ({n, p, t}, also in account-nudge.js's KEYS) remembers what they asked for, so the box says "You asked us to add … We'll add it soon." To add a requested school, read it on the admin page's Feedback tab and add its row to `SCHOOLS`. Nothing uses the choice yet: other scripts can read it with `BackRoomSchool.get()` (the row, or null) and hear changes through the `backroom:school` event on `document` (`{id}`).
- `e63334287a3deaa3/news.js`: What's new (Back Room). A slim strip under the page header, above the Party panel, on the Back Room page and the phone page (one `<script defer>` in each page's `<head>`; the home page's `reveal()` also loads it). It shows the latest entry's kind (an emoji: a game controller for a new game, sparkles for an update, a wrench for a fix, a question mark for a teaser), version and title and when it went live (Today, Yesterday or the date), and, for a week after an entry with new games (`NEW_DAYS`), a "New games!" row with each new game as a colourful chip that opens it (in the home page's or phone page's game layer through `window.BackRoom.openGame` when there is one, otherwise as a plain link to the game's folder). Tapping the strip opens the changes as a card per release (kind badge, title, the new games' chips, bullets), grouped by day, five days at a time (`PAGE`), or fewer once those days hold ten cards (`CARDS`; a day is never split, so a busy day doesn't make a very long list), with Show older for the rest. Its colours come from the page's CSS variables, so it suits the light Back Room page and the dark phone page. A dot on the strip marks entries this browser hasn't opened yet (localStorage `news:seen`, the latest entry's date and version once the list has been opened); nothing is stored online. The changelog is the `NEWS` array at the top of the file, newest first.
