An entry can carry `link: {text, href}`, a link to another page of this site shown under its bullet points (the Marketplace entry uses it; only paths starting with one `/`). **When your pull request changes something players will notice, add an entry at the top of `NEWS`:** `{date: 'YYYY-MM-DD', version: '1.7', title: 'A few words', kind: 'game', games: ['Deepword'], items: ['One plain sentence per change', …]}`, with `date` the day it went live, `kind` `game` (a new game, shown as "New game 1.7"), `feature` (a new feature or big update, "Update 1.7") or `fix` ("Bug fix 1.7.1"; there is also `soon`, a teaser, described below), and `games` (optional) the names of brand-new games in the entry, as on their cards; each opens the Back Room folder named like the game in lowercase with dashes (`'Melon Merge'` → `melon-merge/`), or write `'Name:folder'` when the folder is named differently. Leave `games` out for updates and fixes. Two optional fields make entries easier to notice: `img`, a snapshot or a list of up to three, as a plain path inside the Back Room folder (use the game's phone thumbnail, `'m/thumbs/<folder>.jpg'`; anything else, such as `http:` or `../`, is ignored, so every picture comes from the site), and `tags`, up to four short keywords (`['Battle royale', 'Up to 30 players', 'Build']`, 28 characters each at most). The latest entry's first snapshot sits next to its headline on the strip, the open list shows each card's snapshots and tags, and the "New games!" chips get a tiny picture each by themselves (the game's `m/thumbs/<folder>.jpg` as a background, so a game without one keeps its plain colour). **Spotlight:** `SPOTLIGHT` (under `NEWS`) pins one game at the very top of the strip for `days` days from `from` (now: "New battle royale" Driftfall, 21 days from 9 November 2026): its snapshot, a kicker, its name, two or three keyword tags and a Play button that opens it like the chips do; while it shows, that game is left out of the "New games!" row. It sits under the page header on the Back Room page, the home page's copy and the phone page, so it's the first thing under the title, and on phones (560 px wide or less, or any screen under 500 px tall, so a phone sideways) it's one compact row: thumbnail, kicker and name, Play and ×. The × hides it in this browser (localStorage `news:spot` = the spotlight's `from|folder`); the record is removed once that spotlight has ended or a different one is set, so a new spotlight shows to everyone again. Set `SPOTLIGHT` to `null` for none. `window.BackRoomNews` also lists each entry's `img` and `tags` and the current `spotlight`. **The ad tile:** news.js also draws one house ad next to the strip, on the Back Room page, the home page's copy and the phone page. **To change the ads, edit `ADS` (under `SPOTLIGHT`), one entry per tile (at most two; today Gigantic and Soul’s_Type, Souleymane’s YouTube channel):** `on`, `name`, `line` (one short line in our own words), `button`, `href` (an `https://` address; anything else switches the tile off), `img` and `alt`, `art` (the drawing shown when there's no picture: `'candy'`, `'car'`, `'video'` or `'youtube'`; never a company's logo, except `'youtube'`: YouTube's own red play-button icon, drawn unaltered to its shape and colours, which YouTube's brand guidelines allow on a link to a YouTube channel, so it's only for a tile that links to one. Soul’s_Type uses it, on YouTube's near-black with a red Watch button), and four colours (`bg`, `ink`, `btn`, `btnInk`, as hex); `on: false` hides a tile, and with none on the strip is full width again, exactly as without them. Two tiles stack in the column beside the strip (sharing its height), sit side by side under it when the row is narrower than 960 px (stacked again under 520 px, so on an upright phone), and side by side beside it if the row is ever 1,180 px or wider. `BackRoomNews.ads` lists them (`BackRoomNews.ad` is still the first). Now: Gigantic (candy bars), linking to its store finder, `https://giganticcandy.com/pages/store-locator` (company, product and link checked on its own site on 17 Nov 2026). The tile is a single link marked "Sponsored", opened in a new tab with `rel="noopener sponsored"` and `referrerpolicy="origin"` (the advertiser sees the site's name, never the hidden page's address), with the accessible name "Ad for <name>: <line> <button> (opens in a new tab)". It is not an ad network: no script, tracker or cookie, and nothing is requested from the advertiser's site or any other until someone clicks. Where it sits: when the block is 960 px wide or more (800 px on a screen under 500 px tall, so a phone sideways on the Back Room page), beside the strip, 240 to 300 px wide (210 px sideways) and exactly the strip's height (it's a CSS size container, so it can never make the strip taller, and its contents rearrange to fit: picture over name and button when it's 84 px tall or more, one row when it isn't); otherwise, so on phones upright and always on the phone page (760 px at most), a 44 px banner under the strip, which moves the games down 52 px. While the list of changes is open the tile keeps its size at the top (beside) or sits under the list (banner). With the tile on, `#news` is the row holding the strip and the tile (`.nws-row` > `.nws-cols`), so the sign-in nudge still sits under both; with it off, `#news` is the strip itself, as before. **The picture:** `img` is empty for now, so the tile shows a small candy bar drawn in code in the ad's colours (`AD_ART`, our own drawing, not the company's artwork). To use a real picture, save one the advertiser has agreed to inside the Back Room folder (`ads/<name>.png`: wide rather than tall, about 600 × 200, twice its largest size on the page; a transparent `.png`, or `.jpg`/`.webp`; under about 60 KB) and set `img: 'ads/<name>.png'` and `alt` (what it shows); only plain paths inside the Back Room folder are used, and a missing file falls back to the drawing. `window.BackRoomNews.ad` is `{name, href}` or `null`. **The teaser:** an entry of kind `soon` hints that something is on its way without saying what. It shows as "Coming soon" with a "?" for its icon and no version number (it still has one, for the dot), as a dark pill on the strip and, in the open list, as a dark card in both themes with its picture large (up to 420 px wide, the card's width on a phone). The "?" and a soft glow over the picture's question mark fade slowly in and out (opacity only, so nothing moves; switched off with reduced motion). Now: "Something is coming" (1.29.16, 17 November 2026, so the next entry is 1.29.17), with `news-soon.jpg` (1120 × 700, twice the size the other news pictures are shown at, about 42 KB): a line-up of flat black shapes, four figures and two cars, under a glowing question mark on a dark stage, drawn in code for this entry (our own simple outlines, not copies of anything in the games). Its words tease and never tell: no names, no dates, nothing about what it is. It doesn't use the spotlight, which is still Driftfall's, and it leaves the strip, the spotlight and the ad tiles exactly where they were. It is the one exception to the rule below that nothing hidden gets an entry; take it out (and the picture) when the real news goes in. A new game or big feature bumps the minor number (1.6 → 1.7); a fix-only release adds or bumps a patch number (1.7 → 1.7.1 → 1.7.2). Several entries can share a day. Write the items for players, in your own words, and leave out anything private or admin-only. The text is shown as plain text, never as HTML. The history before the strip existed (1.0 on 13 October 2026 to 1.6) was written from the merged pull requests, one entry per day. Gridpop, 2048, Stackfall and Melon Merge went live on 23 October without an entry, so theirs ("Four puzzle games") was added afterwards with no version number, which is allowed for an entry like that (it shows as just "New game"). **The November 2026 catch-up:** by 17 November about fifty merged pull requests had gone out with no entry, so ten were written in one go from the merge history, each checked against the code: seven dated 17 November (Driftfall, the three new daily puzzles, Tumble Trials, Backrooms, the avatar editor, the Leaderboards and public parties, above the two Shell Crackers entries of that day, which have no version) and three dated 16 November (the graphics passes, a round-up of Escapade, Asteroid Run, Rampline and Edit layout, and one entry of small fixes). They are numbered 1.29.5 to 1.29.14 with patch numbers although most are features, because 1.30 was already given to the holiday entry dated 27 November (which stays hidden until then) and every entry since 14 November has counted up under 1.29; the next entry before 27 November is 1.29.15, and after the holidays the numbering goes on from 1.30. Two stale entries were taken out at the same time: Hivefix (1.23, retired a day later for Patchwork Tower) and the first Lunge Line entry (1.17, which described arenas the Lokop rework removed). Taking an entry out is safe for the dot (a browser whose saved key is gone falls back to counting the entries dated after it); changing the `date` or `version` of an entry that stays is not, so leave those two fields alone on old entries. Nothing that is switched off or hidden behind a `?name=1` switch gets an entry until it is on for everyone.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/gamesearch.js`: the game search box (Back Room). One `<script defer>` line on the Back Room page and the phone page, and the home page's `reveal()` loads it too (it copies the games but doesn't run their scripts). It puts a "Search games" box right under the Online panel, just before the row of game types (`.gs-nav`, or `.gnav` on the phone page), and keeps it there if Edit layout moves that row: layout.js moves the box and the row as one piece (`searchBox()` there), and this file's MutationObserver only puts the box back if something else moved it, giving up after four tries in three seconds rather than fight another script on every frame. (Before 19 Nov 2026 layout.js didn't know about the box: with a saved custom order the two scripts moved the Online panel and the box round each other on every frame, which re-inserted the Online panel about 50 times a second and threw away every click on a person there.) The observer ignores changes inside the box itself and only re-runs a search when games were added or removed. Typing filters the games as you go: every game card (`article.gc`), puzzle tile (`article.pz`) and phone tile (`.tile`) is matched on its name, short line, genre, chips, longer description, its section or row name and its folder name, every word in any order, ignoring accents and punctuation, so "racing", "shooter", "word" or "egg" all find something. Sections, rows and headings left empty are hidden, a line says how many games match (or none, with a Clear button), and clearing the box (×, Esc, deleting the text) puts the page back exactly as it was. "/" anywhere outside a text box jumps to it. Nothing is saved or sent. Its styles are added by the file itself from the page's own colours (`--panel`, `--line`, `--ink`, `--ink-2`, `--accent`), so it fits both pages in light and dark. A new game is found by itself as long as it has a card or tile.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/lang.js`: English / Español for the whole Back Room (see the comment at the top of the file for the full how-to). Loaded with one plain `<script>` in the `<head>` of the Back Room page and the phone page (before their other scripts), and by the home page's `reveal()`. **The setting:** localStorage `lang:v1` (`en` or `es`); with no key, the browser's language decides (any Spanish → `es`), so players in Argentina get Spanish without doing anything. It's in account-nudge.js's `KEYS`, `<html lang>` follows it, and other tabs follow a change. **The switch:** every empty `[data-lang-toggle]` gets a small EN | ES radio group (the Back Room page has one at the end of its header, so the home page's copy gets it; the phone page has one next to the theme switch); switching re-translates the page in place. **Adding Spanish:** a page or game calls `BackRoomLang.add({ English: Spanish })` with its own words, written in our own words in Latin American Spanish; `{name}` parts (`'Storm moves in {t}'`) are copied across. Every text node, and `placeholder`, `title`, `aria-label` and `alt`, whose whole trimmed text matches is shown in Spanish, including text added later (a MutationObserver), and the English is remembered so switching back restores it exactly. `data-noi18n` keeps an element and everything in it as it is (player names, chat, word-game answers, HUD text updated every frame). Text drawn on a canvas or built in code uses `BackRoomLang.t(en, vars)`; `num()` and `date()` format the local way (`es-AR` / `en-US`); `englishOnly(el)` tags content that is English by design (word games, trivia) with "En inglés" while Spanish is on. Other scripts can listen for `backroom:lang` on `document`. Nothing is sent anywhere. **What has Spanish so far:** the Back Room page, the phone page and the home page's copy of them get their words from `e63334287a3deaa3/es.js` (every card, the game types, puzzle tiles and notes; the phone page's rows, tips and frame bar), loaded only while Spanish is on: a small inline loader after the lang.js line loads it at once when the language is already Spanish, or on the first switch to Spanish, and the home page's `reveal()` does the same once lang.js has loaded. The What's new entries' titles, tags and bullet points are in `e63334287a3deaa3/news-es.js`, which news.js loads the same way (when you add an entry, add its lines there too; a line with no Spanish shows in English). Each shared script adds its own words (news.js, party.js, social.js, playingnow.js, feedback.js, leaderboard.js, miles.js, avatar.js, seasons.js, layout.js, gamesearch.js, school.js, events.js, account-nudge.js), as does the Daily Puzzles hub; sentences with a player's name in them are built with `t()` and `{n}` so the word order follows the language, and what people type is never translated. Game names, the status tiers (Economy, Business Class, First Class, Private Jet, Captain), award names and track names stay in English; the word puzzles and Puffer Party get an "En inglés" tag on the Back Room page, the phone page and the Daily Puzzles hub. cosmetics.js (switched off for now) and its catalogue aren't translated yet.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/layout.js`: Edit layout (Back Room). Each player can put the page's big blocks in their own order, and choose which games have big cards. It's loaded with one `<script defer>` in the `<head>` of the Back Room page and the phone page, and the home page's `reveal()` loads it too. A small "Edit layout" button sits at the top of the games: on the Back Room page it's in the Games section's top padding, and on the phone page it's in a slim row above the first heading. It opens an editor where every movable block is a bar (its icon or thumbnail, name and a short line) with a drag handle and ↑/↓ buttons. With a mouse you can drag anywhere on a bar. On touch you drag by the handle, so the list still scrolls, and it scrolls by itself near the edges. The arrow keys also work on the handle, and a line shows where the bar will land. The blocks themselves are hidden while editing (`html.lay-editing`). **Done** saves the order and redraws the page. **Reset** puts the default order back and saves that. The editor has two tabs, **Order** (the bars) and **Game sizes**. Game sizes shows each section's games as tiles (thumbnail, name and a Big/Small label) in the section's own number of columns at the current screen width, so it's a live preview of how the rows will come out. Tapping a tile switches it between big and small (switching a game back to the size the site gives it forgets the choice), and each section has **All small**, **All big** and **Default** (back to the site's sizes); Reset on this tab puts every game back. On the Back Room page (and the home page's copy) big means `wide` + `pw` (two columns on computers, a whole row on phones) and small means neither; a game the player hasn't chosen keeps the classes the HTML gives it, so a new game still gets the size Arjun gives it. On the phone page big is `.lay-big` (a whole row upright, two of three columns sideways with the picture on the left), and every tile is small by default. The puzzle tiles aren't sized. A game's key is its folder, read from the card's links (or the tile's `thumbs/<folder>.jpg`), so every card, including new ones, is picked up by itself, and a game on both pages shares one entry. Gaps: a big card that doesn't fit in what's left of a row starts the next row and leaves a gap, so in a section where the player has chosen any size, the card before a gap stretches to fill the row (`.lay-fill`, an inline `grid-column: span N`, plus `.lay-fw` for the wide card's look). The player's choice isn't changed; the editor shows which card stretches with a one-line note under the section, and it's worked out again when the number of columns changes. Sections the player hasn't touched are left exactly as they are. Card widths come from the page's own CSS (each card's computed `grid-column`), so the preview and gap handling follow `games.css` and the phone page at every width. The blocks on the Back Room page (and the home page's copy) are `party`, `online`, `daily` (only if the page has a Daily Puzzles card, `.gc-daily`; it hasn't since the puzzles became tiles, and a saved order that names it still loads), one per genre section (`puzzles`, `parkour`, `action`, `multi`, `solo`, taken from each `gs-<name>` class, so a new section is included by itself) and `leaderboards`. On the phone page they are `party`, `online`, every GROUPS row (`m-` + the row's name in lowercase with dashes) and `leaderboards`. Each run of rows of one mode gets that mode's heading, with the count for that run. The order is kept per browser in localStorage `layout:v1` = `{v: 1, order: [ids], size: {<game folder>: 'big'|'small'}}`, and it is also in account-nudge.js's synced keys, so the sizes sync with it. `size` is optional (older records without it still work, so no version bump) and only names the games the player chose; with only sizes saved, `order` is `[]`. Saving from one page keeps the sizes of games that are only on the other page. Both pages share one list: each page uses the ids it has, and when it saves, it keeps the other page's ids next to the block they followed. A block the list doesn't name (a new section, or a panel that hadn't loaded yet when the order was saved) goes to its default place. With no saved order (or the default order) and no sizes, the page isn't touched apart from the button, and the key is removed. How a different order is applied: on the Back Room page, the Party, Online and Leaderboards panels move inside `section.br-games` (`.lay-on`), because the genre sections need its styles. The blocks go there in order, with the "Want more games?" note after the last games block. If a page has a Daily Puzzles card and it isn't directly above Puzzles, it gets its own `<section class="gs gs-puzzles lay-daily">`, and Puzzles' count drops "+ daily". On the phone page, the rows and panels go into `.wrap` before `#lists`. party.js, social.js and leaderboard.js add their panels after layout.js runs, and social.js moves Online back under Party once. A MutationObserver on the wrap and the Games section (or `#lists`) puts any block that lands out of place back in order before the next paint. It only moves elements that are out of place, so it doesn't loop with news.js's own observer. **The row of game types** (`.gs-nav` on the Back Room page, `.gnav` on the phone page) isn't a block of its own in the editor: it belongs to the games. In the default order it stays where the page has it, at the top of the games, which is already under Online. In a player's own order it goes right above the first games block that comes after the Online panel, so it is never above Online or Party while there are games below them (before, it stayed at the top of the page, above Party and Online). If the player has put every games block above Online, or the page has no Online panel, it goes right above the first games block. Nothing about the saved record changed, so saved orders load as before and only the row of game types moves. The home page's `reveal()` copy is the default HTML, so layout.js rearranges it there in the same way. Nothing is stored online by this file.
- In-game leaderboards: every game page that loads stats.js gets a "Leaderboard" button that opens that game's boards in a panel over the page (the same boards, checks and data as the Leaderboards section of the Back Room page, which is unchanged). stats.js adds the button (`boardButton`) and, the first time it is pressed, loads `/namefilter.js` if the page hasn't and `leaderboard.js` with `data-game="<folder>"`: its game mode builds the usual section inside a dialog (`.lbd-modal`, with its own colours and type since game pages have none of the hub's CSS variables), fixed to that game, signed in through `BackRoomStats.conn()` (the connection stats.js already publishes with) instead of party.js, read when the panel opens, with the view remembered per game (`lb:view:<folder>`). Close, Escape or a press outside closes it; keys and presses inside stay inside. Where the button goes, so it never sits over play: in a game with a `#menu`, inside that menu as its last item, and while that menu is showing but its button is scrolled out of view (most menus are taller than the screen), a copy (`#brlb-pin`) is pinned bottom right and goes as soon as the real one scrolls into view, the menu is hidden or faded out, or the panel opens; on a page with no `#menu` and no canvas (the puzzles), at the end of the page; on a page with a canvas and no `#menu`, a small round button fixed bottom left that hides while the pointer is locked and for 8 s after any key or press. A game's stats.js tag can override it: `data-board="off"`, a corner (`tl`, `tr`, `bl`, `br`; 2048 uses `bl`), or `data-board-in="<css selector>"` to put it inside a chosen element. A new game with a `#menu` needs nothing. The button is added inside frames too (the home page, phone page and party layer all open games in an iframe, which is how most people play); only the hidden stats sidecar frame has no stats.js of its own.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/elo.js`: ranked ratings (Elo), one per game, for the online games that end with a winner: Boostball, Rumble Karts, Backrooms 1v1–4v4, Driftfall, Slipstream, Turbo Trail, Tumble Trials, Chromaflage, Odd One Out, Puffer Party, Deepword's Brawl, Lokop, Sky Duel and Blackwade. Shell Crackers keeps its own older rating (`shellcr-rank`, `rooms/sc-ratings`) and doesn't load this file; the maths here is the same as its. Each game loads it next to stats.js (`<script src="/e63334287a3deaa3/elo.js" data-game="<folder>" defer></script>`) and calls `BackRoomElo.report({ id, won, opp: [{ u: uid, s }, { bot: 1, s }] })` once where its online match or round ends (search each game for `BackRoomElo`): `opp` is everyone you played against, not teammates, and `s` is your result against that one opponent (1 beat them or your team won, 0 lost, 0.5 drew), so team games give every opponent the same `s`, races and free-for-alls go by finishing place, Driftfall counts everyone already out as beaten and everyone still alive as a loss, Tumble Trials rates once per show (when you go out or the final ends), and in Chromaflage a seeker beats each hider that was found while a hider beats the seekers by staying hidden. Hush Pools is co-op, so it has no opponents in that sense: when an online party escapes, each player is compared with each teammate by what they did for the party (`runScore`: 10 a rescue, 4 a valve opened or the keypad solved, minus 6 for each time they went down), and the one with the higher score "beats" the other (equal is a draw). The change is the usual Elo one (K 36 for the first 10 matches, then 24), averaged over the opponents and multiplied by the number of real opponents (×1 for one, 25% more for each extra, at most ×3); bots count as rated 1000, and a match with no real opponent isn't rated, so practice and bot-only rooms never move a rating. Everyone starts at 1000 and stays in 100–3000. `id` stops a match being rated twice (results screens can be drawn more than once). Storage: this browser's `elo:<folder>` (`{ r, g, wn, pk, uid }`) and the Realtime Database at `rooms/elo-<folder>/players/<uid>` (`n, c, x, z, h, ts` as the shared rules need, plus `r, g, wn, pk`), written by the player's own browser; on load the account's saved rating wins, and opponents' ratings are read from their nodes when a match is reported. So, like every Back Room stat, it is self-reported and not cheat-proof. It never signs in or makes a Firebase app itself (it uses the game's), and every call swallows its own errors, so it can't stop a game. After a rated match a small pill at the top shows the new rating and the change. stats.js reads `elo:<folder>` into the game's `elo` stat (the list of folders is next to `READ`), and leaderboard.js has an Elo board first for each of these games (for Slipstream, after its lap and record boards); as with Shell Crackers, the board keeps the best value it has seen, so it shows a player's highest rating. To rate another game: add the script tag and one `report` call, add its folder to the list in stats.js, and add the Elo row to its entry in leaderboard.js.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/netsmooth.js`: smooth movement for other players in the online games. Updates over school Wi-Fi and Firebase arrive late and in bursts, so a game that moves someone to their newest update makes them lurch and stall, and one that shows them a fixed time in the past freezes them whenever an update is later than that. Each remote player gets a `NetSmooth.lag()`, which measures how late their updates really arrive and shows them just far enough in the past that the next one is almost always already here (about a tenth of a second on good Wi-Fi, more on bad, never more than 0.45 s), easing that delay so the timeline never jumps; `NetSmooth.sample()` draws them between the two updates around that moment and carries them on for up to 0.3 s if an update is late. Used by Shell Crackers, Backrooms 1v1–4v4, Hail Mary, Coil, Quarry, Chromaflage, Odd One Out, Spire, Turbo Trail, Escapade, Updraft, Driftfall, Blockstorm, Lanternhold (teammates' pointers), Sky Duel and Putterby. Kartwheel, Slipstream, Boostball, Rumble Karts and Tumble Trials keep their own approach: cars, karts and racers are projected forward to the present from their speed, which matters more there than perfectly even motion. Settings were tuned with a simulation of good, school and bad Wi-Fi: compared with chasing the newest update, other players stall about a fifth as often and almost never lurch, for roughly a tenth of a second more delay.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/autores.js`: automatic resolution for the 3D games, so they stay smooth on school laptops. Every three.js game except Quarry (which has its own version) loads it right after `three.min.js`. It watches the frame rate, and when frames drop below about 45 a second it makes every three.js renderer on the page draw at a lower resolution, a tenth at a time, down to half; when frames stay smooth it steps back up (more slowly towards a resolution that was too slow before, so it doesn't keep flipping). It multiplies whatever `setPixelRatio` the game asks for, so a game's own graphics setting still counts. The scale each game settles on is remembered in the browser (`localStorage` key `autores:<page path>`), so the next visit starts there. No game code calls it; it wraps `THREE.WebGLRenderer`. `AutoRes.scale` shows the current scale and `AutoRes.off()` turns it off for a visit.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/account-nudge.js`: the sign-in nudge and account save (Back Room). Loaded on the Back Room page and the phone page (one `<script defer>` in each page's `<head>`; the home page's `reveal()` also loads it), never inside a game. **The nudge:** when the page opens and the site's Google sign-in (`account/account.js`, `window.SiteAccount`) reports nobody signed in, a small card under the What's new strip and above the Party panel says "Sign in with Google to save your skins, records and settings across devices", with Sign in (the normal Google popup, `SiteAccount.signIn()`) and Not now, which hides it for 7 days (localStorage `sv:nudge`, the time it was dismissed). It never signs anyone in by itself, isn't shown while signed in, and shows nothing on a page without the sign-in: the phone page opened on its own has none (no top bar, so `account.js` isn't there), but inside the home page's phone frame it uses the home page's sign-in. **The prompt:** once per visit (sessionStorage `sv:prompt`, shared by the page and its phone frame), the first time a hub page (the Back Room page, the home page after its reveal, or the phone page inside the home page's frame) finds nobody signed in with Google, a centred card over the page asks them to "Sign in with Google to save your account, skins and records", with **Sign in with Google** (the same `SiteAccount.signIn()` popup) and **Not now**. It never blocks the page: Not now, Esc, the × button or a click/tap outside close it at once, nothing is signed in automatically, and afterwards the small card is there as before (with its own 7-day quiet period). It waits while a game layer is open over the page (an invite link) and closes itself if a game opens. Nothing is shown while signed in, or on a page without `SiteAccount` (the phone page opened on its own). The test hook `__acctNudgeT.modal` says whether it's open. **The save:** while someone is signed in with Google, a whitelist of the games' localStorage keys is mirrored to the Firebase Realtime Database (same project and rules as the games) at `rooms/sv-saves/players/<uid>` (prefix `sv`, not an invite prefix), so they come back on another device after signing in there. The whitelist (`KEYS` and `PREFIXES` in the file): names (`party:name`, `ol:name` and each game's name key), the Online panel's block and mute lists (`ol:block`, `ol:mute`), its Let people join my games setting (`ol:join`) the party Public / Private choice (`party:pub`) and the colour of the player's name tile (`nametile:v1`, see `avatar.js`), skins, colours and looks (Coil skin; Shell Crackers colour and loadout; Boostball body and accent; Chromaflage colour, shape, size, roughness, recent colours and paint; Spire, Updraft and Odd One Out colour; Tumble Trials look; Showdown avatar; Rumble Karts colour), bests and records (`coil:best`, `shellcr-rank`, `spire:profile`, `updraft:best`, `tumble-stats`, `tumble-best-*`, `sd:pstats`, `trivium.streak`, `trivium.best.*`, `trivium.daily.*`, `g48:best`, `gridpop:best`, `stackfall:best:*`, `melon-merge:best`), settings (difficulty, graphics, sensitivity, invert, volume, music, mute, view, zoom, camera, laps, track, practice map, host settings, Showdown prefs, Trivium quick-play options, 2048 options, Stackfall sand colours, Melon Merge faces, Gridpop help seen) and keybinds (`keybinds:*`). Left out on purpose: room codes, in-progress game saves (`g48:game`, `gridpop:save`, `updraft:run`), stats.js's `lbs:*` keys (it syncs its own record to `rooms/lb-all`), the Online panel's key pair (IndexedDB) and seen lists, admin codes, feedback drafts, analytics ids, and this script's own `sv:*` keys. **Everything in the node can be read by anyone signed in** (the shared rules let any signed-in browser read any room), so nothing private goes in: every key in the whitelist is already visible to other players in-game or on the leaderboards. Only the owner can write their node. Node format: `n` (name, 14 chars), `c` (colour from the uid), `x`/`z`/`h` (0, required by the rules), `ts`, `v` (1), `d` (`{key: value}`, the raw localStorage strings; `.`, `#`, `$`, `/`, `[`, `]` and `%` in a key are written as `%XX`, because Firebase keys can't hold them) and `t` (`{key: ms}`, when each key last changed; a key in `t` but not in `d` was deleted then). Values over 64 KB are skipped and the node is kept under about 200 KB (largest keys dropped first). Merging: on sign-in, and on load when already signed in, the node is read once and for each key the newer copy wins (ties keep the local one); a key this browser changed while the script wasn't running (a game opened on its own) is noticed by a hash of each value kept at the last sync (localStorage `sv:meta`, `{u, t, h}`) and stamped with the current time; a key this browser has never synced for this account has no time, so the account's copy wins over it (a new device's random defaults can't wipe the account's skins and records), and a key the account lacks is uploaded. Afterwards changes are watched (`storage` events from other tabs and from games running in this page's frames, a poll every 15 s, and pagehide) and written a few seconds later, only when something changed. Nodes are read defensively (unknown keys, non-strings, oversized values and bad times are ignored). Firebase comes from party.js (`window.Party.boot()`), and it only writes when that account is the Google account `SiteAccount` shows; it never signs anyone in and doesn't change how the games pick their account. **The leaderboard link:** while the page is signed in anonymously it keeps that anonymous user (party.js's), and when a Google sign-in arrives on the page it loads `stats.js` with `data-lib` and calls `BackRoomStatsLib.link()`, which marks the anonymous Leaderboards entry as merged into the Google one and has the Google entry take its hours and bests (README: stats.js). Nothing happens for an account already signed in when the page opened, or when the anonymous account has no entry. A small "Saved to your account · just now" line sits in the card's place while signed in, and a "Restored your skins and records from your account" toast shows once after a restore that changed something; a restore that changed something also fires `backroom:avatar`, so a restored name tile colour shows and is published straight away rather than after a reload. **Limitation:** a game reads its saves when it opens, so a value restored from the account appears the next time that game is opened (the hub pages restore on load, normally long before a game is tapped), and two devices open at the same time only see each other's changes on their next load or sign-in. A test hook (`window.__acctNudgeT`) exists only when `navigator.webdriver` is set.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/seasons.js`: seasonal looks. Loaded by the Back Room page, the phone page and the Daily Puzzles page (not by games), and by the home page's `reveal()` only once the Back Room is open there, so the home page itself never gets the seasonal look. The viewer's own date decides the season: fall from September 22 to November 26 warms the whole page and draws a harvest scene behind it (below), with leaves and the odd acorn drifting down; winter from November 27 to the end of February tints it light blue, makes the accent blue (`ACCENT.winter`, on every page including the phone page), drops snow, slowly builds a thin snow line along the bottom edge over the first minute, and draws snowy ground and a snowman behind the page. **The holidays** are the first part of winter, November 27 (the day after Thanksgiving) to January 6 (`HOLIDAY_TO`, `holidayFor`): then the winter scene also has lights and hanging gingerbread across the top of the margins, a Christmas tree with presents and a diya with a little rangoli in the bottom left, and a menorah beside the snowman in the bottom right (`drawWinterScene`; on phones a small copy of them all goes in the footer, as in fall), and on the pages that list the games (anything with `.br-games` or `#lists`) seasons.js loads `holiday.js` (next entry). Nothing of winter or the holidays shows before November 27 on the viewer's clock, and a tab left open is checked every second, so the season changes at midnight and the holidays end at midnight after January 6 without a reload (winter itself carries on). The rest of the year it does nothing (`FALL_FROM`/`FALL_TO` at the top of the file). **Sunset Week** (`SUNSET_WEEK`, one line at the top of the file, with the year: November 19 to 23, 2026) swaps fall for the sunset look on those days only, so the Thanksgiving scene comes back on November 24: a pink and violet tint (deep indigo in dark mode; `SUNSETV`, `TINT.sunset`), a hot pink accent on light pages and teal on dark ones (`ACCENT.sunset`), and a scene drawn in code in the same two canvases (`layoutSunset`, `drawSunsetScene`, `drawSunsetFx`): a sunset glow low on the page, a big sun with slices cut from its lower half sinking into a strip of sea with a teal horizon and broken reflections, a far skyline of low blocks at the sides, and tall palm trees in silhouette in the margins whose crowns sway (`crownSprite`, `drawTrunk`), with a few gulls, a small plane and sparkles on the water kept to the margins; on phones the palms in the corners are drawn faint so text over them stays readable, and a small copy of the scene goes in the footer. The footer button shows 🌴 then, and `?season=sunset` shows it on any day. To remove it, make `SUNSET_WEEK` `[]`; to move or extend it, change its two dates. The tint is written as `html:root:root{…}` so it outranks the dark-mode rules of the theme switch (see `theme.js`), and is redone when the theme changes. The tint only overrides each page's colour variables (`--ground`, `--panel-2`, `--line`, `--panel` from site.css, `--bg` on the phone page, `--night` on the Daily page), mixed a little towards a warm or cool colour; text colours are left alone. In fall each variable has its own colour to move towards (`FALL`), so cards come out a warm cream in light mode and a deep chestnut in dark mode that belongs with the background; with one shared tint, dark-mode cards stayed a grey-green on brown. The site's green `--accent` (Play buttons in Cars, the Send button, small labels and links, the What's new glow) becomes harvest gold in fall (`ACCENT`: `#9C6300` on light pages, `#F3B94A` on dark, with matching `--accent-ink`); only a green accent is replaced, so the phone page's yellow one is left alone. **The fall scene** is drawn in code (no image files): a low sun glow in the top right, a field along the bottom edge, a pumpkin patch with wheat in the bottom left margin, a Thanksgiving turkey in a pilgrim hat in the bottom right margin (three sprites, so its tail sways and it bobs, blinks and pecks twice every nine seconds; `turkeySprites`, `drawTurkey`) and a branch with a few leaves in the top left. The parts that never move are on a second fixed canvas under the first, `#season-bg`, redrawn only when the window changes size (`layoutScene`, `drawScene`), so each frame still only draws a few dozen sprites (well under a tenth of a millisecond in `bench`). The pumpkins and the turkey stand in the empty margins either side of the page's column (`.wrap`); where the margins are under 120 px (phones, narrow windows) they aren't drawn there, and a small still copy of the scene is put at the top of the page's footer instead (`footScene`, a `<canvas class="season-foot">`). The shapes are drawn on one fixed canvas, `#season-fx`, inserted first in `<body>` at z-index -1 with `body{isolation:isolate}`, so it sits above the page background and under everything else; it takes no space, ignores the pointer, pauses when the tab is hidden or a game layer covers the page, runs at 30 fps on phones, and with prefers-reduced-motion shows the tint, the scene and a few still shapes only (the turkey stands still). A small 🍂 / 🌴 / ❄ button in the footer turns it off or on for that browser (localStorage `season:off`); during the holidays its label says it turns off the holiday look as well as the snow. For holiday.js it also keeps two things: `used`, whether the visitor has already clicked, typed, scrolled or tapped on the page (asked of the browser, `navigator.userActivation`, for the time before this file ran when it was loaded with the page, and listened for from then on), passed to `BackRoomHoliday.start(used)` so the cabin never lands on a page that is being used (the same is passed when the page had already been on screen for 4 seconds, `LATE`, before holiday.js arrived on a very slow connection); and `BackRoomSeasons.hold(true|false)`, which stops the snow while the cabin covers the page and starts it again as the cabin leaves. For testing: `?season=fall|sunset|holiday|winter|none` in the address (`holiday` is winter with the holidays, `winter` without), or `BackRoomSeasons.set('winter')` in the console (`set()` alone goes back to the date); `BackRoomSeasons.seasonFor(date)` is the date rule and `BackRoomSeasons.bench(n)` returns milliseconds per frame.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/holiday.js`: the holidays (Back Room), loaded only by seasons.js, only from November 27 to January 6, and only on the Back Room page, its copy on the home page and the phone page. Two things. **The cabin**: once per browser tab (sessionStorage `holiday:seen`) the page opens on a log cabin at night, drawn in code as pixel art on one `<canvas>` (`#hol-cabin`, `drawCabin`): log walls, a window with snow falling outside, a tree with presents, a stone fireplace with stockings and a menorah and a diya on the mantel, lights under the beam, and in the middle an old TV showing "THE BACK ROOM / PRESS START". Clicking the TV (or Enter or Space) zooms into its screen, the picture whites out and the page comes up out of the white; Skip or Esc goes straight in with a quick fade. The scene is laid out around the TV at a whole number of the screen's own pixels per art pixel (`layoutCabin`; device pixels, not CSS pixels, so every art pixel is the same size on any display and a phone held sideways gets a bigger room), so on a phone you see the TV, the wall and the rug and on a wide screen the whole room. The cabin opens the page; it never drops onto one already in use: if the visitor has clicked, typed or scrolled before holiday.js arrived, or the page has been on screen for over 4 seconds by then (slow Wi-Fi; `start(busy)` from seasons.js), is in a text box, or has a game open over the page (an invite link, or a game still open when November 27 arrives), there is no cabin that time, and since `holiday:seen` isn't set it opens on the next load of that tab; the Cabin button always works. While it is up the keyboard belongs to it (`ckey`): focus moves to the cabin, Tab goes between the TV and Skip only, Esc skips, and keys don't reach the page behind, so Esc doesn't also close a pop-up the page opened underneath (the sign-in box); when it closes, focus goes back to where it was. The snow behind it is held still (`BackRoomSeasons.hold`) and the scan lines are hidden until it leaves, and it is drawn about 14 times a second. **The retro TV look** (the Retro TV switch at the top right of the page, `#hol-bar`, on by default; localStorage `holiday:tv` = `0` when off): a `tv-on` class on `<html>` swaps the fonts for pixel ones (Pixelify Sans for reading, Press Start 2P for headings, both from Google Fonts like the site's other fonts, and only fetched once the look is on), squares every corner, gives cards hard shadows, shows the game pictures in big pixels (each `<img>` is swapped for a small copy of itself, its address kept in `data-hol-src` and put back when the look is switched off; the copies are made one picture at a time with a pause between, `picNext`, because shrinking thirty at once froze a slow laptop for a second or two), lays faint scan lines with a slow rolling band over the page (`#hol-crt`, a fixed layer that ignores the pointer, z-index 900, under the home page's game layer) and asks seasons.js to draw its snow and decorations in big pixels (`BackRoomSeasons.pixel`). Switched off, the page is the plain winter look. The Cabin button beside the switch shows the cabin again. The ❄ button in the footer (seasons.js) turns all of it off; with prefers-reduced-motion the cabin is a still picture, there is no zoom and no rolling band. Testing: `?season=holiday` (add `&nocabin=1` to skip the cabin, `&cabin=1` to see it again), `BackRoomHoliday.cabin()`, `BackRoomHoliday.tv(true|false)`. To end the holidays on another day change `HOLIDAY_TO` in seasons.js; the What's new entry for it is dated November 27, and news.js keeps an entry dated in the future hidden until that day.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/events.js`: short in-game events, loaded by the games that take part (before their own script, like `autores.js`). It answers two questions so the games agree: is the event running (by the viewer's own clock), and has this browser switched it off. **Turkey Week** runs November 15 to 26 (Thanksgiving Day), 2026 (`TURKEY_FROM`, `TURKEY_TO` at the top of the file; change those to move or repeat it). Taking part: Shell Crackers, Rumble Karts, Melon Merge, Coil, Boostball, Odd One Out, Driftfall, Backrooms, Tumble Trials, Chromaflage, Slipstream and Trivium; each game's own entry says what it draws. Hunch, Quartets, Crosslet and Poopdle don't load the file: they only have their own puzzle for November 26. `BackRoomEvents.turkey.live()` / `.on()` / `.set(true|false)`; `.hat(THREE)` is a pilgrim hat geometry with vertex colours shared by the 3D games. **There is one on/off switch for all the games, and it is outside them**: an empty `<button type="button" data-turkey-switch hidden>` at the end of the Back Room page's `<header>` (which the home page's `reveal()` copies, loading `events.js` after it) and in the phone page's header (in a `.tkrow` line of its own, so the panels other scripts put under the header come after it). `events.js` fills in every such button it finds when it loads (`.wire(el)` does the same for one added later): a small pill with a slider, "Turkey Week is on" or "off", and the last day; it is hidden outside the event, a click flips the setting, and it follows a change made in another tab. Its styles are added by `events.js` (`TK_CSS`) from the page's own colour variables, so it suits the light Back Room page and the dark phone page. The setting is localStorage `event:turkey:off`, shared by every game. The games have no Turkey Week button of their own (they did at first): each reads `.on()` once, when it loads, so a game that is already open keeps the look it started with until it is opened again, never half of each (a game brought back by the browser's Back or Forward button reloads if the setting changed meanwhile). A new game taking part should do the same: read it once into a constant and add no button. Outside those dates the games look exactly as before and the switch is hidden. Nothing is stored online or sent to other players. Testing: `?event=turkey` in the address of a game or of a page with the switch runs it on any day, `?event=none` hides it.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/feedback.js`: the suggestion box (Back Room). A small heading, "Add any suggestions or report any errors", over one empty text box that says "Suggestion here..." at the very top of the Back Room page and the phone page (one `<script defer>` in each page's `<head>`; the home page's `reveal()` also loads it next to `party.js`, because the home page doesn't run the Back Room page's `<head>`). Any element with `data-feedback-open` (the Tell us buttons under the games on the Back Room and phone pages) scrolls to the box and puts the cursor in it, as does `window.BackRoomFeedback.open()`. A value on the attribute (the Puzzles tab's `data-feedback-open="Puzzles: "`) puts that text in the box first if it's empty, so the note says what it's about; it's saved as a draft only once they type, a note that is only that text isn't sent, and the note is stored exactly like any other. Nothing has to be picked first: the box grows and a Send button appears once it has the cursor or some text in it (up to 1,000 characters; a counter shows for the last 200). It used to be a bar that opened into a form asking for a kind (Idea or change, Bug, Something else), a game, the message and an optional name and way to reply; notes from the box are stored the same way with `game` and `kind` both `other` and the name and reply left empty, so the admin page and the rules need no change. Storage: Firestore `feedback` (same `rumblekarts` project as the games), one document per note: `uid` (the sender's Firebase account; the rules require it to match the account that wrote it), `ts` (the server's time), `game` (a Back Room game folder name or `other`), `kind` (`idea`, `bug` or `other`) and `box`, which holds the message, name, reply field and page sealed to the admin key (see `adminkey.js` below), so only someone with both admin codes can read them; `game` and `kind` stay plain so the admin page can filter. Notes sent before sealing was added are plain (`msg`, `game`, `kind`, `name`, `contact`, `page`, `uid`, `ts`) and still show on the admin page, marked "Not sealed". It writes through the Firestore REST API with the browser's Firebase account: a Google account from `account.js` if there is one, otherwise the same anonymous account the games use (it only signs in, and loads `adminkey.js`, when someone presses Send). **Nothing is saved until Arjun adds the rules below**; until then Firestore refuses the write and the box says "Feedback isn't switched on yet" and keeps the message. In the browser it allows one note a minute and five a day (localStorage `fbk:sent`) and keeps the unsent message, name and reply field (`fbk:draft`, `fbk:name`, `fbk:contact`); those are manners, not security, so the rules check every field. There's no email fallback, because no contact address is published on the site. The game list is `GAMES` at the top of `feedback.js`: add new Back Room games there (the rules accept any lowercase folder-style name, so they don't need changing; the admin page's stats also take game names from this list). The notes are read and ticked off on the admin page's Feedback tab (the old `feedback-admin/` inbox has been removed).
- `e63334287a3deaa3/door.js`: the way into the admin page, on the Back Room page (a `<script>` tag) and after the home page's `reveal()` (which loads it next to `feedback.js`). It's three steps, in order: (1) type the door word into the Party panel's "Party code" box and don't press Join (party.js only reads that box when Join or Enter is pressed, so typing does nothing there; door.js stops Enter from trying to join while the word is in the box, and remembers for two minutes that it was typed, in case the box gets emptied); (2) click or tap the "You found it" line above the title (anywhere on it; the "Y" is a `<span data-door>` with extra invisible padding) three times within 2.5 seconds. Taps are counted on `pointerup` as well as `click`, because iPhone and iPad Safari don't send clicks for taps on plain text. If the word is in the box, or was typed there in the last two minutes, the box is cleared and a number keypad opens (big buttons; on a computer the number keys, Backspace, Enter and Escape work too); without it, nothing visible happens. (3) Enter the admin number. The keypad checks it like the home page checks the Back Room code: the first 16 hex characters of SHA-256 of `ascoding11-admin:` + the number name a folder, and the browser goes there only if the folder exists; a wrong number shakes the keypad and clears it (three wrong numbers close it). The number is handed to the admin page in sessionStorage (`bra:n`), which the page reads and deletes at once. The word is checked by its digest too (SHA-256 of `ascoding11-door:` + the trimmed, lowercased box value, first 16 hex characters, `WORD` in the file), so neither the word nor the number is in the repo: ask Arjun for the door word, the admin number and the admin password. The door only works while you're not in a party (the Party code box only shows then). The phone page has the Party panel but no "You found it" line, so the door isn't there; open the admin page directly instead (it then asks for the number and the password).
- `e63334287a3deaa3/adminkey.js`: the admin key. `window.BackRoomAdminKey = {pub, wrapped, salt, iv}`: `pub` is an ECDH P-256 public key (JWK), and `wrapped` is its private key (PKCS#8) encrypted with AES-GCM (`iv`) under a key from PBKDF2-SHA256 of `<admin number>:<admin password>` with the random `salt` and 600,000 iterations. The private key itself is not in the repo in any other form. `window.BackRoomSeal(obj)` seals an object to `pub` the same way `social.js` seals private messages (a fresh ECDH key pair per entry, AES-GCM), giving `1.<ephemeral public key>.<iv>.<ciphertext>` in base64. `social.js` and `feedback.js` load the file the first time they seal something. To change the codes, generate a new key pair and wrapped key with a small script (Node's or the browser's Web Crypto) and replace the object; anything sealed before can then only be opened with the old file and the old codes.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/d06440218d1813a3/`: the admin page. The folder name is the digest of the admin number (see `door.js`); it isn't linked from anywhere, isn't in the sitemap, and has the Back Room's `noindex,nofollow,noarchive` robots tag. Coming through the door, it asks for the password; opened directly, it asks for the number and the password. The page unwraps the admin private key in the browser with both (a wrong password shows "Wrong password"; a wrong number on a direct visit shows "Wrong number") and keeps it only in memory; Lock or reloading forgets it. It reads Firestore (chat, feedback) and the Realtime Database (the Stats tab's leaderboard nodes) with the same anonymous Firebase account the games use in that browser (no Google sign-in), so **the lock on the saved chat and feedback is the sealing, not the rules**: anyone signed in can download the documents, and only the two codes open them. Once in, it says "Welcome, <name>" (the saved Online name, else a party or game name, else it asks once and keeps the answer in localStorage `bra:name`). The page is dark, with a slowly flickering field of light-blue binary digits on one canvas (about eight small updates a second, paused while the tab is hidden, and still with reduced motion). Four tabs:
  - **Stats**, from the Leaderboards' data: one read of `rooms/lb-all/players` in the Realtime Database (the nodes `stats.js` publishes from inside every Back Room game; readable by anyone signed in, so **no rules to add**), checked with leaderboard.js's own `parse` (the page loads `leaderboard.js` with `data-lib`, which only exposes its checks, games table and formatters as `window.BackRoomBoards` and adds nothing to the page), so a node the boards would skip (stale, from the future, over the caps, malformed) is skipped here too and counted as such. On top of that it reads the fields the boards don't show: `ga`/`gn` (the account signed in with Google, and its display name) and `d` (seconds per day per game for the last 30 days: real dates within the last 30 days only, games the boards know, whole seconds, no more in a day than 24 hours or than that game's total; a range's sum for a game is capped at the game's total). It shows totals (hours, players with time in the range, Google accounts among them, accounts on the boards and how many nodes were skipped), hours by game as a bar list (tap one to filter), and a **People** table, one row per account: the Google name with a Google badge when `ga` is set, else the Online name (`n`), the seat tag worked out from the uid (never the node's `st`), their avatar (`avatar.js`) and status tier badge with the miles number (`miles.js`, from the same checked node), hours, miles, games, top game and last seen (the node's `ts`), sortable by each, searchable by name, seat or account; a row expands into that person's hours per game for the range (with the all-time total), their bests (as the boards would show them, Slipstream laps and course records included) and a 30-day activity strip (one bar a day, today outlined; the chosen game only when one is picked). Filters: today, 7 days or 30 days (from the per-day data) or all time (from the totals), game, and Google accounts only. Nothing here is a date-bounded query: it's one read, filtered in the browser, and Refresh reads it again. It doesn't read the analytics (`pv`) or `who` at all. Each person's details have a **Reset hours to 0** button (and **Undo hours reset** once reset) and a **Remove from the leaderboards** button for fake or junk accounts (and **Put back on the leaderboards**): it signs the change with the admin key unwrapped at the door and saves it with leaderboard.js's hours resets, so the boards everywhere count that account's hours from then on; days before the reset drop out of the day views. A person with several devices or browsers that never signed in with Google has an account (row) for each; reset each one. **Same person?** lists pairs of accounts that look like one person, scored and best first (Likely / Possible), with the reasons under each. Only accounts that share a name key (the whole normalized name, the name without trailing numbers, its first three letters) or a custom avatar code (`av`) are compared, so it stays quick with thousands of accounts. Points come mostly from names (Online name `n`, Google name `gn` and its first name, compared without accents, capitals, spaces or punctuation: the same name, the same but for numbers, a Google name's first name, one or two letters apart, one name starting the other; names of three letters or fewer count less, and default names like Player or Guest never count), then the same custom avatar, one account holding nothing the other doesn't, one stopping as the other started (within 3 days, from `d`), and playing mostly the same games; both playing on 3+ of the same days counts against, and two Google sign-ins with different Google names are never paired. The account kept is the Google one, else the one with more hours, else the one seen last; **⇄** swaps it. **Not the same** hides a pair in this browser only (localStorage `bra:notsame`, with a button to show them again). Each person's details also list up to four likely matches (**Might also be**, a looser cut) with Merge and **Tick both**. To merge by hand, tick accounts in the People table (a tick box on each row; a search finds every account, including ones with no time in the date range, and ticks stay while searching), pick which to keep in the bar that appears, and **Merge N into one**. Every merge signs `a` with the admin key (like a reset), so every board, the miles and the Online badges show the accounts as one row: each account's own play added up (hours from batches add; legacy time from before batches, which accounts in one browser may share, counts once; running counts like kills, wins and goals add when the two accounts never shared a device, else the larger; Elo, best scores, fastest times and other bests the better; K/D worked out again), so merged miles add back what each account earned (`fold` in leaderboard.js; miles.js reads the signed merges and the merged accounts' nodes for the Online and Party badges). A group merge is one signed write. The boards only fold an account into one that isn't itself merged, so a merge goes to the end of the kept account's chain, and accounts already merged into the one being merged move along with it (merging an account into one of its own merged accounts turns that merge around). Names aren't unique, so check first. Merged accounts stay in the People table marked Merged and left out of the totals, with **Undo merge** in their details (entries their owner merged at a Google sign-in say so and have no undo).
  - **Players**, a read-only view of each account, with a line at the top saying so. One read of `rooms/lb-all/players` (like Stats; each node checked with leaderboard.js's `parse`, and nodes the boards skip are still listed, marked Skipped, with only their name and last seen). One row per person: accounts merged into one (on the Stats tab, or by their owner at a Google sign-in) are folded into the account kept with leaderboard.js's `collapse`, so its play time, this week, tier, miles, bests and 60-day chart include them (the same rules as the boards), it shows a "+N merged" badge, and its details list each merged account on its own with an Open button; the merged accounts are left out of the list and the summary counts people (and accounts) unless **Show merged accounts** is ticked or a search matches them, and their details say who they were merged into, with a button to open that person. A merge on the Stats tab reads this tab again. Each row: avatar, name (the Google name when `ga` is set, else the Online name), seat, uid, type (Google when the node carries stats.js's `ga` mark, else Anonymous; nodes from before the mark count as Anonymous), tier and miles (`miles.js`), last seen (`ts`), play time (all time, after any hours reset) and this week (leaderboard.js's `week`). Searchable by name, seat or uid and sortable by any column; on a phone the uid, type, tier and week columns fold under the name. Tapping a row shows: per-game time with this week's and the bests (as the boards show them); a day-by-day play-time chart for the last 60 days drawn on a canvas (one bar a day, today outlined, point or tap a day for its games; `d` from the node, checked like Stats, with the `_` other-games time included; stats.js only keeps 30 days, so the older half is shaded and always empty); **Online now** from four single-field reads of `rooms/ol-lobby/players/<uid>` (`ts`, `p`, `pl`, `g`: online if the heartbeat is under 75 s old, party size and leader, game), never the whole node, so its lounge and private messages are never downloaded; and the **account save mirror** size from one read of `rooms/sv-saves/players/<uid>` (bytes, number of saved keys, last saved; the contents aren't shown). No messages, nothing beyond those three nodes, except one thing: **Google account**. A Google player's email travels in their leaderboard node as `ge`, sealed to the admin key by `stats.js` (like the chat log, so anyone can download the node but only the two admin codes open it); the tab opens every `ge` when it loads, with the key unwrapped at the door, and shows the email under the player's name, in their details and in a person's merged-accounts list, and the search finds it. No Google sign-in needed. A sealed box that names a different uid (copied from someone else's node) is shown only in the details, with a warning. Accounts that haven't published since `ge` was added have none yet; their details offer **Look it up in the sign-in records**, an optional Google sign-in on a second Firebase app (`admin-google`, so the page's and the games' anonymous account never changes) that reads Firestore `members/{uid}` (email, Google name, first sign-in, last visit, signed-in page visits; the record `account/account.js` keeps). Who can read `members` is up to the existing Firestore rules (the site owner's Google account, the one the analytics dashboard uses), so **no rules change**; anyone else gets "permission denied". An account never signed in on a page with the top bar has no record, and says so. Nothing in the tab writes (no `set`/`update`/`remove`/`push`/`transaction`), so **no rules to add**. Refresh reads the list again; each account's extra reads happen once per load, when it's opened.
  - **Chat**, the safety log in Firestore `chatlog` (written by `social.js`): cabin-chat messages and reports, newest first, opened with the admin key, filterable by kind, person (a report also matches the person it's about), words and dates (at most 2,000 per load, and it says when it hits that). Entries that won't open (written by something else, or sealed to a different key) are counted and shown as such. There's no delete: the rules don't allow it (old entries can be deleted in the Firebase console).
  - **Feedback**, the `feedback` notes as a to-do list (the newest 500): open ones first, newest first; click a note or its checkbox to mark it done or open again (sets `done`); filter by game and kind; "Show done" shows the done ones after the open ones. "Hide this sender" hides every note from that uid in this browser only (localStorage `fbk-admin:hidden`, same as the old inbox). No delete.

  Everything is shown as plain text (never parsed as HTML). A test hook (`window.__admin`, which swaps Firestore for documents held in the page; sealed ones still need both codes) exists only when `navigator.webdriver` is set. **Who can see what:** the sealed parts of `chatlog` and `feedback` (message text, names, seats, reply details) need both codes. The rest is readable by anyone using the site's anonymous sign-in once these rules are in: which accounts wrote to the log and when, each feedback note's game, kind and done flag, and the `who` documents (Online name, seat and analytics visitor ID per account). The Stats tab's data (`rooms/lb-all/players`) is already readable by anyone signed in, as the Leaderboards need it to be: every player's hours per game and per day, bests, Online name and, for Google sign-ins, the Google display name. Anyone signed in can also tick feedback done or not done. If someone should get the admin page, give them the door word, the number and the password; there's no per-person access.

  **Firestore rules to add** inside `match /databases/{database}/documents { … }` (no emails to fill in). The `feedback` block replaces the earlier `feedback` block: remove that one first. **Before pasting it, look at the existing `feedback` documents in the Firebase console**: notes sent before sealing are plain text, including the optional name and reply details, and this block lets anyone signed in read them, so delete (or copy and delete) any you don't want readable.

  ```
  // Back Room safety log (social.js), read on the admin page (e63334287a3deaa3/d06440218d1813a3/).
  // box is sealed to the admin key in e63334287a3deaa3/adminkey.js: anyone signed in can read the documents,
  // only the admin codes open the box. Entries can't be changed or deleted from the site.
  match /chatlog/{id} {
    allow create: if request.auth != null
      && request.resource.data.keys().hasOnly(['kind', 'uid', 'ts', 'box'])
      && request.resource.data.keys().hasAll(['kind', 'uid', 'ts', 'box'])
      && request.resource.data.kind in ['cabin', 'report']
      && request.resource.data.uid == request.auth.uid
      && request.resource.data.ts == request.time
      && request.resource.data.box is string
      && request.resource.data.box.size() > 0 && request.resource.data.box.size() <= 12000
      && request.resource.data.box.matches('1[.][A-Za-z0-9+/=]+[.][A-Za-z0-9+/=]+[.][A-Za-z0-9+/=]+');
    allow read: if request.auth != null;
  }

  // One document per account: its Online name and seat and its analytics visitor ID (social.js). Nothing reads it any
  // more (the admin page's Stats come from the leaderboard nodes), so this block is optional; social.js still writes it.
  match /who/{docId} {
    allow create, update: if request.auth != null
      && docId == request.auth.uid
      && request.resource.data.keys().hasOnly(['uid', 'vid', 'name', 'seat', 'ts'])
      && request.resource.data.keys().hasAll(['uid', 'vid', 'name', 'seat', 'ts'])
      && request.resource.data.uid == request.auth.uid
      && request.resource.data.vid is string && request.resource.data.vid.matches('[a-z0-9]{0,40}')
      && request.resource.data.name is string
      && request.resource.data.name.size() > 0 && request.resource.data.name.size() <= 40
      && request.resource.data.seat is string && request.resource.data.seat.matches('[0-9]{1,2}[A-F]')
      && request.resource.data.ts == request.time;
    allow read: if request.auth != null;
  }

  // Back Room feedback box (feedback.js). Replaces the earlier feedback block.
  // New notes are sealed (box); the old plain shape is still accepted from browsers with an old copy of feedback.js.
  match /feedback/{id} {
    allow create: if request.auth != null
      && request.resource.data.uid == request.auth.uid
      && request.resource.data.ts == request.time
      && request.resource.data.game is string && request.resource.data.game.matches('[a-z0-9-]{1,30}')
      && request.resource.data.kind in ['idea', 'bug', 'other']
      && (
        (request.resource.data.keys().hasOnly(['uid', 'ts', 'game', 'kind', 'box'])
          && request.resource.data.keys().hasAll(['uid', 'ts', 'game', 'kind', 'box'])
          && request.resource.data.box is string
          && request.resource.data.box.size() > 0 && request.resource.data.box.size() <= 12000
          && request.resource.data.box.matches('1[.][A-Za-z0-9+/=]+[.][A-Za-z0-9+/=]+[.][A-Za-z0-9+/=]+'))
        ||
        (request.resource.data.keys().hasOnly(['msg', 'game', 'kind', 'name', 'contact', 'page', 'uid', 'ts'])
          && request.resource.data.keys().hasAll(['msg', 'game', 'kind', 'name', 'contact', 'page', 'uid', 'ts'])
          && request.resource.data.msg is string
          && request.resource.data.msg.size() > 0 && request.resource.data.msg.size() <= 1000
          && request.resource.data.name is string && request.resource.data.name.size() <= 40
          && request.resource.data.contact is string && request.resource.data.contact.size() <= 80
          && request.resource.data.page is string && request.resource.data.page.size() <= 200)
      );
    allow read: if request.auth != null;
    // Marking a note done or open again changes nothing else. No deletes from the site.
    allow update: if request.auth != null
      && request.resource.data.diff(resource.data).affectedKeys().hasOnly(['done'])
      && request.resource.data.done is bool;
  }
  ```

  Nothing needs adding to the analytics `pv` rules: the admin page doesn't read the page views.
- `joukowsky-dynamics/`: only `preview.jpg` (used by the Engineering cards) and a redirect page. The Joukowsky Dynamics workbench itself is hosted on the [Joukowsky-Dynamics](https://github.com/ASCoding11/Joukowsky-Dynamics) repo's own GitHub Pages site, https://ascoding11.github.io/Joukowsky-Dynamics/, and the cards link there. Keep it there rather than copying it into this repo, so there is one copy to update.
- `tideline/`: only `preview.jpg` (used by the Engineering cards) and a redirect page. The Tideline radar-satellite workbench is hosted in the same [Joukowsky-Dynamics](https://github.com/ASCoding11/Joukowsky-Dynamics) repo, at https://ascoding11.github.io/Joukowsky-Dynamics/satellite/, alongside its CAD.
- `harvest-steps/`: the Harvest Steps workbench (public, in the sitemap, with a card on the Engineering page and in the home page's Engineering card). Unlike the other two engineering projects it lives in this repo: one self-contained `index.html` (Three.js r128 from cdnjs, like the games) plus `preview.jpg` for the cards. Five tabs, kept in the address as `#sequence`, `#blueprint`, `#physics`, `#market` and `#prototype`: an animated footstep through the tile, an exploded parts view with a datasheet per part (click a part or pick it from the list), an energy calculator, a schematic market day, and a bench prototype build guide (how the circuit works, an SVG schematic, parts and tools, frame assembly, a 12-step soldering walkthrough whose perfboard view is drawn by `buildBoard()` from `PB_PARTS` and highlights each step's parts from `STEPS`, multimeter checks, first power-up, the Arduino logger sketch with calibration, test plan and troubleshooting). All the numbers come from one function, `model()`: walker mass × g × plate travel, minus the return springs (60 N preload, 15 N/mm), times the gear, generator and electronics efficiencies; the market tab uses the same result, so changing an input on the physics tab changes it too. Visitors (60,000 on a peak day) and the electricity price ($0.29/kWh) are sourced figures named on the physics tab; steps per visitor (6, 60 or 400 for coverage A, B or C) are estimates. Nothing is stored. `window.__hs` (`seek`, `setTab`, `setMode`, `select`, `showStep`) is there for screenshots and tests. Works with touch: drag to turn, pinch to zoom; on narrow screens the panel moves under the 3D view.
- `daedalus/`: Project Daedalus, a build plan for a compact one-stick Bluetooth LE game controller made in a batch of about 30 (public, in the sitemap, with a card on the Engineering page and in the home page's Engineering card). It is a reading page with a few live parts, not a workbench: `index.html` uses the `.guide` styles from `site.css` plus a `<style>` block of its own (`dd-` classes: inline SVG drawings that take their colours from the site's variables, wide tables in `.dd-scroll` wrappers so they scroll sideways on a phone, the code box, the 3D viewer and the tester). `preview.jpg` is the card picture. **The design has not been built**: the page says so in its first box; take that box out, and update the costs, only when someone has built and tested one. Prices, JLCPCB fees, the US duty rate, shop listings and the FCC and Bluetooth SIG figures were looked up on 14 November 2026, and the parts on the board again on 16 November; they are dated on the page. Re-check them before changing the cost tables. No ads on this page, and nothing is stored. Its parts:
  - `firmware/daedalus/daedalus.ino`: the Arduino sketch for an ESP32-C6-WROOM-1 (esp32 board package 3.x, NimBLE-Arduino 2.5 or newer), with `platformio.ini` beside it for PlatformIO users. The page links to it and loads it into the "Show the code" box with `fetch` when that box is first opened. It compiles (last checked against esp32 3.3.12 and NimBLE-Arduino 2.5.2) but has never run on hardware. **The pin table on the page and `BTN_PIN` / the `PIN_` constants in the sketch must always match**, and so must the Arduino settings listed in both.
  - `bom.json`, `make_bom.py` and `daedalus-bom-jlcpcb.csv`: the parts list. **`bom.json` is the one list of parts**: `board` (one entry for each different part the factory places: reference letters, value, rating, size, what it does, maker's part number, LCSC number, Basic or Extended and whether JLCPCB's Extended fee applies, price each at the quantity a batch of 30 uses, and the reasoning for the value in `why`), `hand` (everything fitted by hand or otherwise in the box, with the amount for one controller, what to buy for 30 with spares, `used` = dollars in one controller and `buy30` = dollars for the batch) and `tools` (bought once). The page's three Parts list tables, their totals and the "where each value comes from" list are built from it in the browser (the block of the script that starts "The three parts tables"), and its download link hands over a CSV made from the same data. `python3 make_bom.py` writes the same CSV to `daedalus-bom-jlcpcb.csv` (JLCPCB's assembly columns: Comment, Designator, Footprint, JLCPCB Part #; board parts only, since the stick is soldered by hand) and prints the sums; `--check` says whether the file is out of date. **Don't edit the CSV or the tables by hand: change `bom.json`, run the script, and commit both.** The board parts' numbers, prices, stock and Basic / Extended class were read from JLCPCB's parts library on 16 November 2026; anything that could not be read from a listing or datasheet has a `confirm` note, shown on the page as "To confirm". Static text that repeats these figures and must be changed with them: the Cost section (the 30-unit breakdown adds the board parts, fees for the Extended parts that carry one, and the `used` figures to $23.18), the "Parts" and "Case" rows of the Specifications table, the picks in "Options for each part", the reference letters (R6, C10 and so on) in the Wiring section, and the prices in `INFO`. The two grip screws are 6 mm long and the four board screws 8 mm: that comes from the case model (`seat_below_pcb` + `insert_depth` leave an 8 mm screw no room at the grips), so if those sizes change, work the screw lengths out again.
  - `case/daedalus_case.py` and `case/sculpt.py`: the case, the button caps and the board outline as one parametric model (`pip install cadquery numpy scipy scikit-image manifold3d`, then `python daedalus_case.py` in that folder; it takes a few minutes). Every size is in the `P` dictionary at the top, with the ones still to be checked against real parts marked MEASURE. Running it rewrites everything else in `case/`: the STL files to print, `board.step`, `board.dxf` (board outline and holes), `template.svg` and `layout.json` (where every part sits on the board). **Don't edit those by hand; change `P` and run the script**, then commit the script and its outputs together. The outline is the `outline_right` list (each corner of the right-hand half with its own radius; the left half is mirrored). **Version 4 is sculpted**: `P["topo"]` sets a shallow dome for the front, a flat back with a swell in each grip, and the radius each face rolls into the sides with. `sculpt.py` describes that body as a distance field, turns it into triangles (marching cubes), and the shell is the space between that surface and the same surface 2 mm further in; the holes, posts and slots are still exact CadQuery solids, cut into the triangles with manifold3d. So the two shells exist only as STL (there is no STEP of them), the inside of each shell slopes (button bases, post heights and the battery pocket are worked out from `body.ceiling` / `body.floor`, not from fixed heights), and there are four face-button files because each top follows the surface where it sits. The shells print rim-down with supports inside; the back shell's STL has to be turned over in the slicer. Same outline, board and part positions as version 3 (125 × 78 mm, a 108 mm board with the radio module at the bottom edge and USB-C dead centre; the board's top corners follow the inside of the case, so `layout.json` carries the board's edge as `board_outline` points and `board.dxf` has the exact curves). Checked in software for clashes between the shells, the board, the buttons, the battery and the parts' datasheet sizes; never printed. The side-view drawing in the page's case section is static SVG generated from the model (two slices of the body field, at X = 0 and X = 50); if `topo` changes, redraw it and update the sizes in the page's tables. `case/v3/` (flat faces, 21.7 mm thick, prints without supports, same board) and `case/v2/` (140 × 85 × 22.7 mm, 122 mm board) are the earlier designs, kept for reference: each has its own copy of the model and a STEP file, and running that copy writes the rest of its files beside it. Leave them as they are. Version 1 (a plain slab) is only in the git history.
