  - The page's scripts (inline, at the end): the board drawing and its table are built from `case/layout.json`, so they always match the model; the **workbench** near the top (`#bench`, the dark panel; its styles are the `.wb` rules and its code the last block of the script) has three tabs. *Build steps* is a 3D viewer with 12 steps (`STEPS`: which parts show, which are lit, how far they are pulled apart, which way the camera looks, and the step's text); it fetches Three.js r128 from cdnjs when the panel scrolls into view and loads the small in-place meshes in `case/view/` (a binary-STL reader is in the page; drag to turn, the steps still read without 3D). *Floor plan* draws four layers (front shell, buttons and pad, circuit board, battery and back shell) from `case/view/plan.json`, and clicking a part fills the side panel from `INFO` (name, part number, price, pin, note). *The working* recomputes play time, charging, delay, weight and cost from the controls and lists each check with its limit and its sum written out (`Work.calc`); at the plan settings it must agree with the Specifications and Cost tables (35 mA, 30 h, 4.2 h, $23.20), so change both together; the per-unit prices for each batch size are `COST` in that block and the first table of the Cost section. `case/view_parts.py` writes `case/view/` (run it after `daedalus_case.py`): simplified shells, the printed buttons in place, and plain blocks at datasheet sizes for the bought parts. When the assembly steps, a pin, a price or a part changes, update `STEPS`, `INFO` and the written sections together; the Specifications table and the parts option tables are static text: when a part, a size in `P` or a setting in the sketch changes, change them too; the tester polls `navigator.getGamepads()` and lights the buttons in the sketch's order (`NAMES` must match the `Btn` enum in the sketch). The front-view drawing near the top is static SVG: its body outline is generated from `outline_right` (lines and arcs through the same tangent points as `rounded_polygon` in the model, at 5 px per mm with X = 380 + 5x and Y = 215 − 5y), and the buttons are placed by hand at the same scale; if `P` changes, redraw both. `preview.jpg` shows the same drawing.
- `applied-robotics/`: only `preview.jpg` (used by the Engineering cards). The Applied Robotics Workbench (six SVG circuit sheets for a two-wheel rover, bench calculators, an Arduino sketch and a parts list) is hosted on the [applied-robotics](https://github.com/ASCoding11/applied-robotics) repo's own GitHub Pages site, https://ascoding11.github.io/applied-robotics/, and the cards link there. Keep it there rather than copying it into this repo, so there is one copy to update.
- `about/` and the guides (`hype-cycle-exchange/guide/`, `boxed/guide/`, `docket/guide/`, `prompted/guide/`): plain reading pages, written in our own words, that explain the site and how to get the most out of each public tool (running Hype Cycle Exchange in class, AMC 10 and AIME prep with Boxed, planning study time with Docket, handling supplemental essays with Prompted). They use the `.guide` styles at the end of `site.css`, are in `sitemap.xml`, and are linked from the Projects and Productivity cards ("…guide" buttons) and from the footers (About). No ads on them. When a tool changes in a way the guide describes (rules, limits, modes), update its guide too.
- `docket/`: Docket, a study planner for Canvas students (plain JavaScript, no backend; everything is saved in the student's browser). It imports assignments three ways: a bookmarklet that reads the Canvas API from the student's logged-in Canvas tab and hands the data to Docket in the URL hash (its source is the `bmSrc` block in the page), the Canvas calendar feed / any `.ics` or Google Calendar `.zip` export, or pasted text from a Canvas assignments page. It estimates time and difficulty per assignment, then schedules study sessions around calendar events and the student's activities. No ads on this page.
- `prompted/`: Prompted, a supplemental essay tracker for college applicants (plain JavaScript, no backend; everything is saved in the student's browser under `prompted:v1`). Students add schools with a plan and deadline, paste a supplement page to pull out each prompt with its word or character limit and whether it's optional (`parsePrompts` in the page), write drafts with a live count, save and restore versions, and start new essays from existing drafts. A reuse map groups similar prompts across schools by kind (Why us, academic interest, community, and so on). Backups download as JSON; all drafts download as one .txt. `prompted/schools.js` is the school library: the U.S. News 2027 top 50 national universities with their 2026–27 supplement prompts (as short summaries in our own words, with real limits), choose-N sets, college- or program-specific prompts keyed to the student's intended major, application plans and deadlines. UC campuses share one application, so they're added as a single school. Update the file each August when schools publish new prompts, and keep every prompt as a summary in your own words: don't paste schools' official prompt text into this repo, even though it's posted publicly. No ads on this page.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/m/`: the phone version of the Back Room: every game (the Back Room ones plus Hype Cycle Exchange) as a tile, under the same six headings as the computer version, in the same play-time order (Cars, Shooters, Parkour, Party games, Puzzles, Arcade; `MODES`), with a row of six buttons above the lists that jump to each heading (`.gnav`), the Turkey Week switch under the title during the event (`data-turkey-switch` in the header's `.tkrow`; see `events.js`), and grouped by kind under each (each `GROUPS` row is `[kind, games, mode, 1 for the puzzle tiles]`, mode `'cars'`, `'shooters'`, `'parkour'`, `'partygames'`, `'puzzles'` or `'arcade'`, with the rows of one mode kept together; Hype Cycle Exchange is under Arcade). Puzzles starts with the small tiles (`.grid.pz`: what it is in plain words, then its name): Deepword, Trivium, the listed daily puzzles and, last, Daily Puzzles (the fifteen least played puzzles, Boxed among them, came off on 18 Nov 2026: see the Back Room page above; their thumbnails stay). The daily puzzles aren't counted as games; Deepword and Trivium are (a `1` at the end of their rows), and the Puzzles heading says "12 puzzles + 4 games". At the bottom of Puzzles, a small tab (`.ask.ask-sm`, `pzAsk`) asks "Miss a puzzle we took away, or want a new one?" with a Tell us button (`data-feedback-open="Puzzles: "`). The Daily Puzzles tile (`daily`, not counted, a whole row wide with `.pz-all` so the puzzle rows come out even) is the page with every listed puzzle and the streak; it opens in the frame like a game, and the daily games it links to open in the same frame (its `scope` is the whole Back Room folder). After the games, a "Want more games, or have an idea?" note has a Tell us button that opens the feedback bar. A game opens full screen in a frame under a thin bar with the way back, and the last one played shows at the top as Keep playing. The Leaderboards section (leaderboard.js) comes after the games. Its own look is dark; it also has a light look (the light variables at the top of its `<style>`, including the section colours `--c-arcade` … `--c-partygames`), used when Light is chosen with the theme switch in its header (`data-theme-switch`, next to Main site), or in Auto on a phone in light mode (see `theme.js`). It can be added to a phone's home screen (`manifest.webmanifest`, `icon-*.png`), where it runs like an app; on Android it also goes full screen and turns sideways games sideways. Tiles use the small copies in `m/thumbs/` (560 × 350) rather than the full previews. On a phone, entering the code on the home page opens this page in place of the Back Room, still at the home page's address, and invite links (`#<room>`) open their game straight away. The Back Room page also links to it (`data-direct`, so the home page opens it as its own page instead of in its game layer); that address is the one to add to a home screen. It isn't in the sitemap or linked from the public pages.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/stats.js`: play time and personal bests for the Leaderboards (Back Room). Every active Back Room game loads it with one line in its `<head>`, `<script src="/e63334287a3deaa3/stats.js" data-game="<folder>" defer></script>`; it runs inside the game page, so it works the same when the game is opened directly or inside the home page, phone page or party layer. Play time: a second counts while the tab is visible and there was a real key, click, touch, mouse movement or gamepad input in the last 30 seconds (made-up events and the still "mouse moves" browsers send when the page changes don't count); a quiet stretch after that (thinking, a cutscene, waiting for the next round) is held back and counts once the player touches something again within 2 minutes, and is dropped if they don't (they walked away) or the tab is hidden. Only one tab or window of the browser counts at a time, the one with the latest input (localStorage `lbs:tab`, a claim renewed every second), so two Back Room windows side by side don't count the same minute twice. At most 24 hours a day are counted for all games together. Bests come two ways. Some it reads from what the game already saves (`READ` in the file): Coil `coil:best` (longest snake), Shell Crackers `shellcr-rank` (Elo, once you've played a rated match), Updraft `updraft:best` (best height, fastest summit), Spire `spire:profile` (fastest full tower, towers climbed), Tumble Trials `tumble-stats` (crowns, shows), Showdown `sd:pstats` (biggest pot and hands played in practice) and Trivium `trivium.daily.*` and `trivium.streak` (best finished Daily score, best Daily streak). The rest come from one-line hooks at the moment it happens in the game, `window.BackRoomStats && BackRoomStats.add('kills', 1)`: Shell Crackers kills (practice and online, not the training range), Rumble Karts wrecks and rounds you top, Backrooms 1v1–4v4 kills and match wins (against the Entity and online), deaths in those three games (`deaths`: Shell Crackers when you're cracked, again practice and online only; Backrooms when the Entity or another player gets you; Rumble Karts whenever you're wrecked, lava included) and, next to each kill hook, `kdkills` (the same kills or wrecks again, counted from the day deaths started being counted, so the K/D board isn't thrown off by kills from before; each game's own scoreboard also shows this match's or round's K/D next to the kills: Shell Crackers' standings table, the Backrooms results screen and the player's own row in Rumble Karts' results), Boostball goals and match wins, Coil cut-offs, Odd One Out props found and rounds your team wins, Chromaflage hiders found and rounds you stay hidden, and Slipstream's fastest lap per track (`BackRoomStats.min('lap:<track id>', seconds)`, every completed lap, including laps with a track cut: the game's penalties only add to the race time). Bests from a single round or race come from the same kind of hook with `max` (or `min` for times), next to the running counts: Slipstream's course record per track and race length (`rec:<track id>:<laps>`, the full race time with any track-cut penalties added, as in the results, for a race you finished over every lap; `onFinish`), Rumble Karts' most wrecks in one round (`rwrecks`, counted per round id), Shell Crackers' and Backrooms' most kills in one match (`mkills`; Backrooms counts the Entity in practice and players online), Boostball's most goals in one match (`mgoals`, from the match's own goal count), Chromaflage's most hiders you found in one round and Odd One Out's most props you found in one round as a hunter (`rfound`), Odd One Out's survival as a prop in seconds (`survive`, the game's own `res.surv`: when you were found, or the whole hunt if you weren't), Spire's fastest full obby (`obby`, all 49 stages in one go from stage 1; a run continued from a checkpoint doesn't count) and Tumble Trials' most crowns in a row (`crownrun`, solo and online shows, counted within one visit to the page). The hooks only record; they don't change the games. The API is `add(stat, n)` (a running count, at most 100 at a time), `min(stat, value)` (a time: lower wins) and `max(stat, value)`; stat names are short lowercase words (`a-z`, `0-9`, `:`, `-`), and a name starting with `lap:` or `rec:` or listed in `LOWER` (`tower`, `summit`, `obby`, `total`, Putterby's `card9`, and the daily puzzles' `fewest`, `guesses`, `tries`, `fastest`, `fast-easy`, `fast-medium`, `fast-hard`) is lower-wins; `LOWER` is one list for every game, so a name in it must mean lower-wins in every game that uses it. At most 240 stats are kept per game (`MAX_STATS`; Slipstream can have a lap, a clean lap and six course records on each of 26 tracks). **Record seasons (how a game's records are reset for everyone):** nobody can delete other players' nodes, so a reset is done with names. `SEASON` (`{ slipstream: 2 }`) lists the games whose record stats (names starting `lap:`, `clap:`, `rec:` or `crec:`) carry a season. The game still calls `BackRoomStats.min('lap:<track>', t)`; `bump` stores it under this season's name, `lap:s2:<track>` (`seasonKey`). A stored record with no season in its name (season 1) or a lower season is `retired`, and `cleanG` leaves retired names out. The browser's store (`load`), the node as read in `apply` and an anonymous account being merged in all go through `cleanG`, so the old records are gone from the browser's store as soon as a page with this file loads and from the player's own node at that page's first publish (about a minute into play, in any Back Room game), without touching `s`, `elo`, the game's other stats or any other game; that also frees their places under the 240-name cap, which old and new names together could pass. Nothing brings them back: the "never below what this browser last saw" rule (`hw`) is for seconds only. A page from before the reset that is still open keeps writing season-1 names; a page with the current file strips them from the shared store the next time it saves and from the node the next time it publishes, and leaderboard.js never reads them, so they can sit in a node for a while but never show. A name from a season above this file's (a newer copy of the file in another tab) is kept. Slipstream is on season 2 since 17 November 2026. **To reset Slipstream's records again:** raise `SEASON.slipstream` by one here, set `SLIP_SEASON` in leaderboard.js to the same number and `SLIP_RESET` there to the date; the game doesn't change (leaderboard.js warns in the console if the two numbers differ on a page that has both). Only ever raise the number. A reset can't be undone by reverting the change: each player's old records are deleted from their node the first time their browser publishes with the new file. It also takes away the miles those records were worth (miles.js works from what leaderboard.js reads) until the player sets new times. **Counting once, and only up:** the browser keeps the seconds it hasn't sent yet, and each publish sends them as a batch that is *added* to the node's totals in a Firebase transaction (an atomic read-modify-write), never its own idea of the total. So two devices or browsers on one account add up, instead of each writing its own total over the other's (which made totals go up and down and kept only the larger device's time), and a browser never hands its time to the next account it signs in with. Each batch has an id (its time in ms) and the node keeps the last batch id from each device (`q`), so a batch that reached the database but wasn't marked sent (the page closed mid-way) is never added twice; a batch made under another account (a sign-in or sign-out since) goes to the current account unless that account's node already has it. Totals never drop below what the browser last saw committed (`hw`), so an older copy of stats.js still open somewhere can't lower them for good. Saved in the browser under localStorage `lbs:v2` (`{un: {<folder>: seconds not sent}, ud: {YYYYMMDD: {<folder>: seconds}}, d: day, ds: seconds counted that day, b: {<folder>: {<stat>: best}}, tc: {<folder>: seconds this browser ever counted}, out: the batch being sent {u, id, g, d}, hw: [[uid, {<folder>: seconds}]…] (last committed totals, 4 accounts), v1, lk, lkd}`), `lbs:dev` (this browser's device id) and `lbs:uid` (the account it last published under). **Before this version** the browser kept running totals in `lbs:v1` (`{g: {<folder>: {s, <stat>}}, d, ds, dy}`), shared by every account used in that browser; on the first load of this version its bests become `b` and its seconds are folded in once, into the account in `lbs:uid` keeping the larger (the old rule), or added to the first account it publishes under if it never published. `lbs:v1` is left alone. Published at most once a minute while something changed, and when the tab is hidden or the page closes (if already connected; otherwise the batch waits in the browser for the next publish of any game), to the Firebase Realtime Database (same project and rules as the games) at `rooms/lb-all/players/<uid>` (prefix `lb`, not an invite prefix): the shared rules only let a browser write its own node, need `n, c, x, z, h, ts` (`x`/`z`/`h` always 0) and refuse names longer than 14 characters, so the node is `n` (the Online name `ol:name`, else the Google account's first name, else the party name, else a game's saved name: the same order as the Online panel; a rename on the Back Room page reaches an open game as a `storage` event and the game republishes about 1.5 s later, at most every 10 s and only once it is already connected), `c` (colour), `ts` (server time), `v` (2; nodes still written by the older version say 1 and read the same), `st` (the seat tag from social.js, for convenience; readers work it out from the uid), `g` (`{<folder>: {s: seconds, <stat>: number}}`, `s` being the account's total over every device), `d` (play seconds per day per game for the last 30 days, `{YYYYMMDD: {<folder>: seconds, _: seconds}}` by the browser's local date; at most 30 days and 8 games a day, and the time in any further games that day goes into one `_` entry for all of them (`DAY_REST`; before it, a ninth game's time was simply left out of `d`), so a day's total is complete for the Leaderboards' This week board even when it isn't split by game past the first eight; at most 24 hours a day, `_` included; the oldest days are dropped until the whole node fits in 7,000 characters, under leaderboard.js's 8,000 limit), `q` (`{<device id>: last batch id}`, the 12 most recent devices), `lg` (`{<folder>: seconds}`, the totals as they stood when this version first wrote the node: "legacy" time, which may overlap between accounts used in one browser, so a merge keeps the larger of it instead of adding), `al` (`{<uid>: ms}`, anonymous accounts merged into this one, at most 8), `to` (on an anonymous account's node: the account it was merged into) and, only when the account it publishes under is a Google sign-in (not anonymous: the hub page's `account.js` sign-in that party.js reuses, or the game's own Firebase user), `ga: 1` and `gn` (the Google display name, 40 characters, never the email or photo URL); `av`, the player's edited avatar as a code (see `avatar.js`; taken from this browser's `avatar:v1` when it is for this account, else kept as the node has it); and `nt`, the colour of their name tile (`'t1:<index>'` or `'t0'`, see `avatar.js`; taken from this browser's `nametile:v1` only when it is for this account and the account is a Google sign-in, else kept as the node has it). The admin page reads `d`, `ga` and `gn` (it skips `_`, which isn't a game); the Leaderboards read `d` for This week and ignore `ga` and `gn`. Older copies of stats.js still open somewhere drop `_` when they next write the node (their `cleanDays` doesn't know it), which only loses that overflow time from `d`, never from the totals. The node is never removed on disconnect, because it is the player's entry; clearing the browser doesn't wipe it either (only unsent seconds live in the browser). The older version overwrote the whole node, so while a tab with it is still open it can briefly lower a total or drop `q`, `lg` and `al`; the next publish of this version puts the total back. **Linking accounts:** the games sign in anonymously, and signing in with Google on a hub page makes a new account, so before this version the anonymous account's entry stayed on the boards as a second row with the same name (its hours were usually also in the Google entry, because the browser's record was shared). Now account-nudge.js keeps the anonymous user the hub page had and, the moment a Google sign-in arrives on that page, loads stats.js with `data-lib` and calls `BackRoomStatsLib.link()`: it writes `to: <Google uid>` on the anonymous node while it can still act as that account (a second Firebase app in memory, given that user with `updateCurrentUser`), then the Google node merges it in its next transaction (time counted since batches is added, legacy time keeps the larger, bests the better, `al[<anon uid>]` set; done once, and remembered in `lbs:v2` as `lk`/`lkd`). leaderboard.js leaves an entry out only when both sides agree, so nobody can hide someone else's. Entries split before this version can't be linked this way (the anonymous sign-in is gone from the browser); the admin page suggests them and can merge them. Firebase: inside the home, phone or party layer it borrows that page's sign-in (`window.parent.Party`), so it's the same account the panels show (looked up again at every publish, so a Google sign-in on the hub page while a game is open is followed); if the game is online it uses the game's own Firebase; otherwise, after a minute, it loads the SDK in a hidden same-origin frame and signs in there (the same saved sign-in, so the same uid), so it never touches the game's own Firebase globals (Trivium's are another project). **Everything is self-reported by the player's own browser**, so someone with developer tools can write any numbers into their own node; leaderboard.js checks and caps what it shows, but it isn't cheat-proof. To add a stat for a new game: load stats.js in the game with its folder name, then either add a reader to `READ` for something the game already saves or add a one-line hook where it happens, and give the game a row in `GAMES` in leaderboard.js. A test hook (`window.__lbs`) exists only when `navigator.webdriver` is set. **Email (`ge`):** on a Google account, each publish carries the account's email sealed to the admin key (`adminkey.js`'s `BackRoomSeal` of `{ k: 'ge', e, u: uid }`, about 200 characters, loaded only when there's something to seal), sealed once per account in each browser (localStorage `lbs:ge`) and otherwise kept as the node has it; only the admin page opens it. Never on anonymous accounts, and never in the clear. **Miles kept across a records reset (`sx`):** before a game's retired record names are dropped (Slipstream's season 1), each write folds them into `sx` on the node, `{ slipstream: { l: { <track>: best lap }, r: race records } }`, only ever added to (the quicker lap, the larger count; accounts merged in bring theirs) and carried on in every write. leaderboard.js reads `sx` and any retired names still on a node not written since the reset (`p.sx`, for miles only, never shown on a board), and miles.js counts per track the better of that lap and this season's, and the larger of the two record counts, so a reset never takes miles away.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/leaderboard.js`: the Leaderboards (Back Room). A "Leaderboards" section under the games on the Back Room page and the phone page (one `<script defer>` in each page's `<head>`; the home page's `reveal()` also loads it, because the home page doesn't run the Back Room page's `<head>`). A **Players | Aisles** tab sits above the controls (not in a game's own panel): Players shows the Board menu, which picks "Miles (all games)" or a game; Aisles swaps it for Aisle Miles | Aisle Elo, which aisles.js draws (see `aisles.js`). Going back to Players reopens the player board you had before (`lb:view` keeps it as `p`). **The Miles board** (the first one, `view.g` `'all'`) ranks players by miles, the same "miles with us" as the status tiers (miles.js): rank, avatar, name · seat, tier badge and the game played most, then the miles ("12,450 mi"). On every board a row takes the colour of the player's name tile when their node carries a valid `nt` (`avatar.js`: `learnTile` checks it, `paint` colours the row; colours only, so rows keep their height), and your own row then keeps a ring in the page's accent in place of its tint. Hours aren't shown on this board, nor anywhere else players can see: only the admin page shows hours. A **This week / All time** toggle sits beside the Board menu; it opens on This week, and once someone picks the other it's remembered in the browser (`lb:span`, `'week'` or `'all'`). All time is `BackRoomMiles.of(node).miles`, the number behind the badge (until miles.js has loaded, play-time miles only; the board redraws when it arrives). This week is **miles from play time only**, `BackRoomMiles.HOUR` (100) an hour, though the note under the board just says the miles come from play time, since Monday: the week starts on Monday, so it is the dates from this week's Monday to today by the viewer's clock (the board empties at midnight on Sunday night), plus tomorrow (a player in a time zone ahead), from the per-day history `d` that stats.js keeps on each node (seconds per game per day, keyed by the player's own local date). Wins and bests carry no date, so they can't be put in a week honestly and only count in All time; a line under the board says so. Each day is checked like the hours: at most 24 hours a day, only games the boards know (at most 12 a day; stats.js keeps 8) plus `_` (the day's time in any further games, below); a game's week can't be more than its all-time hours, nor the week more than all the hours; players with nothing this week aren't on it. After an hours reset only days from the reset's date count, still capped at the hours since the reset, so a reset zeroes the week straight away. Merged entries take per game the larger week, like the hours. Parse adds `wk` (`{ <game>: seconds, _ }`), `week` (seconds) and `wtop` (the game played most this week) to each player. The same toggle switches each game's play-time board between this week and all time (not its other boards: a best has no date). That board (key `s`, seconds played) is labelled "Miles from play time" (chip "Miles") and shows the seconds as miles (`HOUR` an hour, rounded down), ranked by the seconds as before; it never shows hours or minutes. Days kept before stats.js had `_` lost the time of a ninth game and on in a day, so a few heavy days from late October 2026 count a little short in This week (all time is unaffected). Each game shows its boards as buttons (the play-time Miles board first, then the game's bests, best single rounds before all-time counts) and opens on the first one anyone is on: Rumble Karts most wrecks in a round, wrecks and round wins; Shell Crackers Elo, most kills in a match, kills and K/D; Backrooms most kills in a match, kills, K/D and match wins (Rumble Karts also has a K/D, wrecks over times wrecked); Slipstream, which opens on **every track's fastest lap** (one row per track, all 26: the fastest lap there, who set it and how many drivers have a time; a track with none says "no times yet"; tapping a track opens its own board), then, with a Track menu (tracks with times first, most drivers first, picked once the data is in, so it isn't stuck on the first track in the list), that track's course record for each race length (3, 5 and 10 laps, the lengths the game offers, plus any other length someone has a record for: "3-lap record"; these boards add a small line, "Race times include any track-cut penalties (+2.5 s each).") and its fastest lap; Boostball most goals in a match, goals and match wins; Coil longest snake and cut-offs; Trivium best Daily score and streak; Showdown biggest pot and hands played (practice); Chromaflage most hiders found in a round, hiders found and rounds stayed hidden; Spire fastest tower, fastest full obby and towers climbed; Updraft fastest summit and best height; Odd One Out most props found in a round, longest survival as a prop, props found and rounds won; Tumble Trials crowns, most crowns in a row and shows. Times show as m:ss.mmm (survival as m:ss), counts with their unit ("12 kills"). **K/D (all time)** is worked out by the board, never read from the node: `kdkills` / max(1, `deaths`) to 2 decimals, both checked counts (each at most half a kill a second played, and `kdkills` no more than the all-time kills), ranked only with at least 20 kills and capped at 100.00; `deaths` and `kdkills` aren't boards of their own (`hide` in `GAMES`; the admin page still lists them). A remembered board that has nobody on it gives way to the game's first board with someone, but a board picked by hand on the page stays even when empty. Each board shows the top 25 and, if you're further down, your own row after a gap; your row is highlighted with a "You" tag (you are the account party.js signs in with, or the one stats.js last published under). The choice of board (and Slipstream track) is remembered in the browser (`lb:view`, `{g, b, t}`). It reads nothing until the section is near the screen (or a board is picked), then reads `rooms/lb-all/players` once; Refresh reads it again (at most every 8 seconds). One exception, for the **"#1 all time" / "#1 this week" tags** that miles.js puts by the tier badge of whoever is first on the Miles board (see miles.js): when a tier badge is drawn anywhere on the page and miles.js has no answer newer than 15 minutes, it calls `BackRoomBoards.readTops()`, which makes this same read early; the section then uses it as its own for 5 minutes (`FRESH`) rather than reading again when it's scrolled to. After every read (`take`), `tops(people, everyone)` gives `{all, week, allTie, weekTie}`: the uids that `rows()` ranks 1 on the Miles board for All time and for This week (all of them on a tie, plus any entry shown as one row with a leader), and that goes to `BackRoomMiles.setTops`, so the tags can't disagree with the board. The same answer carries the **Slipstream ace** (`ace` and `aceN`), the earned award miles.js draws: `ace(people, everyone)` counts, for each driver, the tracks where `trackRows` names them (the fastest lap this record season, so the same checked nodes, merged accounts as one driver, removed ones never), and gives the award to whoever leads the most, 3 or more (`ACE_MIN`), everyone tied for most, or nobody. Slipstream's lap and record boards (not the play-time board or Elo), on the Leaderboards and in the game's own panel, carry one line under the heading: "Slipstream ace: <name>, leading 4 tracks. It goes to whoever holds the fastest lap on the most tracks, 3 or more.", or that it is unclaimed (the game's panel works this out itself, since game pages don't load miles.js). The same answer also carries **Top Cracker** (`crack` and `crackN`), the second earned award: `cracker(people, everyone)` takes the rows `rows()` gives Shell Crackers' Kills (all time) board (the `kills` stat in `GAMES`) and gives it to whoever is ranked first, everyone tied for first, but only with 25 kills or more (`CRACK_MIN`), else nobody. That board, on the Leaderboards and in Shell Crackers' own Leaderboard panel, carries the same kind of line under its heading: "Top Cracker: <name>, with 1,520 kills. It goes to whoever has the most Shell Crackers kills of all time, 25 or more.", or that it is unclaimed. `BackRoomBoards` exports `cracker` and `CRACK_MIN` beside `ace` and `ACE_MIN`. `topsOf(nodes, now)` does the same from raw nodes for the admin page. It signs in through party.js (`window.Party`). Every node is untrusted and checked before it's shown: at most 3,000 players shown and 8,000 characters a node; nodes from the future, from before the boards started (`EPOCH`, 21 October 2026) or not updated for 180 days are skipped (the timestamp is checked first, so a stale node costs one comparison and doesn't count toward the 3,000; entries are never removed from the database, so every view still downloads them all); play time can't exceed 24 hours for every day since `EPOCH` (a node claiming more is skipped); running counts can't exceed a rate per second played in that game (`rate` in `GAMES`, e.g. half a kill a second), and each stat has a range (Elo 100–3000, Updraft height up to 1005 m and a summit no quicker than 2 minutes, a Spire tower no quicker than 20 s and the full obby no quicker than 2 minutes, a Slipstream lap no quicker than the track's length, as the game builds it, at 120 m/s, the game's hard speed cap (`VMAX` 92 m/s × 1.3, which boost and DRS can't pass; real best laps average 60–80 m/s), from `TRACKS`; Slipstream's fastest laps and course records count laps with a corner cut too, so for those the floor is half of it, `CUT_FLOOR`, which still throws out impossible times); a best single round has a cap (most kills in a Shell Crackers match 30, the first to 25 wins; Backrooms 80; Rumble Karts wrecks in a 3-minute round 60; Boostball goals in a match 40; Chromaflage hiders in a round 7 and Odd One Out props 15, a full room less one; Odd One Out survival 900 s, the longest hunt) and can't be more than the player's all-time count it's part of (`le`; it's dropped if there's no such count); a Slipstream course record needs 1–20 laps, must be no quicker than the track's quickest lap times the laps and no quicker than the player's own fastest lap there times the laps (every lap of a race is also a lap time, so a record with no lap time is dropped); Slipstream's lap and race records are read for the current **record season** only (`SLIP_SEASON`, 2 since the reset of 17 November 2026, `SLIP_RESET`): `parse` takes only this season's names (`lap:s2:<track>`, `clap:s2:…`, `rec:s2:<track>:<laps>`, `crec:s2:…`) and hands them on without the season (`lap:<track>`…), so the boards, the drivers-per-track counts, miles.js and the admin page all see this season and nothing older, an empty board says "yet this season", and the lap and record boards carry a small line saying when the records were reset and that Elo and play time were kept (to reset again, see stats.js, "Record seasons"); unknown games, stats and tracks are ignored; at most 240 stats per game; names are cleaned, run through social.js's word filter and only ever set as text. To add a game, add a row to `GAMES` (folder, name and its stats: key, label, format, unit, range and, for running counts, a rate, or for a best single round the all-time count it can't exceed); for a new Slipstream track, add it to `TRACKS`. `low: 1` ranks a count lowest first (the daily puzzles' fewest-guess boards; the key must also be in stats.js's `LOWER`), `grp: 'Daily Puzzles'` puts a game in that group of the Board menu (the menu has a Games group, then Daily Puzzles), and `rateOf: 'daily'` checks a count against the seconds of every Daily Puzzles game together (the hub's `done`, as the hub page is only open for seconds). A node with more than 80 games is skipped (stats.js keeps at most 80, `MAX_GAMES`; it was 40 before the daily puzzles). A test hook (`window.__lb`) exists only when `navigator.webdriver` is set. Loaded with `data-lib` (the admin page does this), it exposes its checks, `GAMES`, `TRACKS` and formatters as `window.BackRoomBoards` and stops before adding anything to the page. **One person, two accounts:** an anonymous entry whose owner merged it into a Google account (its own `to`, and the Google node's `al`, both written by stats.js) is left out, since the Google entry already has its hours; an entry the admin merged into another (a signed `a`, below) is shown as one row with it, each account's own play added up (hours from batches add; legacy time from before batches, which accounts in one browser may share, counts once; running counts like kills, wins and goals add when the two accounts never shared a device, else the larger; Elo, best scores, fastest times and other bests the better; K/D worked out again), so merged miles add back what each account earned; this week adds up for two version-2 nodes, else the larger. Running counts are the stats with a `rate`, or `sum: 1` for ones that only go up but have none (hands, towers, crystals, crowns, shows) (`collapse`, also used by the admin page, which lists merged entries marked). **Hours resets:** play time can be set back to zero from the admin page. Totals only ever grow (stats.js adds each device's new seconds and never lowers a total), so a reset is a zero point: the seconds each game had at that moment, subtracted from then on (personal bests are untouched). Resets are stored at `rooms/lb-rs/players/<admin's uid>` as `r`, the JSON of `{ <uid>: { t, b: { <game>: seconds } } }` (no `b` means the reset was undone), with `sig`, an ECDSA P-256 / SHA-256 signature of `r` made with the admin key (the same key pair as `adminkey.js`'s ECDH one). Anyone can write a node there, so only nodes whose signature checks out against the public key in `adminkey.js` count, and for each uid the latest `t` wins. The same entries can also remove an account from the boards altogether (`h: 1`, for fake or junk accounts): every board skips it, while the admin page still lists it, marked Removed and left out of its totals, with a button to put it back; or say an account is the same person as another (`a: <uid>`, one row; one step only, so a merged account can't itself be a target). Older copies of leaderboard.js ignore `a`, `to` and `al` and show every entry.
- `e63334287a3deaa3/gamechat.js`: in-game party chat (Back Room). Every multiplayer game loads it with one line in its `<head>` (`<script src="/e63334287a3deaa3/gamechat.js" defer></script>`): Shell Crackers, Rumble Karts, Slipstream, Boostball, Coil, Showdown, Backrooms 1v1–4v4, Chromaflage, Spire, Turbo Trail, Escapade, Updraft, Odd One Out, Tumble Trials, Deepword, Puffer Party and Putterby. It does nothing unless the game is open inside a Back Room page whose Online panel offers `window.BackRoomChat` (the home page's game layer, the phone page, or party.js's own layer) and the player is in a party with someone else; a game opened on its own shows nothing new. Then the party chat shows as a small feed on the left (about a third of the way down; fewer lines on a short screen) that fades after 9 seconds and never takes clicks, with a one-time hint when the game opens. **T** opens a box to type in (Shell Crackers and Turbo Trail use **Enter** and Chromaflage **Y**, because T is one of their keys: `data-key` on the script tag, by `e.code`), Enter sends, Esc, the × or a click outside closes it; on a phone, the Chat button that `social.js` adds to the game's top bar opens the same box, so there's nothing new over the game's own touch controls. Spire's script tag has `data-left="72"` so the feed sits right of its progress bar. While the box is open the game is kept out of it: the keys and mouse buttons the game thinks are held get a keyup / mouseup first; every key event stops in a capture listener on `window` (added after the game's own, so only keybinds.js's capture listener, which only acts while its Controls panel is open, runs first), which leaves typing working; pointer lock is let go and asked for again when the box closes (Enter and clicks are user gestures, so it comes straight back), and the game's own `pointerlockchange` for that unlock is held back so it doesn't open its pause menu; if the browser won't give the lock back, the game is then told the mouse is free and shows its own pause or "click to play"; a see-through layer over the game catches clicks and taps. The key isn't taken while the player types in one of the game's own fields, or when they've moved one of the game's actions onto it in the Controls panel (read from `keybinds:<game>` in localStorage); the top-bar Chat button still works then. Everything goes through `social.js` (encryption, block, mute, word filter, rate limit), so this file never touches Firebase and stores nothing. The box and feed live in a shadow root, so the game's CSS and this file's CSS don't mix.
- Archived Back Room games: `e63334287a3deaa3/starhop/`, `quarry/`, `kartwheel/`, `abyssal/` and `hail-mary/` are kept in the repo but taken off the Back Room page, the phone page (`GROUPS` in `m/index.html`) and the Parties list (`GAMES` in `party.js`). Their files, saved data and keybinds still work if someone opens the folder directly, and their prefixes stay in the invite routing (an old invite link opens nothing rather than the wrong game). The Online panel still names someone who is in one (`GAMES` in `social.js`, listed without `room`, so it offers no room invites), and the admin page keeps their names for old feedback notes (`SITE` in its script); they're left out of the feedback box's game list (`GAMES` in `feedback.js`). Abyssal's questions and answers were merged into Deepword's bank in November 2026 (Abyssal's own page is unchanged). To bring one back, restore its card, its `GROUPS` row, its feedback-box entry, its `room` in `social.js` and (for Kartwheel, Quarry and Hail Mary) its `GAMES` row in `party.js` from git history.

Touch: every game plays on a phone. Each game draws its own touch controls (a move stick, buttons, and drag-to-look for the first-person games) and shows them only on touch screens (`pointer: coarse`, or after the first touch), so the keyboard-and-mouse version is unchanged. The first-person games (Shell Crackers, Backrooms 1v1–4v4, Quarry) skip pointer lock on touch screens; in Quarry, taps in the inventory and crafting screens go to the same code as mouse clicks.

- `functions/api/traffic.js`: `GET https://ascoding11.com/api/traffic`, the showcase page's live traffic numbers (last 30 UTC days including today, in the analytics dashboard's "Copy showcase numbers" shape: anonymous totals, no names, emails or visitor IDs; the homepage is left out of the top pages, and sessions made only of 0-second homepage views with no referrer are treated as bots). It reads the analytics `pv` pageviews with the shop's `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` secret, so no Firestore rules change. To keep reads low it saves each finished day's summary in `traffic_days/{YYYY-MM-DD}` once and reuses its last answer (`traffic_days/_latest`) for 30 minutes; only this function touches `traffic_days`. `showcase/index.html` loads it (from ascoding11.com when opened on github.io) and shows its saved `trafficData` snapshot only if it can't be reached.
- `store.js` and `functions/` (site root): the shop, for selling skins and other items with Stripe. Buying needs a Google sign-in, and an item belongs to that Google account on every device and on both addresses. A page loads `<script src="/store.js" defer></script>` and calls `Store.buy(sku)` from a click (signs the player in with Google if needed, then sends them to Stripe's own payment page), `Store.owned()` / `Store.has(sku)` to see what they own, and `Store.returned()` / `Store.waitFor(sku)` when Stripe sends them back (`?purchase=success&sku=…`). The server side is three Cloudflare Pages Functions, which run only on ascoding11.com (GitHub Pages can't run code), so the shop on ascoding11.github.io calls them there: `functions/api/checkout.js` (checks the Google sign-in, refuses items already owned, starts a Stripe Checkout), `functions/api/stripe-webhook.js` (Stripe's signed "payment finished" message: the only thing that gives an item) and `functions/api/owned.js`; shared code in `functions/_lib.js`. The same checkout and webhook also sell the Back Room's emerald packs (lookup keys `emeralds_7` … `emeralds_4000`, credited to the server wallet; see "Server wallet" under `cosmetics.js` above); item purchases work exactly as before. Items are Stripe prices: an item exists when an active price has its sku as the lookup key (lowercase letters, digits and `_`, starting with a letter, e.g. `boostball_flame`), and its price comes from Stripe. Purchases are written only by the webhook, with a Firebase service account, to Firestore `entitlements/{uid}` (`items: { sku: time bought }`) and `orders/{Checkout session id}`; the site's pages never read or write those collections directly. Secrets live only in Cloudflare (the site's Settings → Variables and secrets): `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` (the whole JSON key). The Stripe webhook endpoint is `https://ascoding11.com/api/stripe-webhook` with `checkout.session.completed`, `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded`, `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created` and `charge.dispute.closed`. The item is granted to the uid our checkout put in `metadata.uid` (never `client_reference_id`, which a Payment Link takes from its address); a Payment Link payment, a session without our metadata, or an event in the other Stripe mode grants nothing and is recorded in `orders` as `unclaimed`. Refunds take emerald packs back (see "Server wallet" above) but don't take item-shop items back. `GET /api/owned?uids=…` (no sign-in) is the public list of what players own, for games (see `owned.js` under `cosmetics.js` above); `GET /api/owned` with a sign-in is unchanged.
- `marketplace/` and `functions/_market.js` (with `functions/api/market/[action].js`): the Marketplace, a public page in the site's top bar where people sell second-hand things. Sellers post an item (title, asking price, category, condition, a rough area, up to three photos and private contact details); each buyer gets their own haggling thread with the seller (offer, counter, accept, decline, withdraw; offering the asking price is an instant deal), and the listing's card shows how many offers are open and the best one. When a price is agreed the listing is reserved ("Sale pending") and the buyer pays it on Stripe's page. **Nobody sees who they are dealing with until the payment has gone through**: buyers show as "Buyer 1", "Buyer 2" to the seller, the seller has no name at all, and listings and offer notes with phone numbers, emails, links or usernames are refused. Stripe's signed webhook (`functions/api/stripe-webhook.js`, the branch for `metadata.kind == "market"`) is the only thing that marks a deal paid; from then on the buyer gets the seller's name and contact details and the seller the buyer's name and email. Like the shop, the page never touches the database: everything goes through `https://ascoding11.com/api/market/<action>` (Cloudflare Pages Functions, same three secrets as the shop) with the Google sign-in from `account.js`, and the server reads and writes Firestore with the service account, so **no security rules are needed** and the rules should not open these collections: `marketListings/{id}`, `marketPhotos/{id}_{n}` (JPEG bytes, shrunk in the browser to under 400 KB, which also drops location data), `marketOffers/{listing}_{hash of buyer}` (one thread per buyer per listing), `marketSellers/{uid}` (the seller's Stripe account) and `marketReports/{listing}_{uid}`. Queries filter on a single field, so no Firestore indexes need creating. **Money:** sellers are paid through Stripe Connect (Express accounts, set up from the page's "Set up payouts" button; a seller must finish it before they can accept or counter). The payment is a destination charge, so Stripe passes it straight to the seller and the site never holds it. Out of the seller's payout come the site's cut and Stripe's card fee (2.9% + 30¢, which Stripe otherwise bills to the site). **The site's cut is `FEE_PERCENT` at the top of `functions/_market.js`, 0 for now; change it to 10 for 10%** (or set `MARKET_FEE_PERCENT` in Cloudflare, which wins). A listing keeps the cut it was posted with, and the page's fee wording follows the server. To go live: turn on Connect in the Stripe dashboard (the same account as `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`); nothing else is new. Optional Cloudflare variable `MARKET_ADMINS` (comma-separated Google emails) lets those people remove any listing; three reports from different people hide a listing. There is no email or push notice: people see new offers on the page (it refreshes every 20 s, and "My deals" shows a count). Refunds and disputes are done by hand in Stripe and don't change a deal's status here. Counted in analytics as `marketplace`.
- `adrefresh.js` (site root, so the public games can load it without linking to the Back Room): loads a fresh ad in the ads that are showing (menu banners, side rails) when someone clicks or taps a button on the screen. Keyboard presses don't count (their clicks have `detail` 0), nor do the in-game touch controls (anything in `#touch`, `.tb`/`.tbtn`, Coil's `#boostBtn`, Hail Mary's `#tA`/`#tB`, or anything marked `data-no-ad-refresh`), and it refreshes at most once every 30 seconds, only on screen and only ads that were filled. AdSense doesn't allow timed refresh, only refresh on what the person does, and these limits keep it that way. Every game page with ads loads it with `<script src="/adrefresh.js" defer></script>` in its `<head>`; with no ad slots set up it does nothing.
Ads: Google AdSense runs only on the game pages. The home page carries just the `google-adsense-account` meta tag, which verifies the site with AdSense without loading any ads. `ads.txt` (site root) and `privacy.html` are required by AdSense. Hype Cycle Exchange uses automatic ads. Boxed has no ads: its menu banner and side rails were removed while the site is under AdSense review, because its problems come from other sites (the AoPS Wiki); to bring them back, copy the `AD_SLOT` block and side rails from another game. The other games show banners only on their menu and results screens, switched on by pasting a display ad unit ID into `AD_SLOT` near the top of each game's script.

The game's secret results file (`rounds.json`) is intentionally **not** in this repository. Keep it on your own computer and choose it when you host a game.
